### T-815 – Startup log file
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** The built app is windowed and silent: on the user's first launch the process sat in Task Manager with no window for a long while and nothing said why. DESIGN.md §18.9 asks for problems to be reportable. The app writes a log file so a stuck or crashed start can be diagnosed from the file.
- **Scope:** `core/logbook.py`: `setup_logging(data_dir)` writes `<data dir>/logs/retro-ai-lab.log` (rotating, 1 MB × 3), logs a header (version, Python, platform, frozen or not, data dir, argv), installs an excepthook that logs uncaught exceptions and chains to the previous one, is idempotent, and never fails startup (an unwritable folder logs nowhere and returns None). `cli._default_launch` sets it up before Qt is imported; `ui.app.run_app` logs the milestones "Qt application created", "main window shown", "event loop finished". The README's Windows section says where the file is. **Out of scope:** a log viewer in the UI; log levels per module.
- **Acceptance criteria:** after any launch, the log file exists under the data dir and names the version and the three milestones; an uncaught exception's traceback is in the file; the app starts even when the log folder cannot be created.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/core/logbook.py` (new), `core/__init__.py`, `cli.py`, `ui/app.py`, `README.md`, tests, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** unit – `tests/unit/core/test_logbook.py`: creates folder and file with the header; second call reuses the handler (one file handler, same path); unwritable location (a file where the folder should be) returns None and raises nothing; the excepthook writes the traceback and calls the previous hook; env override of the data dir is honoured through `default_data_dir`. UI – `tests/ui/test_main_window.py`: the real entry point in a fresh process with `RETRO_AI_LAB_DATA_DIR` set leaves a log naming the three milestones. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new unit tests + 1 extended UI test pass; full suite 968 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.11 and 3.13; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #82. **PR:** #82.
- **Completion report:** The log is set up in the CLI launcher before Qt is imported, so a start that never shows a window still leaves the header line; `run_app` logs its three milestones; uncaught exceptions are written with their traceback and the previous excepthook (PyInstaller's dialog in the built app) still runs. The README's Windows section says where the file is and asks for it in reports.

### T-816 – BizHawk: a LuaSocket loader next to the bridge script
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run, finding 1)
- **Objective:** On the user's PC (64-bit BizHawk, Lua 5.4) the bridge script stopped at `require("socket")` with `module 'socket' not found`, so the app said "Couldn't find BizHawk on port 43110". ADR-005 assumed EmuHawk bundles LuaSocket; it does not. Archipelago's connector works because Archipelago ships a `socket.lua` and `x64/socket-windows-5-4.dll` beside it and loads the DLL with `package.loadlib`. Do the same.
- **Scope:** `emulator/bizhawk/socket.lua` (loader: finds its own folder, picks `luasocket/<arch>/socket-<os>-<5-1|5-4>.<ext>`, loads `luaopen_socket_core`, adds `bind`/`connect`, clear error if the DLL cannot load); the bridge script puts its own folder first on `package.path`; package-data and the PyInstaller spec ship the loader and the `luasocket` folder; docs/BIZHAWK.md and ARCHITECTURE §5 corrected. The DLLs are Archipelago's MIT LuaSocket builds (`x64/socket-windows-5-4.dll`, `-5-1.dll`), vendored with their licence after the user chose to ship them; README gets a Credits section listing every third-party source (the user asked for this). **Out of scope:** 32-bit EmuHawk; Linux/macOS EmuHawk (the error says what was looked for).
- **Acceptance criteria:** under Lua 5.4 with a Windows `package.config`, the loader asks `package.loadlib` for `<script dir>/luasocket/x64/socket-windows-5-4.dll` with symbol `luaopen_socket_core`; Lua 5.1 → `-5-1`; a 32-bit process → `x86`; a failed load raises an error naming the path and "64-bit"; the returned module has every core function plus `bind` (tcp + reuseaddr + bind + listen) and `connect`; the real script, loaded from its file, puts its folder first on `package.path`; the script's own lupa suite still passes; the two shipped DLLs are PE files (`MZ`) and the licence file names LuaSocket and Archipelago; README has a Credits section.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/{socket.lua,retro_ai_lab.lua}`, `pyproject.toml`, `packaging/retro_ai_lab.spec`, `docs/BIZHAWK.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, tests, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** unit – `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_luasocket_loader.py`: the loader under lupa with a fake `package.loadlib` for each case above, the module shape, `bind`/`connect` calls, and the script's `package.path`; `tests/unit/test_packaging.py`: the spec and package-data name the loader and the folder. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 974 passed, 10 skipped (Python 3.13); bridge and packaging tests also pass on 3.11; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #83. **PR:** #83.
- **Completion report:** `socket.lua` and `luasocket/{README.md,LICENSE,x64/socket-windows-5-4.dll,x64/socket-windows-5-1.dll}` ship beside the bridge script in the wheel and the PyInstaller build; the script puts its own folder first on `package.path`; docs corrected; README Credits section lists every third-party source. Not yet run on the user's EmuHawk: that is the next step of T-117.

### T-817 – Frame capture off the GUI thread
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run, finding 2)
- **Objective:** With BizHawk connected the app barely reacts: the window drags in jerks and buttons take ages to click. `FramePump.tick` runs on the GUI thread and each tick is a blocking socket round trip (request, EmuHawk takes a PNG, base64, reply) 30 times a second, on top of the play clock's 60 stepping calls a second (T-818). TESTING_STRATEGY §2.3 already requires that long operations don't block the UI thread.
- **Scope:** `FramePump` captures on a one-thread `QThreadPool` and hands the frame back to the GUI thread by signal; a tick that finds the previous capture still running is skipped (so the real rate settles to what the link can do) and counted; a frame that arrives after `stop()` is dropped. `EmulatorAdapter` serialises its public operations with a re-entrant lock so the worker, the GUI thread (play clock, training) and the watchdog never interleave on one socket; `check_link` becomes final and calls a new `_do_check_link` hook, and when the lock is busy it answers True without waiting (someone is talking to the emulator right now, so the link is evidently up). **Out of scope:** the play clock (T-818), the training tick, making the Lua side cheaper.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a capture runs on a thread other than the GUI thread and the subscribers run on the GUI thread; while a capture is slow, GUI events keep flowing (a zero-delay timer fires within a few ms); a tick during an in-flight capture is skipped and counted; after `stop()` a late frame is not shown; a dropped link found by the worker stops the pump and the panel reports it; transient errors are counted and the pump keeps running; adapter operations from two threads never overlap; `check_link` returns True without blocking while another thread holds the adapter.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/game_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/base.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `tests/ui/test_game_panel.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/test_base.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** UI – `tests/ui/test_game_panel.py`: existing tests wait for the asynchronous frame; new tests for thread identity, GUI responsiveness under a slow capture, in-flight skipping, late frame after stop, disconnect found by the worker. Unit – `tests/unit/emulator/test_base.py`: two threads hammering one adapter never run a backend hook concurrently; `check_link` under a held lock. Regression – full suite on Python 3.11 and 3.13.
- **Results:** 985 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.11 and 3.13 (5 new UI tests, 2 new adapter tests; three disconnect tests now wait for the window to hear of a drop found on the worker); the new tests pass five runs in a row; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #84. **PR:** #84.
- **Completion report:** `FramePump.tick()` now returns whether a capture started and delivers the frame later on the GUI thread; `in_flight`, `skipped` and `captured` expose what happened. A frame from before `stop()` is dropped by a generation counter. The base adapter's lock makes the worker safe alongside the clock, training and watchdog. Not yet tried on the user's PC; T-818 moves the play clock's stepping too, since that is the other 60 round trips a second.

### T-818 – Play clock steps off the GUI thread
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run, finding 2, second half)
- **Objective:** T-817 took frame capture off the GUI thread; the play clock still steps there: 60 times a second it calls `advance_frames(1)` (one socket round trip, at least one emulated frame long) and then `observation.advance(1)` (a memory read, another round trip). With BizHawk that is two blocking waits per tick on the thread that draws the window.
- **Scope:** `PlayClock.pulse()` (what the timer now calls) runs the step and the observation on a one-thread `QThreadPool` and finishes on the GUI thread by signal: `frames` and the error count update there; a pulse while a step is still running is skipped and counted; a result from before `pause()`/a disconnect is dropped (generation counter, as in the pump). `tick(n)` stays synchronous ("advance now and wait": tests and tooling drive time by hand). `ConnectionService._finish_disconnect` takes a lock and reports a drop once even when two workers hit it together. ARCHITECTURE §3 threading paragraph updated. **Out of scope:** fewer round trips per frame (bundling step, read and screenshot into one request; a later task), the training tick.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a pulse runs the step and the memory read off the GUI thread and reports on it; GUI events keep flowing while a step is slow; a pulse during an in-flight step is skipped and counted; a late result after `pause()` is dropped and `frames` is unchanged; a drop found by the worker stops the clock; a transient error on the worker is counted and the clock keeps running; two workers dropping at once produce one `EmulatorDisconnected`; the synchronous `tick(n)` behaves as before.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/play_clock.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/connection.py`, `tests/ui/test_play_clock.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_connection_resilience.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** UI – `tests/ui/test_play_clock.py`: new tests for each criterion above with a gated `_do_advance_frames`; existing timer tests wait for `frames` rather than reading it at once, and the keyboard test waits for the in-flight step before checking that time stands still. Unit – `tests/unit/services/test_connection_resilience.py`: two threads whose operations raise `EmulatorDisconnectedError` at the same moment → one event. Regression – full suite on 3.11 and 3.13, the new tests five times.
- **Results:** 992 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.11 and 3.13 (6 new clock tests, 1 new connection test); the thread tests pass five runs in a row; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #85. **PR:** #85.
- **Completion report:** The timer now calls `PlayClock.pulse()`, which steps and snapshots on a worker and reports back by signal; `in_flight` and `skipped` show what happened; `tick(n)` is unchanged for callers that drive time by hand. With both the pump and the clock off the GUI thread, the window no longer waits on BizHawk at all; what remains is the cost per frame (two or three round trips), which a later task can cut by bundling step, read and screenshot into one request. Not yet tried on the user's PC.

### T-819 – Log how long the emulator takes to answer
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run)
- **Objective:** T-817/T-818 stopped the window waiting on BizHawk, but how fast the game can run in the app now depends on the per-call round trip, and nothing measures it. Before deciding whether to bundle step, read and screenshot into one request (a later task), the log should say what each command costs on the user's PC.
- **Scope:** `Link` (the request/response layer both the BizHawk and mGBA adapters use) times every call and, every 5 s of calls, writes one INFO line to the log: per command, how many, average and slowest, e.g. `Emulator link, last 5 s: screenshot ×28 avg 34 ms max 61 ms; step ×55 avg 17 ms max 40 ms`. `Link.stats` and `Link.summary()` expose the same for tests and tooling. README's log note mentions it. **Out of scope:** changing the protocol; measuring on the Lua side.
- **Acceptance criteria:** after two calls the stats show the count, average and maximum per command; a call that fails is still timed; once the window has elapsed the next call logs the summary and starts a fresh window; a link that made no calls logs nothing; the log line names every command used in the window, slowest total first.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_protocol.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** unit – `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_protocol.py`: an injected clock drives the timings; `stats`/`summary()` after two commands; the failing call; the report line via `caplog` at the window boundary and the reset after it; no report for an idle link. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 997 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13; emulator tests on 3.11; 5 new protocol tests; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #86. **PR:** #86.
- **Completion report:** `Link` times each call (failures included) and logs `Emulator link, last N s: <cmd> ×count avg … max …; …` once per 5 s window, slowest command first. The user's next log will show what `screenshot`, `step` and `read` cost on their PC.

### T-820 – A worker must not turn a clean disconnect into a lost link
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (regression from T-817/T-818, caught by CI on PR #86)
- **Objective:** `test_backend_error_on_disconnect_click_does_not_break_the_ui` failed once on Windows: after the Disconnect click the Connect button stayed disabled. Cause: the GUI's `ConnectionService.disconnect()` closes the adapter (which marks it disconnected and releases the adapter lock) and only then records the disconnect; in that gap a worker's call fails with `EmulatorDisconnectedError` and records the loss first, as an *unexpected* drop from its own thread, so the window hears "Lost the connection" through a queued event and the GUI's own bookkeeping finds nothing left to do. A child pressing Disconnect could see the app claim the link was lost.
- **Scope:** `disconnect()` holds the service lock for its whole run, so a worker that fails meanwhile waits and then finds the disconnect already recorded. **Out of scope:** `connect()` (a disconnect during CONNECTING is refused by state, and the Connect bar disables the button).
- **Acceptance criteria:** with a worker blocked on the adapter while the GUI disconnects, and the service slow between the backend's failure and its bookkeeping, exactly one `EmulatorDisconnected` is published and it is the expected one; the worker's call raises `EmulatorDisconnectedError`.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/connection.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_connection_resilience.py`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** unit – `tests/unit/services/test_connection_resilience.py`: the gated worker / slow disconnect scenario above (deterministic: the worker's second call starts only once the backend's disconnect hook has been entered, and the service's warning log is slowed). Regression – full suite; the Windows UI test that failed.
- **Results:** 998 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #86. **PR:** #86 (rides with T-819).
- **Completion report:** `disconnect()` is serialised with `_finish_disconnect()` on the service lock; the worker's late failure is swallowed as already-recorded.

### T-821 – Button presses off the GUI thread
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run, finding 3)
- **Objective:** With BizHawk connected the app still froze on every button press after T-817/T-818. `ControllerPad._send` runs `set_input` on the GUI thread: one socket round trip, and before it a wait for the adapter lock that the frame pump's and play clock's workers hold almost continuously, so a press stalls the window for several round trips.
- **Scope:** the pad sends on a one-thread `QThreadPool` and finishes by signal; while a send is in flight the latest held set is kept and sent once the worker is free (coalescing: a press and a release during one send become one request with the final set); errors are counted on the GUI thread as before; a drop found by the worker is left to the service's event, which resets the pad. `held` and `input_changed` stay synchronous (the pad's own state), as does `observation.record_input`. **Out of scope:** a single shared emulator worker for pump, clock and pad (the pools serialise on the adapter lock, which is enough for now).
- **Acceptance criteria:** `press()` returns at once while the adapter is busy on another thread; the send reaches the adapter on a worker thread; two changes during one send produce exactly one further send carrying the final set; a transient error is counted and the next change still goes out; a drop during a send does not crash and the pad resets; the keyboard end-to-end test still moves the car.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/controller_pad.py`, `tests/ui/test_controller_pad.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** UI – `tests/ui/test_controller_pad.py`: existing send tests wait for the adapter to see the set; new tests for the gated adapter (press returns at once; worker thread; coalescing to one extra send; GUI events keep flowing). Regression – full suite on 3.11 and 3.13; the new tests five times.
- **Results:** 1001 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.11 and 3.13 (3 new pad tests; five existing pad tests and the keyboard test now wait for the worker's delivery); the thread-sensitive UI tests pass five runs in a row on 3.12; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #87. **PR:** #87.
- **Completion report:** `press`/`release` change the pad's state and return at once; the request goes out on the pad's worker and later changes are merged into one follow-up send. `sending` and `sends` show what happened. With this, the pump, the clock and the pad are all off the GUI thread; T-822 replaces the three pools with one shared worker queue so inputs are never stuck behind frame traffic.

### T-822 – One request per frame: a bundled `step` in the adapter contract
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance, "nail it down" per the user)
- **Objective:** Every frame the app makes several round trips to the emulator: `step`, then one `read` per memory region for the observation snapshot, plus `screenshot` from the pump and `input` from the pad. The bridge protocol already lets one `step` request carry buttons, frames, reads and a screenshot (ARCHITECTURE §5.3), but the adapter contract exposes only the primitives. Add the bundled operation to the contract so the UI (T-823) can make one request per frame.
- **Scope:** `EmulatorAdapter.step(frames, *, pressed=None, reads=(), screenshot=False) -> StepResult` and `read_many(reads) -> tuple[bytes, ...]`, both `final`, validated like the primitives (capabilities, buttons, regions, ranges, lengths), with default `_do_step`/`_do_read_many` hooks that compose the primitive hooks so the fake adapter and any other backend get them for free; `BizHawkAdapter` overrides both with one request each; `MemoryCapture` snapshots with one `read_many`; `StepResult(frames, reads, frame)` in `core.types`. **Out of scope:** using `step` from the play clock, pump and pad (T-823); the Lua script (unchanged, it already serves these).
- **Acceptance criteria:** on the recording adapter, `step(3, pressed={A}, reads=[("ram",0,4)], screenshot=True)` calls input, advance, read and frame in that order and returns the bytes and the frame; each field's capability is checked only when that field is used (a step with no screenshot works on an adapter without screen capture); bad buttons, regions, ranges and counts raise `ConfigurationError` before the backend; a short read is refused; `read_many` returns the bytes in order. Against the simulator, `step` with everything is exactly one `step` request and `read_many` of two regions is one `read` request with two entries; a snapshot of the Toy Racer's RAM via `MemoryCapture` is one request. Against the real Lua script (lupa host), `step` with reads and a screenshot returns the right RAM bytes and a frame.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/core/types.py`, `core/__init__.py`, `emulator/base.py`, `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `observation/memory.py`, tests (`test_base.py`, `bizhawk/test_adapter.py`, `bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `observation/test_memory.py`), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** as the criteria above; regression – full suite on 3.11 and 3.13.
- **Results:** 1014 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.11 and 3.13 (7 new base tests, 2 simulator tests, 1 real-script test, 1 memory capture test); ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #88. **PR:** #88.
- **Completion report:** The contract now has `step` and `read_many`; the fake adapter gets them through the default hooks, BizHawk (and so mGBA) sends one request each; a memory snapshot is one `read` request with every region. The UI still uses the primitives; T-823 switches the play clock to `step` with reads and a screenshot so a running game costs one round trip per frame.

### T-823 – One request per frame while the game runs
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance)
- **Objective:** With T-822's `step` in the contract, make a running game cost one round trip per frame. Today each play-clock pulse is a `step` plus a `read`, the pump adds a `screenshot` up to 30 times a second and the pad an `input` per change: three requests per frame on a real emulator.
- **Scope:** `PlayClock.pulse()` (and `tick`) send one `step(1, pressed=<pad's held set>, reads=<snapshot regions>, screenshot=<every k-th pulse>)`; the worker hands the memory bytes to the observation service without a second request (`ObservationService.advance(frames, snapshot=…)` takes prefetched bytes and `snapshot_reads()` names the regions; `MemoryCapture.reads()`/`from_reads()` plan and build a snapshot); the clock emits `frame_captured` for the screenshot. `GamePanel` shows those frames, feeds them to `observe_frame`, and keeps the pump stopped while the clock runs (the pump still serves a paused game, training, and emulators that cannot step). The pad keeps sending its own `input` on a change so a press lands between pulses too. `screenshot_every` = clock fps ÷ pump fps, so the picture rate is unchanged. **Out of scope:** the training episode's step (T-824), the screenshot transport (T-825).
- **Acceptance criteria:** against the socket simulator, one pulse is exactly one request, a `step` carrying `buttons`, `reads` and (on the screenshot pulse) `screenshot`; the observation service's latest snapshot holds that step's RAM without a `read` request; the clock emits a frame on every k-th pulse (the first included) and the game panel shows it; while the clock runs the pump is stopped, when it pauses the pump runs, and on disconnect both stop; a step without a pad callback sends no buttons; an emulator without `memory_read` or `screen_capture` gets a plain step; a dropped link found by the step still stops the clock and a glitch is still counted.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/observer.py`, `observation/memory.py`, `ui/play_clock.py`, `ui/game_panel.py`, tests (`test_play_clock.py`, `test_game_panel.py`, `services/test_observer.py`, `observation/test_memory.py`), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** as the criteria; the simulator test counts requests per pulse; fake-adapter tests check held buttons reach the game, the prefetched snapshot, and the frame signal; game panel tests check the pump/clock hand-over. Regression – full suite on 3.11 and 3.13; the thread-sensitive UI tests five times.
- **Results:** 14 new tests (6 play clock incl. the simulator request count and no-pad/no-capability steps, 4 game panel hand-over and rate tests, 3 observer, 1 memory capture); 3 tests updated for the new hand-over (the pump no longer runs while the clock does). Full suite 1031 passed, 10 skipped on 3.13 and 3.11; the five thread-sensitive UI files pass 5/5 repeats; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #89. **PR:** #89.
- **Completion report:** a running game is now one `step` request per frame on a real emulator: the pad's held set, the planned memory reads and (every k-th pulse) the screenshot ride along, and the observation service takes the prefetched bytes instead of reading again. The pump is stopped while the clock runs and takes over when the game pauses or the emulator cannot step. Note for the user's log: with the clock at 30 fps and pictures at 30 fps, `step` should now be the only command in the five-second summary while playing; the screenshot cost inside it is T-825's target and the training episode's three calls per step are T-824's.

### T-824 – Training steps use the one bundled request
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance)
- **Objective:** The training episode's step was three round trips (`set_input`, `advance_frames`, then the observation's `read`); with T-822/T-823 in place it is one `step`.
- **Scope:** `EpisodeRunner` sends `step(frames, pressed=held, reads=snapshot_reads())` and feeds the prefetched bytes to `advance(frames, snapshot=…)`. **Out of scope:** the screenshot transport (T-825).
- **Acceptance criteria:** against the socket simulator, one training step is exactly one `step` request carrying `buttons` and `reads`; the episode's learning, input log, dropped-link and checkpoint behaviour are unchanged (existing tests).
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- **Results:** 1 new test; full suite 1032 passed, 10 skipped on 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #90. **PR:** #90.
- **Completion report:** a training step is one request on a real emulator. With T-823 that makes playing and training both one message per frame; what remains inside that message is the screenshot's PNG + base64 cost (T-825).

### T-825 – Screenshots as a file on the same PC
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance)
- **Objective:** A screenshot was a PNG the emulator wrote to disk, read back in Lua, base64'd byte by byte in Lua (measured: 2 ms per 2 KB, 70 ms per 200 KB on Lua 5.4) and sent through the socket. With the emulator on the same PC, the app can read the file itself.
- **Scope:** request field `image_file` (a `.png` path the app chooses) on `screenshot` and `step`; the scripts (BizHawk and mGBA) write the screenshot there and answer `image_file` instead of `image`; `BizHawkAdapter(image_transfer="auto"|"file"|"socket")`, `auto` → file for a loopback host; the adapter reads and deletes the file and refuses a path it did not name; the simulator serves `image_file`. **Out of scope:** large memory reads without base64 (noted in ARCHITECTURE §5.4 as the next step if the log shows them).
- **Acceptance criteria:** through the real scripts (lupa) and the simulator, a screenshot on a loopback host is one request with `image_file`, the answer carries no base64, the file is gone afterwards and the frame is right; `image_transfer="socket"` and a non-loopback host keep the base64 route; a bad `image_file` is refused by the script and a file the emulator claims but did not write gives a plain error naming `image_transfer='socket'`; an answer naming a file the app did not ask for is a protocol error.
- **Expected changes:** `emulator/bizhawk/{adapter.py,simulator.py,retro_ai_lab.lua}`, `emulator/mgba/retro_ai_lab.lua`, tests (`bizhawk/test_adapter.py`, `bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `mgba/test_lua_script.py`), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** as the criteria; regression – full suite on 3.11 and 3.13.
- **Results:** 4 new tests, 2 updated (the default route is now the file); full suite 1036 passed, 10 skipped on 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #91. **PR:** #91.
- **Completion report:** on the user's PC a picture costs the emulator one PNG write and the app one file read; nothing is base64'd in Lua any more for screenshots. Memory reads keep base64 (small on NES/SNES); ARCHITECTURE notes the GBA-sized case as the next candidate.
### T-826 – Play at console speed (60 fps)
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance)
- **Objective:** `GamePanel` stepped the play clock at its picture rate (30 fps), so a game the user plays ran at half speed while training (`LearningPanel`) ticks at 60. With T-823 a step and its picture are one request, and a slow link just skips pulses, so the clock can run at console speed with a screenshot on every second step.

### T-827 – Watch only live memory on BizHawk
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance)
- **Objective:** The user's screen stayed on "Not connected" after connecting to Super Mario Bros. with the one-request-per-frame build. The BizHawk adapter offered every domain under 256 KB as a region, and the observer reads every region whole every frame: on a NES that is `RAM` (2 KB) plus `System Bus` (64 KB), `PRG ROM` (32 KB), `CHR VROM` (8 KB), `PPU Bus`, `CIRAM`… – some 100 KB a frame through `read_bytes_as_array`, `string.char` and base64 in Lua, which can take seconds and time out (a timeout is an `EmulatorError` the clock counts silently). Reading the bus also pokes PPU registers.
- **Scope:** `is_live_memory(ident)` in the BizHawk adapter: regions whose name has the word `rom`, `vrom`, `bus` or `bios`, or starts with `cart`, are listed by the bridge but not offered; `_do_memory_regions` applies it with the size cap. Applies to mGBA (same adapter base: `cart0`, `bios` left out; `wram`, `iwram`, `vram`, `oam`, `palette`, `io` kept). **Out of scope:** showing step failures in the UI (T-828).
- **Acceptance criteria:** through the real script (lupa) with `RAM`, `WRAM`, `System Bus`, `PRG ROM`, `CHR VROM` listed, `memory_regions()` is `ram`, `wram`; the description still lists all five; a read of `system_bus` is refused as an unknown region; the name rule is covered for NES, SNES, GB and GBA-style names.
- **Expected changes:** `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, tests (`bizhawk/test_adapter.py`, `bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `bizhawk/lua_host.py`), `docs/BIZHAWK.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** as the criteria; regression – full suite on 3.13 and 3.11.
- **Results:** 19 new tests (18 name-rule cases, 1 script test extended); the lupa host now lists ROM and bus domains too. Full suite 1055 passed, 10 skipped on 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #93. **PR:** #93.
- **Completion report:** a NES frame step now carries about 2 KB of memory instead of about 100 KB, and the PPU is no longer poked through the bus every frame. Whether this alone brings the user's screen back is for their log to say; T-828 makes a failing step visible in the window either way.
### T-828 – A failing frame step shows in the window
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance)
- **Objective:** When the user's screen stayed blank (T-827), nothing in the window or the log said why: `PlayClock` and `FramePump` counted an `EmulatorError` and went on, 60 times a second.
- **Scope:** `PlayClock.step_failed(str)` and `FramePump.failed(str)` emit a failure's message once per new message (and log a warning once), cleared by the next good step or picture (`last_failure`); `GamePanel` shows it in the status line ("The emulator did not finish a frame step: …" / "did not send a picture: …") until the next frame.
- **Acceptance criteria:** three failures with the same message then a different one give two signals and two log lines; a good step clears the slate so the old message is news again; the panel status shows the step message and goes back to "Frame N." on the next frame; a failing capture shows likewise and is said once while counted every time.
- **Expected changes:** `ui/play_clock.py`, `ui/game_panel.py`, tests (`test_play_clock.py`, `test_game_panel.py`), `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** as the criteria; regression – full suite on 3.13, UI suite on 3.11.
- **Results:** 3 new tests; full suite 1040 passed, 10 skipped on 3.13, UI suite 174 passed on 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #94. **PR:** #94.
- **Completion report:** a step that fails now tells the user what the emulator said, in the window and in the log, without flooding either.

- **Status:** Done
