### T-830 – Measure what a play session costs
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (performance)
- **Objective:** T-819's log line shows what each bridge command costs in a 5 s window, but nothing shows whether the app itself keeps up: how many frames the clock steps against how many it wants, what the snapshot bookkeeping costs on the worker, what the screen diff and display cost on the GUI thread, and whether the GUI thread stalls. Before more performance work, measure the app's own side so the next change is driven by numbers, on the simulator here and on the user's PC against the real emulator.
- **Scope:** `tools/profile_play.py`: `profile(adapter, seconds, host, port)` opens the real `MainWindow`, connects (`simulator` starts a `BizHawkSimulator` and connects the BizHawk adapter to it; `fake`, `bizhawk`, `mgba` connect as the app would), lets the play clock run for `seconds`, and returns a `Report` (steps against wanted, pictures, skipped pulses, last failure, the link's per-command totals, `CallStats` for `observation.advance` and `observation.observe_frame`, the longest gap of a 1 ms GUI heartbeat); `main(argv)` prints the five report lines. `Link.totals` and `summary(lifetime=True)` keep per-command stats since the link opened (the window stats still reset every 5 s); `BizHawkAdapter.link_summary` exposes them. README and ARCHITECTURE §2/§5.4 describe the tool. **Out of scope:** changing what the app does per frame; a Lua-side profile.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Against the simulator the report counts every step and every picture, names `step` first in the link summary, times every step and picture, measures a stall, and prints five lines ending with the stutter note.
  2. The fake emulator profiles too, with "not a bridge link" for the round trips.
  3. `main` prints the report and returns 0; an emulator it cannot reach returns 1 with the adapter's own message on stderr; bad arguments (`--seconds 0`, a non-number, an unknown adapter, a bad port) return 2 with usage.
  4. `profile` rejects an unknown adapter and a non-positive duration with `ConfigurationError`.
  5. The link's totals survive the 5 s window reset; the adapter's `link_summary` says "no calls" before connect and counts every call after.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/tools/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/tools/profile_play.py` (new), `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `tests/ui/test_profile_play.py` (new), `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_protocol.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_adapter.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** UI – `tests/ui/test_profile_play.py`: the simulator run (criteria 1), the fake run (2), `main` success, unreachable emulator and four bad-argument cases (3), `profile` validation (4), a report with no work; unit – `test_protocol.py` lifetime totals and `test_adapter.py` `link_summary` (5). Regression – full suite on 3.13, UI suite on 3.11.
- **Results:** 12 new tests (10 in `test_profile_play.py`, 2 unit); full suite 1074 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13; UI suite 189 passed on 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #96. **PR:** #96.
- **Completion report:** Measured here against the simulator with NES-sized (256×240) pictures: the clock keeps every frame at 60 fps, the app's own work is about 0.15 ms per step and per picture, and the round trip to the simulator is about 1 ms, so on this side nothing is near the 16 ms budget. The user can run the same tool on their PC against EmuHawk to see where the time goes there.

### T-831 – A frame step on a real EmuHawk never finished
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run, finding 4; the first real-emulator bug)
- **Objective:** The user's log with the T-827/T-828 build: `hello ×1 avg 17 ms`, then every `step ×1 avg 5000 ms` and "The emulator did not answer within 5 seconds", with EmuHawk sitting paused. Reading EmuHawk's `MainForm.cs`, `LuaConsole.cs` and `LuaLibraries.cs` (master) settles the hold-mode assumption T-110 left open: a frame runs only when the emulator is unpaused or on the user's Frame Advance key, and only a frame resumes a script waiting in `emu.frameadvance()`; `emu.yield()` waiters are resumed at the top of every turn as well. The script paused EmuHawk on `hello` and then called `emu.frameadvance()` inside `step`, so the script blocked forever, `bye` was never served, and the user saw a frozen emulator. The `lupa` host hid this by advancing a frame on every `frameadvance` yield whether paused or not.
- **Scope:** Script `run_frames(frames)`: in hold mode, `joypad.set` the pad, one `emu.yield()` so `InputManager.RunControllerChain` latches it (the override store is cleared by `ButtonOverrideAdapter.FrameTick` before each frame), `client.unpause()`, then `emu.frameadvance()` per frame with the pad set again between frames, and `client.pause()` from the resume after the last frame, before another turn can run a frame; free-run mode unchanged. Host `_tick` runs EmuHawk's loop order: latch the pad, resume yielders (`ResumeScripts(false)`), then only when unpaused clear the overrides, run one frame with the latched pad, and resume every waiter (`UpdateToolsAfter`). ARCHITECTURE §5.3 rewritten from the source. **Out of scope:** the mGBA script (its own loop; unverified on a real mGBA, docs/MGBA.md); the per-step cost this implies (n + 1 emulator turns per step of n frames), measured next with the user's log and `tools/profile_play`.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With the host running EmuHawk's loop order, the old script fails every hold-mode step with the user's exact error (reproduced before the fix: `test_screenshot_over_the_socket_through_the_real_script` and every other stepping test timed out).
  2. With the new script a one-frame step from hold mode answers, moves exactly one frame with the pad in that frame, calls `unpause` then `pause` once each, leaves nothing armed, and nothing moves afterwards; the existing five-frame and bundled-step tests pass unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/lua_host.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`.
- **Test plan:** unit – `test_hold_mode_step_unpauses_runs_and_pauses_again` (criterion 2) plus every existing script test under the corrected host (criterion 1 by construction: they fail against the old script); regression – full suite on 3.13, emulator suite on 3.11.
- **Results:** 1 new test; the corrected host alone fails 5 existing stepping tests against the old script with the user's exact message; full suite 1075 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13; emulator suite 315 passed on 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #97. **PR:** #97.
- **Completion report:** The T-110 assumption ("Lua `frameadvance` moves a paused emulator") was wrong and T-117's hold-mode check never ran on a real PC; the first real run found it in one log line. The fix follows EmuHawk's own `client.unpause` documentation ("put this and emu.yield (not frameadvance) inside a loop that runs until the desired frame") with the pad latch order read from the source. A step of n frames now costs n + 1 emulator turns, so live play in hold mode is bounded by how fast EmuHawk's loop turns; the user's next log (or `tools/profile_play --adapter bizhawk`) says whether free-run should drive live play.

### T-832 – A memory domain EmuHawk lists but cannot read
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run, finding 5)
- **Objective:** With the T-831 build the user's frame steps ran, then every step failed with `NLua.Exceptions.LuaScriptException: [string "main"]:394: A .NET exception occured in user-code: <hash>`: line 394 is `memory.read_bytes_as_array`, so one of the domains the core lists (and the adapter watches as live memory) throws on read, and the message named neither the domain nor the read. The game stopped on it every frame.
- **Scope:** Script: `domains()` peeks the first byte of each listed domain and leaves out any whose read raises, printing which to the Lua console; `read_bytes` wraps the read in `pcall` and raises "The emulator could not read N bytes at A of 'D': <reason>". Host: `unreadable` domains whose peek raises. **Out of scope:** the mGBA script (`readRange` on mGBA has not failed; revisit with a real mGBA run); learning which domain the user's core rejects (the Lua console says; the next log or screenshot tells).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With one listed domain unreadable, `hello` leaves it out, the adapter's regions leave it out, the console line names it, and the remaining reads work.
  2. A domain that starts failing after the handshake makes `read_memory` raise `EmulatorError` naming the length, address, domain and the emulator's reason, and the link stays up.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/lua_host.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`.
- **Test plan:** unit – the two criteria as tests under the `lupa` host; regression – full suite on 3.13, emulator suite on 3.11.
- **Results:** 2 new tests; full suite 1077 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13; emulator suite 317 passed on 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #98. **PR:** #98.
- **Completion report:** Which domain the user's core rejects is still unknown here (the Lua console line will say); with the probe the app never asks for it, so the game keeps stepping with the domains that work. If the rejected domain turns out to be the console's main RAM, the console line is the lead for the next task.

### T-833 – A region refused after the handshake stops the game
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-117 real run, finding 6)
- **Objective:** With the T-832 build the status line read "The emulator could not read 12 bytes at 0 of 'CPU registers': NLua.Exceptions.LuaScriptException: A .NET exception occured in user-code": NesHawk lists a 12-byte `CPU registers` pseudo-domain whose first byte reads (so the hello probe kept it) but whose whole read throws, and T-827's live-memory filter let it through. Every step failed on it, so the AI watched 0 values. The same screenshot showed EmuHawk's Lua console and screen flooded with "<path> saved." on every picture.
- **Scope:** Adapter: `"registers"` joins `NOT_LIVE_MEMORY_WORDS`; a step or read that fails with the script's "could not read N bytes at A of 'D'" message marks region D unreadable (`unreadable_regions`, cleared on disconnect, one warning in the log) and `memory_regions()` leaves it out. `MemoryCapture.reads()`/`_read_all` plan again whenever the offered regions differ from the plan (default region set only; an explicit region list still raises if one vanishes). Script: `client.displaymessages(false)` on hello, `true` when the app leaves. Host: `displaymessages` stub. **Out of scope:** reading the pseudo-domain byte by byte (nothing to learn from CPU registers); mGBA.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `cpu_registers` is not live memory.
  2. A read the script refuses marks the region unreadable, `memory_regions()` leaves it out, and the other regions still read.
  3. Through `ConnectionService` and `ObservationService`: a step whose bundled reads are refused raises once; the next `snapshot_reads()` plans without the region, the step and `advance(snapshot=...)` land a snapshot, and the unbundled `observe()` replans too.
  4. Messages are off after `hello` and on again after `bye`.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/memory.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/{lua_host.py,test_lua_script.py,test_adapter.py}`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`.
- **Test plan:** as the criteria (one parametrized row plus three script tests under the `lupa` host); regression – full suite on 3.13, emulator suite on 3.11.
- **Results:** 4 new checks; full suite 1080 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13; emulator suite 320 passed on 3.11; ruff and mypy (strict, `src`) clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #99. **PR:** #99.
- **Completion report:** The user's screenshot after this finding showed the game picture in the app for the first time (frame 2101, stepping at the clock's rate), with "Watching 0 values" because every bundled read failed on the pseudo-domain. With register domains out and refused regions dropped, the snapshots should land on the next build; the next log says what a step costs on their PC.

### T-834 – The dropped-link Game panel test raced its own pump
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (CI, found on PR #99)
- **Objective:** `test_dropped_link_is_reported_in_plain_words` failed once on `windows-latest / py3.12` (the view still held a frame after the drop) while passing everywhere else; it passed 15 of 15 locally but failed 2 of 60 runs under full CPU load. The test connects a fake emulator that drops on its second operation, waits for the pump's first frame, then ticks the pump by hand expecting that tick to be the second operation. The pump's 60 fps timer kept running meanwhile, so under load a timer tick spent the operation first: the hand tick found a capture in flight (or the link already gone) and returned False, or its frame landed after the disconnect had cleared the view.
- **Scope:** The test builds its own panel at 1 fps, pauses the clock before connecting, stops the pump after the first frame and waits until nothing is in flight, then ticks by hand; the cleared view is awaited rather than asserted at once. No production change. Also `test_profiles_a_run_on_the_simulator` (T-830), which failed on the same job on this PR with the worker's step count one above the clock's: a step in flight when the run ends is timed on the worker but never delivered to the clock, so the test now allows that one step (and picture). Running that test before the panel tests in one process also showed the tool's `app.exec()`/`app.quit()` leaving Qt's per-thread `quitNow` flag set, after which every later `QEventLoop.exec()` (pytest-qt's waits included) returned at once: 17 panel tests then timed out. The tool now runs a local `QEventLoop` for the timed run. **Out of scope:** the pump itself (its generation counter is right: a late result is dropped; only the test's assumption was wrong).
- **Acceptance criteria:** 0 failures in 60 runs under CPU load (was 2); the UI suite passes on 3.13 and 3.11.
- **Expected changes:** `tests/ui/test_game_panel.py`, `tests/ui/test_profile_play.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/tools/profile_play.py`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** the stress loop above (the scratch script runs the test 60 times with one CPU hog per core); UI suite on both Pythons.
- **Results:** 0 failures in 60 stressed runs; the profiling and panel tests pass together in one process (33, were 16 of 33); full suite 1080 passed, 10 skipped on 3.13; UI suite 189 passed on 3.11; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #100. **PR:** #100.
- **Completion report:** PR #99 was merged after one re-run of the job, with this test identified as the cause and fixed here on its own.

### T-835 – Video and audio memory are not game state
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's first recording of the AI learning on Super Mario Bros.)
- **Objective:** With the real game the app watched 1646 values, switched on 1442 rewards, and ended every go after one or two steps on a "reset" (several values back to their starting values with one dropping) or a "setback of Lives at oam[171]". The regions offered on the NES were RAM, WRAM, CIRAM (nametables), PALRAM and OAM: the last three are the PPU's picture memory (tile map, palettes, sprite attributes), rewritten every frame from the game's state in RAM. Sprite bytes bounce between a few values and often land back on their first one, so they classify as lives or progress and fire resets; they also made most of the bytes read each step. The picture is already what the screen capture shows, so watching its memory adds noise and cost without facts.
- **Scope:** `NOT_LIVE_MEMORY_WORDS` gains the video and audio domain words EmuHawk and mGBA use (`oam`, `vram`, `ciram`, `nametables`, `palram`, `palette`, `palettes`, `cgram`, `cram`, `vsram`, `apuram`), so those regions are listed by the handshake but not offered as regions. RAM, WRAM, IWRAM/EWRAM, HRAM, SRAM and battery RAM stay. T-827's test rows for `oam`, `vram`, `ciram` and `palette` flip to not live, with the reason in this section. **Out of scope:** noise in RAM itself (timers, random numbers), which the classifier's noise rule handles; the per-go overhead and the training step speed (next tasks, after the user's log).
- **Acceptance criteria:** `is_live_memory` is False for every video and audio word above and True for the system-RAM names of the NES, SNES, GB/GBC, GBA and Mega Drive; the Lua host (which lists RAM, WRAM, System Bus and the ROMs) still offers `ram` and `wram`; suites green on both Pythons.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_adapter.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/BIZHAWK.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** the parametrised `test_live_memory_is_ram_not_rom_bus_or_bios` rows: each video/audio word False, each RAM name True (`ciram_nametables`, `palram`, `cgram`, `apuram`, `cram`, `vsram`, `cgb_palettes` added False; `ewram`, `hram`, `battery_ram`, `main_ram`, `68k_ram` added True); the existing Lua-host region test; full suite on 3.13, emulator suite on 3.11.
- **Results:** 23 filter rows pass (11 video/audio words False, 11 RAM names True, plus the T-827 and T-833 rows); the Lua host still offers `ram` and `wram`; full suite green on 3.13 and the emulator suite on 3.11.
- **Commit:** see PR #101. **PR:** #101.
- **Completion report:** A one-set change in the adapter; the real effect (fewer watched values, goes that last) is for the user's next run to show. Noise inside RAM itself stays with the classifier's noise rule.

### T-836 – "Practise fast" runs the emulator flat out and rests the pictures
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's recording: about one training step a second on the real game)
- **Objective:** "Practise fast" only removed the GUI timer's gap between steps. Each step still ran its four frames at the emulator's own 100% throttle (67 ms) inside a hold-mode round trip, and every turn of EmuHawk's paused loop sleeps 15 ms (`Throttle.Step`), so a step cost 100 ms or more; meanwhile the Game panel's pump asked for 30 pictures a second over the same link, each a round trip of its own through those sleeps. The link belonged mostly to the pictures.
- **Scope:** (1) `TrainingService` asks the emulator for its top speed (`FAST_SPEED`, 64x: EmuHawk's `client.speedmode` tops out at 6400%) whenever training is running in an AI mode at `Speed.FAST`, and puts it back to 1x on pause, stop, a change to `Speed.LIVE` or a mode change; an emulator without `speed_control` (mGBA) is left as it is, and a refused call is logged, not fatal. `set_speed` announces a `TrainingStateChanged("speed")` when the speed changes. (2) `FramePump.set_fps` changes the picture rate while running; `GamePanel` drops to `PRACTISE_FPS` (2 pictures a second) while the AI practises fast and returns to its normal rate after, so the link belongs to the training steps. (3) The bridge script puts the speed back to 100% when the app leaves, if the app changed it, so a crash does not leave EmuHawk racing. **Out of scope:** free-run training (the game running on between steps), the GUI-thread training tick, the per-go save of the brain.
- **Acceptance criteria:** against the fake adapter, start at FAST sets speed 64 and stop/pause/LIVE set it back to 1, resume and a return to FAST apply it again; a refused speed call leaves training running; the pump's interval is 500 ms while the AI practises fast and the panel's own rate again after stop; the Lua host sees `speedmode(100)` when the app disconnects after changing the speed, and no speed call when it did not; suites green on both Pythons.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/game_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, tests under `tests/unit/training`, `tests/ui`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/BIZHAWK.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_fast_practice_runs_the_emulator_flat_out` (the state machine above, plus the "speed" announcement); `test_a_refused_speed_call_does_not_stop_training` (the fake adapter's `_do_set_speed` raising `EmulatorError`); `test_pictures_rest_while_the_ai_practises_fast` (MainWindow, learning panel at FAST, pump interval); `FramePump.set_fps` rejects 0; the Lua speed test extended with the disconnect; a Lua test that a plain connect and disconnect sends no `speedmode`.
- **Results:** 6 new tests pass (3 service, 2 UI, 1 Lua, plus the extended Lua speed test); full suite green on 3.13; UI, training and emulator suites on 3.11.
- **Commit:** see PR #102. **PR:** #102.
- **Completion report:** What it buys on the user's PC is for the next log to show: at 64x the four frames of a step cost a few milliseconds, leaving the two 15 ms turns of EmuHawk's paused loop as the floor (about 30 steps a second). Going past that means free-run training, a separate decision.

### T-837 – Panels that move, resize, float and tab, like an editor's
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's ask while testing: "make all the panels resizable and movable like Unity")
- **Objective:** The six panels sat in fixed splitters: the child could drag the bars between them and collapse one, but not move a panel to another side, drop one on another to make tabs, pull one out as its own window, or hide one and bring it back. DESIGN.md §10 asks for all major information visible together with the ability to expand individual sections; an editor-style layout keeps that as the default and lets anyone rearrange it.
- **Scope:** Each panel lives in a `QDockWidget` (`dock_<key>`, movable, floatable, closable, every edge allowed; nested and tabbed docks on, grouped dragging on) with no central widget, so the docks fill the window. The default layout is the one the window had: Game on the left, "What the AI is doing" over Rewards on the right, Progress, Learning and Compare AIs along the bottom. A "Panels" button on the toolbar lists the six with a tick each (`toggleViewAction`) to hide and bring back, plus "Reset layout". The arrangement is saved to `layout.bin` under the app's data folder when the window closes and put back on the next start; a missing or unreadable file means the default. Tests build windows without a layout file. The `Panel` class (header, collapse, "What's this?") is unchanged inside its dock. **Out of scope:** saving the layout per game; a menu bar.
- **Acceptance criteria:** every panel sits in a dock named after it, visible and docked by default in the areas above, with no central widget; closing a dock hides its panel and the Panels menu's tick brings it back; a floated dock and a hidden one survive close and reopen with the same layout file; "Reset layout" puts every panel back docked and visible; a missing or corrupt layout file gives the default; the window still opens and closes cleanly in the launch smoke test; UI suite green on both Pythons.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/main_window.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/app.py`, `tests/ui/test_main_window.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_all_six_panels_present_with_child_friendly_titles` asserts the docks instead of the splitter; `test_default_layout_shows_all_six_together` (areas, nothing floating, no central widget); `test_a_hidden_panel_comes_back_from_the_panels_menu`; `test_the_layout_is_remembered_between_runs` (float one, hide one, close, reopen from the same file); `test_reset_layout_puts_every_panel_back`; `test_a_bad_layout_file_is_ignored` (garbage and missing files); the existing collapse tests and every panel test still pass; the launch smoke test.
- **Results:** main window suite 22 passed (5 new); every panel test unchanged and green; full suite green on 3.13 and the UI suite on 3.11.
- **Commit:** see PR #103. **PR:** #103.
- **Completion report:** The Panel class and every panel test were untouched; only the window's composition changed. The layout file is versioned (`LAYOUT_VERSION`) so a future change to the set of docks falls back to the default rather than a half-restored window.

### T-838 – A "reset" needs a value the AI is fairly sure of
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's second recording: every go ended after exactly 3 steps by "the game started over")
- **Objective:** The event detector called a reset whenever two tracked values returned to their first values in one snapshot with one of them dropping. On the real Super Mario Bros. the RAM holds bytes that do that all the time with no restart: the 6502's stack page (`ram[502]`, `ram[508]` in the user's event line) and other scratch bytes bounce between a few values and land back on their first ones together, and at LOW confidence they count. So each go ended after a few steps, before the AI could learn anything.
- **Scope:** A reset still needs two values back at their start together with a drop (`RESET_NEEDS`), and now at least one of them at `RESET_SURE` (MEDIUM) confidence or better. Three was tried first and broke the toy racer's game over, where only lives and the checkpoint go home together. The restore-looks-like-a-reset guard in the episode runner and the reset reward rule are unchanged. **Out of scope:** leaving the stack page out of the regions (a system-specific rule; the classifier's noise rule catches the busy stack bytes and this rule covers the quiet ones).
- **Acceptance criteria:** two SURE values going home with a drop is a reset; one is not; three LOW ones are not; two where one is MEDIUM is; the toy racer's game over still registers as a reset after its flag, and the first-demonstration integration test (the point-lover runs out of lives sooner than the careful AI) still passes; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/events.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_events.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_reset_needs_two_values_returning_home_with_a_drop` unchanged; `test_reset_needs_one_value_the_ai_is_fairly_sure_of` (three LOW: no; two with one at MEDIUM: yes); the toy racer story test and the integration test unchanged; understanding, training, rewards and services suites.
- **Results:** the new detector test passes; the toy racer still registers its game over as a reset; understanding, training, rewards, services and the goals/discovery panel suites green (265); full suite green on 3.13.
- **Commit:** see PR #104. **PR:** #104.
- **Completion report:** The first cut (three values) made the integration test's point-lover last as long as the careful AI, since the toy's game over only sends lives and the checkpoint home: caught by the full suite before the push. Whether the user's goes now run their course is for the next log to show (T-840 logs why each go ended).

### T-839 – No automatic button experiments while the AI is training
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's second recording: play froze for seconds every ten seconds or so)
- **Objective:** `DiscoveryService` runs a button experiment every `experiment_every` (600) snapshots from inside `_on_memory`, that is from inside whichever step delivered the snapshot: it takes a save state, runs a trial per button and loads the state back, dozens of round trips on the real emulator, on the GUI thread, while the AI is mid-go on the same link. Each one froze the window and the game for seconds, and the trials moved the game under the AI's feet.
- **Scope:** `DiscoveryService.experiments_allowed` (default True, reset at connect) gates the scheduled experiment only; `experiment()` on demand and the panel's "Try the buttons" are unchanged. `TrainingService` turns it off at `start()` and back on at `stop()`, in every mode (in manual mode the child is driving the same link). The schedule keeps counting while held back (`experiment_due`), and in an AI mode the training service runs a due experiment between goes (`run_due_experiment`, just before the next go restores the checkpoint), so what the buttons do is still learned during training; the first-demonstration integration test depends on that (the point-lover never scored without the lane the experiment finds). **Out of scope:** experiments while the play clock runs with nobody training; moving experiments off the GUI thread.
- **Acceptance criteria:** with experiments disallowed the 250th snapshot runs none and the count stays 0; `experiment()` on demand and `run_due_experiment()` still run while disallowed; allowed again, the next snapshot runs one; training start turns them off and stop turns them on; during training every experiment runs on the first step of a go; the integration test still passes; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `tests/integration/test_first_demonstration.py` (its "picks up points" measure read the toy's score after the go ended, which a restart zeroes, so it depended on how many goes happened to hit the step limit; it now records the most points held during the go), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_scheduled_experiments_can_be_held_back` (discovery); `test_training_holds_the_button_experiments_back` and `test_due_experiments_run_between_goes_not_mid_go` (training service); services, training, integration and UI suites.
- **Results:** the three new tests pass; services, training and the learning/discovery panel suites green; full suite green on 3.13 (the first cut, which held experiments back entirely, failed the integration test: caught before the push).
- **Commit:** see PR #104. **PR:** #104.
- **Completion report:** The experiment still runs on the GUI thread when it does run (training idle); moving it to a worker is a later task if the pause between goes is not enough.

### T-840 – The log says why each go ended
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's goes ended after 3 steps and nothing on their PC said which values the detector blamed)
- **Objective:** The app's log file records every bridge command's timing but nothing about training. When a go ends on a "reset", the only trace is the Discovery panel's scrolling "What just happened" list, which the user cannot easily copy. The next log the user sends should say, per go, how it ended and, for a reset, which values the detector saw going home at which frame.
- **Scope:** `EpisodeSignals` keeps the detail and frame of the last reset it counted (`last_reset`, cleared by `start()`); `EpisodeRunner.last_reset` exposes it; `TrainingService._record` logs one INFO line per go: number, how it ended, steps, frames, points, and the reset detail when there is one. **Out of scope:** logging every step; a UI for the event log.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a go ended by a published reset logs "Go N ended by reset after S steps (F frames), +P points: <detail> (frame X)"; a go ended by the step limit logs "ended by max_steps" with no detail; `last_reset` is empty at the start of a go; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_signals_remember_the_reset_that_ended_the_go` (episode); `test_the_log_says_why_each_go_ended` (service, `caplog`); training suite.
- **Results:** both new tests pass; training suite green; full suite green on 3.13.
- **Commit:** see PR #104. **PR:** #104.
- **Completion report:** One INFO line per go; at 600-step goes that is a line every few seconds at most.

### T-841 – Confidence computed once, watch lists cached
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log: the emulator could do 20 steps a second, the app managed 6; a profile of the real window with 1,600 known values shows the snapshot handler at 80 ms a step)
- **Objective:** `Variable.confidence` recomputes from the evidence on every read, and the event detector and the long look rebuild their list of believed variables from the whole knowledge on every snapshot (two passes over 1,600 variables plus a failed decode for every variable whose region the snapshot does not carry). Compute confidence once per variable and build each watch list once per knowledge change.
- **Scope:** `Variable` stores its confidence at construction (`__post_init__`); `Knowledge.version` counts every change to its variables or relationships; `EventDetector` and `LongLook` keep their believed lists until the version or the snapshot's regions change, and leave out variables whose region the snapshot does not carry. **Out of scope:** the suggestion rebuilds (T-842) and the panel refreshes (T-843).
- **Acceptance criteria:** `Variable(...).confidence` is unchanged for every combination the existing tests use and `replace()`/`with_evidence` give the new value; `Knowledge.version` grows on add, update, support, confirm, correct, reject, remove, set/confirm/reject/remove relationship and stays put on reads; a detector built over 1,600 stale variables and 32 live ones does the per-snapshot work of 32; a variable added or changed after the first snapshot is still picked up on the next; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/events.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/longlook.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_events.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_longlook.py`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_version_counts_every_change` (knowledge); `test_confidence_follows_new_evidence` (knowledge, through `support`/`confirm`/`reject`); `test_stale_variables_cost_nothing_per_snapshot` (events: decode count via a counting snapshot); `test_a_new_variable_is_watched_from_the_next_snapshot` (events); `test_the_long_look_picks_up_knowledge_changes` (longlook); understanding suite.
- **Results:** five new tests pass; understanding suite 79 passed; full suite 1114 passed, 10 skipped (3.13). Profile of the real window with 1,610 known values against the simulator: the detector and the long look left the top of the profile (they were 21 ms of every 80 ms snapshot); steps went from 8 to 32 a second with the suggestion rebuilds (T-842) now the whole of the rest.
- **Commit:** see PR #105. **PR:** #105.
- **Completion report:** `confidence` is a field worked out in `__post_init__` (`init=False`, left out of `to_dict`), so `replace()` recomputes it; `Knowledge.version` moves on the seven mutators; the watch lists are keyed on `(version, snapshot regions)` so a variable for memory the emulator no longer shares (the user's stale `ciram_nametables` beliefs) costs nothing per snapshot.

### T-842 – Suggestions rebuilt in linear time, not every step
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** Each relationship the finder reports makes the reward and goal services rebuild every suggestion: `merge_suggestions` searches the goal for each progress variable (quadratic) and `Goal.add` recomputes the completed set per dependency check; `apply_suggestions` rebuilds 2,400 behaviours. With 1,600 values that is 210 ms, and it happens on most steps.
- **Scope:** index objectives by trigger key in `merge_suggestions`; `Goal.observe` and `Goal.ready` build one completed set per pass; a `RelationshipWatch` in the reward and goal services rebuilds suggestions on a relationship update only when the set of confident relationships (id and confidence level) differs from last time, and always on a variable change. `Knowledge.version` now counts variable changes only, since the finder rewrites every relationship's rates on every event and the watch lists never read them. **Out of scope:** changing what is suggested.
- **Acceptance criteria:** suggestions identical before and after for the existing tests; ten relationship-only `DiscoveryUpdated` events with nothing crossing a confidence level call `apply_suggestions`/`merge_suggestions` once; a relationship reaching MEDIUM refreshes (the progress boost appears); a variable change still refreshes at once; merging 400 progress objectives and observing events stays well under 2 s; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/rewards/goals.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/rewards/suggestions.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/rewards.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/goals.py`, tests, docs.
- **Test plan:** `test_relationship_updates_refresh_suggestions_sparingly` (rewards and goals services, counting the rebuilds with `monkeypatch`); `test_merge_handles_many_objectives_quickly` (goals, 400 objectives, dependency chain completes in order); `test_version_counts_every_change` adjusted; suites.
- **Results:** three new tests pass; rewards, services and understanding suites 202 passed; full suite 1117 passed, 10 skipped (3.13). Profile of the 1,610-value window: 8 → 43 steps a second with T-841; the suggestion rebuilds now run on 34 of 126 snapshots (the simulator's relationships still cross levels often early on) at 57 ms each instead of 210 ms on 30 of 82.
- **Commit:** see PR #105. **PR:** #105.
- **Completion report:** The remaining per-step cost in the profile is the Discovery panel rebuilding its 1,610-row list on every update (T-843). `suggest_behaviours` itself (2,400 `Behaviour` constructions, 40 ms) is untouched; it runs only on real changes now.

### T-843 – Panels redraw a few times a second, not every step
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** The Discovery panel rebuilds its whole 1,600-row list on every `DiscoveryUpdated` (30 ms here, more on the user's PC), the Rewards panel rebuilds 2,400 rows on every `RewardProfileChanged`, and both arrive on most steps. Coalesce: an update marks the panel dirty and one refresh runs at most every 250 ms, on the GUI thread's next free moment.
- **Scope:** `ui/coalesce.py`: `Coalescer` runs the first request at once and folds the ones that follow within 250 ms into one run at the end of the window (`request`, `flush`, `cancel`, `pending`). The Discovery panel sends every `DiscoveryUpdated` through it and rebuilds the variable list only when a variable was added or changed (events alone refill the events, relationships and summary). The Rewards panel coalesces "suggestions" changes only; a person's own control, a personality or a load refreshes at once. **Out of scope:** other panels (they already refresh cheaply).
- **Acceptance criteria:** the first of a burst shows at once and the rest cause one more rebuild when the window closes, with the list current then; events alone never rebuild the variable list; a person's reward change shows at once even inside the window; existing panel tests that assert straight after a burst flush first; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/coalesce.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/rewards_panel.py`, tests, docs.
- **Test plan:** `test_first_request_runs_at_once_and_a_burst_runs_once_more` (coalesce); `test_many_updates_rebuild_the_list_once` (discovery panel); `test_many_profile_changes_rebuild_the_tree_once` (rewards panel, including the person's change); UI suite; full suite.
- **Results:** three new tests pass; four existing panel tests gained a `refresher.flush()` where they asserted straight after a burst; UI suite 199 passed; full suite 1131 passed, 10 skipped (3.13). Profile of the 1,610-value window against the simulator: 60 steps a second (8 before T-841); the remaining per-step work is the state encoder and the suggestion rebuilds that real changes still cause.
- **Commit:** see PR #105. **PR:** #105.
- **Completion report:** A first cut deferred every refresh, which made a person's own change wait up to 250 ms and broke the tests that model that; the leading-edge run and the "suggestions only" rule in the Rewards panel fix both. On the user's PC the panel rebuilds were the largest per-step cost left after T-841/T-842 (real widgets, 1,600 rows).

### T-844 – The AI works out which button gets the game going
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log: 15 identical goes of 35 steps on Super Mario Bros.; the AI was watching the title-screen demo, and the user wants it to work out for itself how to start the game and how to go again when the lives are gone, with nothing coded in per game)
- **Objective:** When the game ignores the AI's buttons (a title screen with a demo, a game-over screen), the AI must find out by itself which button gets the game going, remember it for that game, press it whenever the game stops listening, and carry on practising from in the game. No button name is assumed: the search tries every button the console has.
- **Scope:** `understanding/wakeup.py`: `inputs_matter(connection, buttons)` saves the spot, runs the same frames with nothing held and with each button held in turn (ending with a few idle frames so a controller mirror settles) and says whether the memory ends up different; `find_wake_button(connection, buttons, first=...)` presses each button in turn (the remembered one first), waits for the game to settle, and returns the first after which inputs matter, leaving the game there. `Knowledge.wake_button` remembers it (saved with the knowledge; shown in the Discovery summary). `EpisodeRunner` keeps a fingerprint of each go (the state keys, bounded) and whether the actions varied, presses the known wake button when a screen stops changing, and ends the go with `ended_by="stuck"` when none is known. `TrainingService` runs the search before the next go when a go ended stuck, or when two goes in a row ran identically although the AI pressed different buttons, then takes a new checkpoint in the game so later goes start there; the log says what it found. The old hard-coded "press Start when stuck" default goes. **Out of scope:** games whose start needs a sequence of buttons (the search presses one at a time); menus with choices.
- **Acceptance criteria:** on the toy racer's game-over screen (only Start restarts it) `inputs_matter` is False and `find_wake_button` returns "start" with the game running afterwards; with Start left out of the buttons it returns None and the game is back where it was; `Knowledge.wake_button` round-trips through JSON; a runner with no wake button ends a stuck go with "stuck"; the training service on a game-over screen finds Start by itself, logs it, remembers it in the knowledge, and the next goes are played with lives going down again; two identical goes with differing actions also trigger the search; nothing in `src/` names "start" as the button to press; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/wakeup.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_wakeup.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_inputs_matter_in_play_but_not_on_the_game_over_screen`, `test_find_wake_button_learns_start_from_the_game_over_screen`, `test_no_button_helps_leaves_the_game_where_it_was`, `test_misuse` (wakeup); `test_wake_button_round_trips` (knowledge); `test_a_stuck_go_ends_when_no_wake_button_is_known`, `test_the_known_wake_button_is_pressed_when_stuck`, `test_goes_carry_a_fingerprint` (episode); `test_the_ai_works_out_which_button_starts_the_game`, `test_identical_goes_trigger_the_search`, `test_goes_look_identical` (service); training and understanding suites; full suite.
- **Results:** eleven new tests pass (wakeup 4, knowledge 1, episode 3, service 3); the game-over unstick test now passes the button explicitly and the learning-panel goes list knows "the screen stopped changing"; understanding and training suites green; full suite 1128 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #105. **PR:** #105.
- **Completion report:** First cut accepted a candidate as soon as "inputs matter" afterwards, which on the toy racer's game-over screen made Up the answer (Start, among the probe's buttons, restarted the game during the probe). The rule is now two-part: the press must change something lasting compared with just waiting, and the *playing* buttons (the action space, which keeps Start and Select out) must matter after it. A second cut restored the checkpoint before searching, which on an in-game checkpoint made Up the answer again; the search runs from the screen that ignores the AI. A go stuck part-way (lives gone) does not search: the next go's restore is the right answer there. Limitation: a start that needs a sequence of presses, or a menu choice, is not found; the search is logged so the user's log shows what was tried.

### T-845 – Values that cycle back to their start are not a restart
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log from the T-844 build: every go ended "by reset after 3 steps (12 frames)" blaming the same eleven "Score"/"Progress" bytes that go back to their first value every few frames, so the wake-button search never got a chance)
- **Objective:** A group of values the AI believes in that returns to its starting values every few frames (an animation or demo counter on Super Mario Bros.' title screen) ends every go within a second as a "reset". The AI must notice that pattern itself, stop treating those values' return as the game starting over, and say so in the knowledge and the log, so goes get long enough for the T-844 search to run.
- **Scope:** `GameEvent.variables` names the values a reset blamed; `EpisodeSignals.last_reset_ids` keeps them; `TrainingService` counts consecutive goes ended by a reset within `RESET_SUSPECT_STEPS` (3) steps blaming the same group, and after `RESET_SUSPECT_GOES` (3) calls `DiscoveryService.doubt_reset(ids)`, which adds `cycles` evidence (weight 0, so confidence is untouched) to each and announces the change (saved, shown in the panel's evidence). `EventDetector` leaves a variable with `cycles` evidence out of the reset judgement (its other events still fire). **Out of scope:** re-kinding those values (the long look's job).
- **Acceptance criteria:** a value with `cycles` evidence returning home does not count toward a reset, before and after a knowledge reload; a reset event names the variables it blamed; three goes in a row ended by the same group within 3 steps make the service doubt it once, log it, and the next goes run past 3 steps; two such goes, or three blaming different groups, do nothing; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/events.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, tests for each, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_cycling_value_no_longer_counts_toward_a_reset` and `test_reset_events_name_the_values_they_blame` (events); `test_signals_keep_the_ids_a_reset_blamed` (episode); `test_doubt_reset_adds_the_evidence_once_and_announces` (discovery); `test_three_short_resets_from_the_same_values_teach_the_ai_they_cycle` and `test_short_resets_that_differ_are_left_alone` (service); full suite.
- **Results:** six new tests pass; understanding, training and services suites 234 passed; full suite 1137 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #106. **PR:** #106.
- **Completion report:** The evidence has weight 0 on purpose: the AI's belief that the byte is a score is untouched (the long look, not this rule, decides that); only the reset judgement changes. Three goes within 3 steps was chosen because a restore puts values home at frame 0 of a go (already ignored) and a real restart cannot follow within 12 frames of it; a legitimate quick death three times in a row from a pinned spot would need the same values to go home inside 12 frames of each go, which the restore makes impossible.

### T-846 – A restart is most of the game going home, not a few values
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log from the T-845 build: 14 goes in 50 seconds, every one ended "by reset" after 1–2 steps, each blaming a different handful of "Score"/"Progress"/"Flag" bytes, so the three-in-a-row rule of T-845 fired only twice and the wake-button search still never ran)
- **Objective:** With 1,600 believed values, some handful is back at its first value on nearly every step while hundreds of others are not. That is not the game starting over. A reset must mean most of what has moved goes home together.
- **Scope:** `EventDetector` counts the watched values that have ever left their first value (`moved`, kept as a counter so a snapshot costs nothing extra) and declares a reset only when the values going home are at least `RESET_SHARE` (half) of them, on top of T-838's two values with one at MEDIUM and a drop. **Out of scope:** per-go re-arming of the detector (it watches since connect, and a restore already puts values home at frame 0, which is ignored); pruning the polluted knowledge.
- **Acceptance criteria:** two of six moved values going home is no reset, three of six is; a value that never moved does not count against a reset; `reset()` clears the count; every earlier reset test still passes unchanged; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/events.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_events.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_reset_needs_most_of_the_moved_values_to_go_home`, `test_values_that_never_moved_do_not_count_against_a_reset`; the existing reset tests (T-838, T-845) unchanged; full suite.
- **Results:** two new tests pass; understanding, training, services and integration suites 237 passed; full suite 1139 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #107. **PR:** #107.
- **Completion report:** Half was chosen so that a real restart seen from mid-game (the detector first saw the game after a connect, so "home" is wherever it was then) still counts when the bulk of the state returns, while the title demo's handful out of hundreds never does. The user's log also showed a `dictionary changed size during iteration` in `Knowledge.confident_relationships` from the goal service's handler; that is T-847.

### T-847 – The knowledge can be read while another thread writes it
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log from the T-845 build: `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration` in `Knowledge.confident_relationships`, from the goal service's handler of a `DiscoveryUpdated` while the relationship finder was rewriting the relationships on another thread)
- **Objective:** Memory snapshots are published from the play clock's worker thread (T-818) and from the training step on the GUI thread, and every discovery handler runs on the publisher's thread, so the finder can rewrite the relationships while a handler on the other thread reads them. The knowledge must give a consistent view to any thread, and the discovery service must observe one snapshot at a time.
- **Scope:** `Knowledge` keeps an `RLock`; every mutator and every reader that walks the variables or relationships (iteration, `at`, `by_kind`, `confident`, `needs_help`, `relationships`, `confident_relationships`, `to_dict`) runs under it, and iteration walks a copy. `DiscoveryService` keeps an `RLock` around `_on_memory`, `review`, `experiment`, `doubt_reset` and `_adopt`, so the detector, finder and long look are driven by one thread at a time. **Out of scope:** moving the handlers onto one thread (a larger change than the log asks for).
- **Acceptance criteria:** a thread rewriting 2,000 relationships and variables in a loop while another reads them with `confident_relationships()`, `list(knowledge)` and `to_dict()` raises nothing (the same test fails without the lock); memory observed from several threads at once is counted in full and no handler fails; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_knowledge_can_be_read_while_another_thread_rewrites_it` (knowledge, shown failing first); `test_memory_from_several_threads_is_observed_one_at_a_time` (discovery); full suite.
- **Results:** both new tests fail without their lock (three runs each) and pass with it; understanding and services suites 54 passed; full suite 1141 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #108. **PR:** #108.
- **Completion report:** The lock is an `RLock` because the mutators call the readers (`add` calls `at`, `support` calls `update`), and `__iter__` hands out an iterator over a copy so a walk never holds the lock. The discovery lock also covers `review`, `experiment` and `doubt_reset`, which drive the same detector, finder and long look; `_announce` runs under it, which is fine because the handlers never observe memory. Handlers still run on whichever thread published; moving them onto one thread would be its own task.

### T-848 – Every go starts by checking the game listens to the AI
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log from the T-847 build: one go of 125 steps on the title demo, stopped by hand, and the T-844 search never ran because it only started after a go stuck from its first step or two identical goes; the user: "it still cant work out how to get the game started")
- **Objective:** Waiting for a go to prove the game is ignoring the AI takes too long on a title demo that keeps the screen moving. The AI must ask the question directly: at the start of a go, from the spot goes start from, do my buttons change anything? If not, find the button that gets the game going right away.
- **Scope:** `TrainingService` probes with `inputs_matter` (T-844) at the first go after Start and after any go that ended stuck or by a reset, from the restored checkpoint; a game that does not listen triggers `_wake_up` at once. The T-844 triggers (stuck from the start, identical goes) stay. **Out of scope:** probing before every go (two seconds of the link each time while watching live), the per-step cost (T-849).
- **Acceptance criteria:** with the game on a screen that ignores the pad, the very first step after Start works out the button and plays inside the game; a game that listens is probed once at Start and not again after goes that end by the step limit, but again after one that ends by a reset; no save states: no probe, no error; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_first_go_checks_the_game_listens`, `test_a_listening_game_is_probed_only_after_a_go_that_went_wrong`; the T-844 tests unchanged; full suite.
- **Results:** two new tests pass; the two T-844 trigger tests now switch the probe off so they still exercise their own path; training and integration suites 72 passed; full suite 1143 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #109. **PR:** #109.
- **Completion report:** The probe costs about 120 frames of the link (three runs of 40 frames) plus two save-state loads, so it runs only at Start and after a go that ended stuck or by a reset, not before every go. It runs from the restored checkpoint, which is where the goes start from, so a pinned spot inside the game is never searched from. `_listens` returns True when there is no checkpoint, no save states or the buttons are unknown, so an emulator without save states behaves exactly as before.

### T-849 – The long look stops pairing values that cannot be checkpoint counters
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log from the T-847 build: 7 steps per 5 seconds with the link taking 70 ms a step, so the app spent about half a second of its own time per step; a profile with 3,400 believed values over memory that churns like a NES title demo put `LongLook.observe` first at 35 ms a snapshot, from one `Counter` per (stepper, driver) pair per step up)
- **Objective:** The long look records, for every believed flag/progress/position value that steps up, where every believed position/progress value was at that moment: quadratic in the believed values, and on a title demo hundreds of them step every frame. A value that steps up nearly every frame, or that has shown more than `FEW_VALUES` faces, cannot be a checkpoint counter, and a (stepper, driver) pair whose recorded driver values can no longer form `FEW_PLACES` places cannot show "the same few places"; the look must stop spending on them.
- **Scope:** `_Stepper` gains `seen`, `tracking` and `dead`; once `len(distinct) > FEW_VALUES`, or after `SETTLED` (64) observations when it stepped up on more than one observation in `TOO_FAST` (8), its places and rounds are dropped and the driver loop is skipped; a pair whose recorded driver values exceed `MAX_PLACE_VALUES` (`FEW_PLACES * (PLACE_SLACK + 1)` = 40) is dropped and skipped. `LongLook.tracking` lists the steppers still paired. **Out of scope:** the reward and goal suggestion rebuilds (next), the review's own cost.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a timer that steps every frame and a counter cycling 0..7 stop being paired with drivers while the checkpoint and lap of the test track keep their evidence; a pair that stepped at more than 40 driver values is dropped; with 800 fast values and 800 drivers every stepper is unpaired after 64 observations; the T-305 long-look tests pass unchanged; the churn profile shows the long look well under the step; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/longlook.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_longlook.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_values_that_cannot_be_checkpoint_counters_stop_being_paired`, `test_a_pair_with_too_many_places_is_dropped`, `test_many_fast_values_are_all_unpaired_after_settling`; full suite.
- **Results:** three new tests pass; the T-305/T-709 long-look tests unchanged; full suite 1144 passed, 10 skipped before the rebase onto T-848 and 1146 passed, 10 skipped after it. Churn profile (3,400 values, 300 random and 200 cycling bytes a frame): `LongLook.observe` fell from 35 ms a snapshot to the warm-up only; what remained was the event storm (T-850).
- **Commit:** see PR #110. **PR:** #110.
- **Completion report:** The pairing work is now bounded per stepper: at most `FEW_VALUES` faces, at most one step up in eight observations once settled, and at most 40 recorded driver values per pair. The first 64 observations of a fast value still pay for the pairing (about two seconds of CPU spread over them on 1,600 values), which is the price of not judging a counter before it has had a chance. A stepper dropped as too fast keeps its `distinct` set, so a flag that reached a third value still gets `third-value` evidence at review.

### T-850 – The AI acts on its best few guesses of each kind
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the same churn profile after T-849: the event detector raised 1,000 events in 7 snapshots from 3,400 believed values, the finder stored 41,000 relationships from their pairs, each event was matched against 5,000 reward behaviours, and the state the agent learns from had 3,400 parts; the user's Super Mario Bros. knowledge holds 1,600 such guesses, nearly all wrong)
- **Objective:** A game has one lives counter, a score, a handful of progress values and a few flags. Believing 400 of each, and watching, rewarding, pairing and learning from all of them, is why every step costs so much and why the AI cannot learn: it never sees the same state twice. The knowledge keeps every guess, but what the AI acts on must be its best few of each kind.
- **Scope:** `Knowledge.believed(at_least, per_kind=BELIEVED_PER_KIND)` returns, per kind, the strongest non-rejected variables at or above `at_least` (confirmed first, then confidence, then evidence weight, then address), at most `per_kind` (16) of each. The event detector, the long look, the state encoder, the reward suggestions (and their cause lookup), the goal suggestions and the goal panel's trigger options read it instead of walking the whole knowledge. The Discovery panel and the knowledge itself still show everything. **Out of scope:** pruning or demoting the guesses (they stay, and a better one takes a seat when its evidence grows).
- **Acceptance criteria:** with 40 LOW flags the detector watches the 16 strongest; a confirmed variable always has a seat; the state encoder and the reward suggestions use at most 16 of a kind; the earlier tests for each consumer pass unchanged; the churn profile's events, relationships and rewards per step are bounded; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `understanding/events.py`, `understanding/longlook.py`, `learning/state.py`, `rewards/suggestions.py`, `rewards/goals.py`, `services/goals.py`, tests for knowledge, events, state and suggestions, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_believed_keeps_the_best_few_of_each_kind` (knowledge); `test_only_the_best_few_of_a_kind_are_watched` (events); `test_encoder_uses_the_best_few_of_a_kind` (state); `test_suggestions_cover_the_best_few_of_a_kind` (suggestions); full suite.
- **Results:** four new tests pass; two earlier tests now expect the budget (the long look's 400 fast values become 32 watched, the 400-objective merge becomes 16) and the detector drops the watch of a value that lost its seat; understanding, learning, rewards, services, training and integration suites 349 passed; full suite 1150 passed, 10 skipped. Churn profile (3,410 values, 300 random and 200 cycling bytes a frame, LIVE): 100 steps a second against the simulator, up from 6 steps in 13 s before T-849/T-850; rewards on 97 instead of 5,010; relationships 304 instead of 41,409; the app's own time per step about 23 ms.
- **Commit:** see PR #111. **PR:** #111.
- **Completion report:** The budget is per kind so a weak lives guess is never crowded out by strong flags. It applies where the AI acts (watching, pairing, rewarding, setting goals, building the state) and nowhere a person looks (the Discovery panel, the knowledge file, `needs_help`), so the person still sees and can confirm or reject every guess, and a confirmed one always has a seat. The detector forgets the watch of a value that lost its seat, and `believed(regions=...)` lets memory the emulator no longer shares not take seats from live values. Sixteen is generous for any one kind of game value and keeps the state the agent learns from small enough to see the same situation twice.

### T-851 – The wake-button search presses a promising button again
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 22:34 log: at Start the AI found the game not listening, tried all eight buttons in four seconds and logged "No button got the game going"; the 600-step go that followed earned 9,557 points from the title demo. On Super Mario Bros. pressing Start during the demo only returns to the title screen, which is a lasting change but still ignores the pad; the game starts on the *next* press, and then a "WORLD 1-1" splash of a few seconds ignores the pad too)
- **Objective:** A button that changes something lasting but leaves the game deaf is the most promising candidate, not a failure: press it again. The search gives each candidate up to a few presses while every press keeps changing something lasting and the playing buttons still do not matter, and waits longer after a press so a splash screen cannot hide the answer. Nothing is assumed about which button or how many presses.
- **Scope:** `find_wake_button` gains `presses` (default `DEFAULT_PRESSES = 3`): after a press whose outcome differs from just waiting it asks whether the playing buttons matter; if not it saves that spot, plays the wait once more from it as the new comparison, and presses the same button again from there, up to `presses` times; a press that changes nothing lasting moves on to the next candidate. `DEFAULT_WAIT` rises from 180 to 300 frames (five seconds). The log names the number of presses when more than one was needed. **Out of scope:** the training service (it already passes defaults), the emulator speed during the search (batched frames already run unthrottled: eight buttons took four seconds), and the points the demo earns (a separate task on values that move while the AI is not in control).
- **Acceptance criteria:** on a game whose game-over screen needs Start twice (the first press reaches a "title" that still ignores the pad) the search returns Start and leaves the game running; with `presses=1` it still returns None and leaves the game where it was; a button that changes something lasting on every press but never makes the game listen is pressed at most `presses` times and the search ends with the game where it was; the T-844 tests pass unchanged; `presses=0` is refused; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/wakeup.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_wakeup.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_button_that_needs_two_presses_is_found` (a Toy Racer whose game-over screen first goes to a title that ignores the pad), `test_one_press_only_misses_it`, `test_a_button_that_keeps_changing_things_is_not_pressed_forever`, `test_misuse` (presses=0); the whole wake-up test file; full suite.
- **Results:** three new tests pass and `test_misuse` covers `presses=0`; `test_one_press_only_misses_it` shows the old one-press search returning None on the same screen; the T-844 tests pass unchanged; understanding, training and integration suites 169 passed; full suite 1153 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #112. **PR:** #112.
- **Completion report:** The test double (`TwoPressRacer`) models exactly what the log showed: a fresh Start press changes a byte and the pad stays ignored until the next fresh press. Each repeat press is compared with just waiting from the same spot, so the "changed something lasting" test still means what it meant; the noise set from the start of the search is reused. The bound of three presses keeps a menu toggle from being pressed forever, and the longer wait costs nothing noticeable because batched frames run unthrottled over the link (the whole eight-button search took four seconds in the log).

### T-852 – No go runs while the game ignores the AI
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 22:34 log: after the search gave up, Go 1 ran 600 steps on the title demo and earned 9,557 points from values that move on their own; the user: "it's getting all it's points while the demo menu screen plays still")
- **Objective:** Points earned while the AI is not in control mean nothing and teach the brain and the knowledge wrong things. When the game is found not to listen and the search for a button fails, the AI should let the game run a few seconds (an intro may still be playing) and ask again, a few times at most, and then stop training and say so in plain words instead of running goes on a demo.
- **Scope:** `TrainingService._ensure_runner`: when the game does not listen and `_wake_up` finds no button, it lets the game run `DEAF_WAIT` (300) frames, takes a new checkpoint there and probes again, up to `DEAF_TRIES` (3) searches in all; when they all fail it ends training (status IDLE, `TrainingStateChanged` with reason "not listening") and logs `NOT_LISTENING`. The Learning panel shows that text as its note. An emulator that cannot try buttons (no save states) keeps the old behaviour. **Out of scope:** the junk score/progress guesses already in a knowledge file (later), and the search itself (T-851).
- **Acceptance criteria:** with the search failing every time, Start then stepping runs no go, ends with the service idle, three searches, the warning logged and the panel's note explaining; with the search failing once and succeeding on the second try, a go runs in the game; without save states nothing changes; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/learning_panel.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `tests/ui/test_learning_panel.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_training_stops_instead_of_playing_a_game_that_ignores_the_ai`, `test_a_second_search_after_a_wait_can_still_find_the_button`, the existing no-save-states case; `test_the_note_says_when_the_game_ignores_the_ai` (panel); full suite.
- **Results:** three new tests pass; the T-844/T-848 tests pass unchanged; training and learning-panel suites 49 passed; full suite 1156 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #113. **PR:** #113.
- **Completion report:** The search already costs a few seconds, so three searches with five seconds of play between them is under half a minute before the AI gives up, and a fresh checkpoint before each probe means an intro that finished during the wait is seen. Stopping is announced as its own state change ("not listening") so the panel can explain in plain words without the service knowing about the panel; Start works again straight away. The game is left where it was, so the person can get it going by hand.

### T-853 – A long step waits for its frames, and the search runs flat out
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 23:04 log: the search's first 308-frame step at normal speed took 5.1 seconds, the link's 5-second call timeout fired ("step ×5 avg 1070 ms max 5003 ms") and the app dropped the link; the user: "that one disconnects from bizhawk shortly after i press start". The 22:34 search had run at "Practise fast", so the same steps took milliseconds)
- **Objective:** A step of many frames at normal speed takes as long as those frames take, so the link must wait for it rather than give up at a fixed five seconds. And the search for a wake button (and the T-852 wait) should not make the person watch a demo for a minute: when the emulator can change speed, run it flat out for the search and put the speed back afterwards.
- **Scope:** `BizHawkAdapter._do_step` passes a timeout of the call timeout plus the frames at half of 60 a second (`frames / SLOWEST_FPS`, `SLOWEST_FPS = 30`), so 300 frames may take up to 15 seconds; other calls keep the call timeout. `BizHawkSimulator` gains `frame_delay_s` so a test can make frames slow. `TrainingService._flat_out()` (a context manager) sets `FAST_SPEED` around `_listens` and `_wake_up_or_wait` when the emulator has speed control and the speed is not already fast, and restores 1x (or leaves fast on) afterwards; a refused call is logged and ignored. **Out of scope:** mGBA (no speed control), the search itself.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a 6-frame step on a simulator whose frames take 30 ms succeeds with a 0.2-second call timeout; one whose frames take 100 ms still raises the disconnect error; `read` keeps the plain timeout; the training service runs the probe and the search at 64x on the fake (speed 64 while they run, 1.0 afterwards at LIVE, 64 kept at FAST); an emulator without speed control is left alone; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `emulator/bizhawk/simulator.py`, `training/service.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_long_step_waits_for_its_frames` (adapter); `test_the_search_runs_flat_out_and_puts_the_speed_back` (training); full suite.
- **Results:** both new tests pass and the adapter test fails without the change ("DID NOT RAISE": before, calls used the connect timeout); emulator, training and services suites 488 passed; full suite 1158 passed, 10 skipped. The Lua-script suite failed once (`test_handshake_hold_mode_and_reconnect`) in a run alongside 480 other tests and passed in three runs on its own and in the full suite; it does not touch this change and is noted here rather than hidden.
- **Commit:** see PR #114. **PR:** #114.
- **Completion report:** Investigating the timeout showed `call_timeout_s` had never governed calls: `open_link` built the link with the connect timeout, which happened to be five seconds too. Both now mean what they say, and a step's wait grows with its frames at a floor of 30 a second so a slow PC is covered without a long wait masking a dead link. Running the probe and search flat out needs no new emulator capability: the training service already switched speed for "Practise fast", and the context manager restores exactly the state that mode wants.

### T-854 – A late answer from the emulator is skipped, not fatal
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (seen while fixing T-853: when a call times out, the emulator's answer still arrives later, and the next call reads it, sees the wrong request id, raises a protocol error and drops the link. That is how one slow step in the user's 23:04 log became a disconnect)
- **Objective:** One slow answer should cost one call, not the link. When the link is waiting for the answer to request N and reads an answer to an earlier request, it is a late answer to a call that already gave up: skip it and keep waiting for N within the same deadline.
- **Scope:** `Link.call` reads until its deadline, skipping well-formed answers whose id is below the current request's (logged at INFO); an answer to a later or unknown id, non-JSON and the rest stay protocol errors that close the link. **Out of scope:** retrying the timed-out call itself.
- **Acceptance criteria:** after a timed-out call, the next call succeeds when both answers then arrive in order; an answer to a future id still closes the link; the deadline covers the skipping (a stream of late answers does not wait forever); suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_protocol.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_late_answer_is_skipped`; the T-824 protocol tests unchanged; full suite.
- **Results:** the new test passes; the earlier protocol and adapter tests pass unchanged once the timeout message reports the whole wait rather than the remaining slice; BizHawk suites 130 passed three times running; the Lua-script suite (whose handshake test had failed once in a loaded run during T-853 and once during this task) passed twelve runs in a row under the full suite's load, so no cause was found and the failure is still noted; full suite 1159 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #115. **PR:** #115.
- **Completion report:** The deadline is shared by the skipping, so a stream of stale answers cannot stall a call beyond its timeout, and an answer to an id not yet sent is still treated as a broken link, since that can only mean the two ends disagree about the conversation.
