### T-890 – BizHawk serves steps within a turn
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user: "there doesn't seem to be much difference in speed between watch it play and practise fast, if anything practise fast is slower")
- **Objective:** The user's 18:06 log shows every `step` at 36–46 ms and every other command at ~18 ms, in "Watch it play" and "Practise fast" alike, with "speed ×1" sent: the 64x speed never mattered. A plain command is one turn of EmuHawk's loop, and a paused turn is throttled to about a frame, so the turn that serves a request costs ~18 ms whatever the speed; a four-frame step at 64x costs two such turns. "Practise fast" ran about 26 steps a second against 15 live, and felt the same. The script now serves back-to-back requests within one turn: after answering a request in hold mode it waits up to `SERVE_WAIT_S` (4 ms) on the socket (`socket.select`) and serves what arrives, at most `SERVE_MOST` (8) between yields so EmuHawk's window keeps its turns (a step hands the loop back frame by frame anyway); a training loop that answers within a few milliseconds never pays the serving turn. Where the other ~18 ms goes (a paused latch turn, or slow frame turns) cannot be told from here, so every step answer reports `took.latch` and `took.frames`, kept in the link log as `step:latch` and `step:frames`; the next log decides whether the pad-latching yield moves. Tried and dropped: unpausing before the latch yield so the latch turn runs the first frame, which under the loop order the test host models (frame decision after the yield resume) runs an extra frame with the stale pad in the serving turn.
- **Scope:** `emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`: `wait_for_request`, `service` (served count), `served_since_yield`, `now()`, `took` in `run_frames` and the step body. `emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`: `Link.note`. `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`: `_note_took`. Test host: `socket.select`, `socket.gettime`, `turns`, `answered_at`, `on_select`. **Out of scope:** the app's tick scheduling; mGBA; moving the latch yield (waits for the log).
- **Acceptance criteria:** (1) Two 4-frame steps pipelined on the wire are answered 4 turns apart (the second served in the turn of the first answer, costing only its frames). (2) A third step sent from inside the wait is answered 4 turns after the second. (3) A turn that served nothing does not wait. (4) A step answer carries `took` with `latch` and `frames`, and the adapter's link summary lists `step:latch` and `step:frames`. (5) The pad still reaches the very first frame (existing tests). (6) Suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/lua_host.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_protocol.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py::test_a_step_costs_its_frames_in_turns_and_requests_share_a_turn`, `::test_the_link_log_keeps_the_scripts_step_timing`; `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_protocol.py::test_link_notes_timings_the_emulator_reports`; the existing hold-mode, pad and free-run tests; full suite.
- **Results:** 3/3 quality gates passed. Full suite 1269 passed, 10 skipped; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #145. **PR:** #145.
- **Completion report:** Back-to-back requests are served within a turn and the step answer says where its time goes. Honest limitation: no EmuHawk runs here; the saving is reasoned from the user's log and the loop model, and the next log (`step ×…`, `step:latch`, `step:frames`) is the measurement.

### T-891 – An old bridge script is refused with instructions
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 22:43 log and video after the T-887–T-890 build: "Frame step failed: Backend stepped without reporting the pad it watched", no `step:latch` lines, and the Game panel blank: BizHawk's Lua Console still had the previous build's script open)
- **Objective:** T-887 and T-890 changed what the scripts answer, but the protocol version stayed 1, so an old script connected fine and then every watched step of the play clock failed (the base refuses a watched answer without `pressed`): the Game panel showed nothing and the person could not play. The scripts are now version 2 and the adapter's existing version check refuses an old one at Connect with a message that says what to do: which emulator, which version it runs and needs, and to remove that script in the Lua console and open the one that came with this app (its full path, so the built app's `_internal\…` location is spelled out). Rule for every later task: a change to what a script answers bumps `SCRIPT_VERSION` in both scripts and `PROTOCOL_VERSION`.
- **Scope:** `emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py::PROTOCOL_VERSION = 2`; both scripts' `SCRIPT_VERSION = 2`; `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py::script_version_message(found, emulator, script)`; `docs/BIZHAWK.md` step 2 says to reopen the script after every new build. **Out of scope:** tolerating an old script (a refusal with instructions is clearer than a half-working link).
- **Acceptance criteria:** (1) A simulator answering version 1 is refused with "an older bridge script", the versions, "remove that script", "open the one that came with this app" and the script's path; version 3 with "a different bridge script". (2) The real scripts answer 2 and the Lua hosts connect. (3) Suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/mgba/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_adapter.py`, `docs/BIZHAWK.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_adapter.py::test_wrong_version_and_garbage_are_protocol_errors` (reworded), `::test_an_older_script_is_named_as_older_with_its_replacement`; the Lua host suites; full suite.

- **Completion report:** An old script is refused with the file to open. CI (ubuntu 3.12) failed once in `tests/ui/test_profile_play.py` with 31 steps against 30 frames: the report counted a step still on the worker, whose frames land later; it now counts landed steps only, read on the GUI thread together with the frames. The log line the user saw ("Backend stepped without reporting the pad it watched") cannot recur from a version mismatch.

### T-893 – Steps that follow each other keep BizHawk running
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 22:47 video: "the options at the bottom of the bizhawk screen is going 1000 mph")
- **Objective:** Every hold-mode step unpaused EmuHawk before its frames and paused it after, so while a person played (15 to 26 steps a second) the status bar toggled its paused state at that rate. A step now leaves the emulator running; the turn waits up to `PAUSE_AFTER_S` (50 ms, three frames) for the app's next request and pauses only when none comes. A step that arrives while running skips the latch turn (its `took.latch` is 0), since the pad set just before `emu.frameadvance()` is latched at the top of the turn that runs the frame, exactly as between frames. No frame runs during the wait: the script holds EmuHawk's loop in `socket.select`. The video was recorded with the previous build's script, where every watched step failed, so the user's next video decides whether anything else flickers; the controller going off was the old script's overrides, which a watched step no longer sets (T-887).
- **Scope:** `emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`: `running_on`, `PAUSE_AFTER_S`, `run_frames`, `wait_for_request`, `service`, `main`. **Out of scope:** mGBA (no pause); the script's answers are unchanged, so no version bump.
- **Acceptance criteria:** (1) Six steps sent one after another within the wait run their frames with one unpause and no pause, the pad changes reaching every frame, and the second onward report `took.latch == 0`. (2) Silence after a step pauses the emulator and no frame runs while paused. (3) A step from paused takes a latch turn and an unpause as before. (4) A turn that served nothing and follows no step does not wait. (5) Suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py::test_steps_that_follow_each_other_keep_the_emulator_running` (new), `::test_hold_mode_step_unpauses_runs_and_pauses_again`, `::test_step_advances_exactly_and_keeps_the_hold`, `::test_a_step_costs_its_frames_in_turns_and_requests_share_a_turn` (the pause now follows the wait); full suite.
- **Results:** 3/3 quality gates passed. Full suite see PR; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #148. **PR:** #148.
- **Completion report:** Back-to-back steps keep EmuHawk running; the pause comes only when the app goes quiet.

### T-894 – The log says where the app's own time goes
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user, 00:03: "it was better playing it but still with freezes")
- **Objective:** The 23:48 log showed the game at 39 frames a second (Go 55: 6,637 frames in 170 s) while each emulator step cost 70 ms of a 140 ms cycle; the other half was the app's own work on the GUI thread, and no log line said what. The profile tool measures that only on a developer's machine. The app now keeps its own timings the way the link keeps its calls: "ai step" (an AI step, emulator round trip included), "learn" (learning from the person's play, which runs on the GUI thread in the memory handler), "picture" (frame display), and "GUI stall" (a 10 ms timer's gap beyond 50 ms), and logs "App work, last 5 s: …" next to the link's line. The user's next log then says whether the freezes are the emulator, the learning, the display or something else on the GUI thread. Nothing is sped up in this task: measure first.
- **Scope:** `core/worklog.py` (`CallStats` moved here, `WorkLog`, `app_work`), `ui/stall_watch.py`, hooks in `training/service.py` and `ui/game_panel.py`, `ui/main_window.py` starts the watch. **Out of scope:** moving work off the GUI thread (the next task, once the log says which).
- **Acceptance criteria:** (1) `WorkLog` keeps count, average and max per name, summarises most-total-time first, times a block that raises, and starts a new window on `take_window`. (2) A 120 ms sleep on the GUI thread is one stall of about that length; a window with nothing timed writes no line; a window with timings writes one "App work" line and resets. (3) An AI step and a manual learning step each add one timing. (4) The main window runs the watch. (5) Suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/core/worklog.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/stall_watch.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/game_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/main_window.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/tools/profile_play.py`, tests, docs.

### T-895 – A confirmed guess that behaves unlike its kind is questioned
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user, 00:03: "Even when i was playing it and 'doing well' i was getting negative rewards")
- **Objective:** The 23:48 log showed "Lifes goes down" paying 24 to 33 times in every 1,200-step go, and 3 times in the user's own 148-step go: no life count changes that often. The guess was confirmed, and `doubt_bouncing` skipped confirmed guesses, so the AI could never say so. The rule now judges confirmed guesses too, but a person's word is kept: instead of doubting or dropping, the service keeps a question ("You confirmed "Lifes", but it changed N times in M steps; a life count changes rarely. The AI keeps your word; if it is not a life count, reject it in Discoveries.") while the byte behaves that way, and the Learning panel's insights list 'Check "Lifes"' first after a farming warning. The question clears when the byte behaves like its kind again or the guess is rejected or corrected.
- **Scope:** `services/discovery.py`: `Question`, `questions`, `doubt_bouncing` judging confirmed guesses; `training/insights.py::suggest_experiments(questions=())`; `TrainingService.experiments` passes them. **Out of scope:** dropping or weakening a confirmed guess (never without the person); a new reward rule.
- **Acceptance criteria:** (1) A confirmed "lives" on a bouncing byte is neither doubted nor rejected, gains no evidence, and yields one question with the reason and the rejection advice, announced once with the doubted guesses and logged. (2) The same question is not announced again; rejecting the guess clears it with one announcement and a log line. (3) `suggest_experiments` lists 'Check "<name>"' after a farming warning, with the question's text. (4) `TrainingService.experiments()` shows the check for a confirmed busy byte after a review. (5) Suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/insights.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_insights.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, docs.
- **Test plan:** `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py::test_a_lives_guess_that_bounces_is_doubted_then_dropped` (extended), `tests/unit/training/test_insights.py::test_suggestion_rules` (extended), `tests/unit/training/test_service.py::test_behaviour_changes_and_experiments_follow_the_goes` (extended); full suite.
- **Results:** 3/3 quality gates passed. Full suite see PR; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #150. **PR:** #150.
- **Completion report:** The person is told when a guess they confirmed does not behave like its kind, and how to fix it; the AI never overrides them. CI (ubuntu 3.11) failed once in `tests/ui/test_research_dialog.py::test_memory_view_lists_names_and_filters`: the Research window shows a guess the AI rejected as a bare address, but naming that address revived the rejected guess with its old kind ("Progress (confirmed)"); `name_value` now brings a rejected guess back as a named value of unknown kind.

### T-896 – The Discoveries list changes in place
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 00:38 log: "App work" lines put the AI step at 2 ms and learning at 4 to 8 ms, yet the GUI stalled 350 to 900 ms every 15 s, each time next to a discovery review line; and the T-895 question was logged at every review because its counts changed)
- **Objective:** A review's `DiscoveryUpdated` carries the handful of guesses it added or changed, but the Discovery panel threw its whole list away and rebuilt it (135 ms here for 500 guesses, two to three times that on the user's PC), which froze the game at the worst moments. Change only the rows that changed, and keep a question quiet while only its counts move.
- **Scope:** `DiscoveryPanel` keeps a map from variable id to row and the rows' `(region, offset)` keys; `_on_updated` collects the added and changed variables, and the coalesced `_refresh_from_update` calls `update_rows`, which inserts a new row in order, repaints a changed one (line, grey when rejected, the usual colour when taken back), refreshes the evidence when the selected one changed, drops the row of a variable the knowledge no longer holds, and falls back to `refresh` if the knowledge and the list disagree in size. `refresh` (connect, disconnect, import, the person's verdicts) still rebuilds. `DiscoveryService.doubt_bouncing` keeps the fresh question text but logs and announces it only when the variable, its name or the reason is new. **Out of scope:** the events and relationships lists (eight rows each).
- **Acceptance criteria:** a burst of updates adds rows without one `refresh` call; a new guess lands between its neighbours in (region, offset) order while the selection holds; a changed guess keeps its row object, its line and colour follow, and the evidence follows when it is the selected one; a removed guess loses its row; an unannounced change still shows; the benchmark's update after a review is far cheaper than a rebuild; the T-895 question with changed counts neither logs nor announces again; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/ui/test_discovery_panel.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_many_updates_rebuild_the_list_once` (now asserts no rebuild at all) and `test_updates_touch_only_the_rows_that_changed` (panel); `test_a_lives_guess_that_bounces_is_doubted_then_dropped` extended (discovery); UI, services and training suites; full suite.
- **Results:** one new test and two extended ones pass; the panel benchmark (500 guesses) went from 135 ms for a full rebuild to 8 ms for the coalesced update after 500 additions and 2 ms after a review; full suite 1280 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #151. **PR:** #151.
- **Completion report:** The "App work" line proved the app's own thinking was cheap (T-894), which pointed at the widgets: a `QListWidget.clear` and 500 `addItem` calls, with the layout and selection work they cause, was the whole stall. The fallback to a rebuild when sizes disagree covers a removal nobody announced, at the cost of one rebuild then.

### T-898 – Find and sort in the "What the AI is doing" list
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user: "there's loads in there with no search function i see at least 10 saying lives but no lifes" and "a sort function please")
- **Objective:** With hundreds of guesses in memory-address order, a person cannot find the one they named. A find box keeps only the lines containing what they type (name, address or badge, any case), with "N of M" beside it, and a sort choice orders the list by address, by name, or "Most sure first" (what the person confirmed at the top, then by the AI's confidence, what they rejected at the bottom).
- **Scope:** `DiscoveryPanel`: `discovery_find` (`QLineEdit` with a clear button), `discovery_found` (the count, empty when the box is), `discovery_sort` (`QComboBox` over `SORTS`); `find_rows`, `find_text`, `found_text`, `visible_ids`, `sort_by`, `sort_name`. The rows' keys come from the chosen sort; `refresh` sorts by it and applies the filter; `update_rows` (T-896) inserts by the key, moves a changed row whose key changed (keeping it selected), and hides or shows each touched row by the filter. Changing the sort rebuilds once (a person's action). Both boxes follow the panel's enabled state. **Out of scope:** filtering the events and relationships lists; remembering the choice between runs.
- **Acceptance criteria:** typing part of a name, an address or a badge leaves only the matching rows and says "N of M"; a row arriving while the box is in use obeys it and the selection holds; clearing the box shows all; the sorts order as described and keep the selection; a guess whose standing changes moves to its new place without a rebuild, selected or not; the boxes are disabled until a game is connected; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, `tests/ui/test_discovery_panel.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_find_box_narrows_the_list`, `test_sorting_puts_your_own_verdicts_first`; UI suite; full suite.
- **Results:** two new tests pass; UI panel and window suites 37 passed; full suite 1282 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #153. **PR:** #153.
- **Completion report:** The filter hides rows rather than removing them, so T-896's row map stays whole and the find box costs one pass over the rows when its text changes. `find` could not be the method's name: `QWidget.find` exists with another signature.

### T-899 – Panels and the window can be made smaller than their content
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user: dragging the separator between Rewards and Learning "doesn't let me", and the window "is almost full height of my screen" at its smallest)
- **Objective:** Each panel's minimum height was the sum of its widgets' minimums (the Learning panel's pickers, buttons, status, chart, insights and goes table), and the window's minimum the sum of the stacked panels', so neither a separator nor the window could go smaller. A panel must shrink to a few lines with the rest scrolling, and a squeezed panel must not draw its labels over each other (the Rewards panel's tip over its note in the user's screenshot).
- **Scope:** `ui/panels.py`: `Panel` puts its content widget in a `QScrollArea` (`widgetResizable`, no frame, transparent viewport, `MIN_CONTENT_HEIGHT` = 40 px) and exposes it as `scroll_area`; collapsing hides the scroll area. `set_body`, `content` and every panel's own widgets are unchanged. **Out of scope:** remembering sizes (the saved layout already does), the Research dialog.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a panel holding a 600 px body reports a minimum height under 200 px, keeps the body at its full height when resized to 150 px (it scrolls), and still hides its content when collapsed; every dock in the main window has a minimum height under 200 px and the window under 600 px; the existing panel, dock and layout tests pass unchanged; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/panels.py`, `tests/ui/test_main_window.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_panel_can_be_dragged_smaller_than_what_it_holds`, `test_the_window_is_not_pinned_tall_by_its_panels`; UI suite; full suite.
- **Results:** two new tests pass; UI suite 253 passed (3.12 and 3.13); full suite 1284 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #154. **PR:** #154.
- **Completion report:** CI (ubuntu, 3.12) failed once in `test_an_idle_window_writes_no_line`: a main window closed by an earlier test kept its stall watch's 10 ms timer running, and the bigger teardown of this task's scroll areas made that watch log an "App work" line during the idle test. The window now stops its stall watch in `closeEvent`, and `test_a_busy_gui_thread_is_a_stall_and_the_window_is_logged` drains the deferred deletions and the garbage collector before it starts measuring (the previous windows' deletion was being counted as its stall) and reports over a 1 s window so a slow machine cannot clear the stats before they are checked. A scroll area resizes its widget to the viewport whenever the viewport is larger, so expanding widgets (the game picture, the charts) still fill a big panel; only when the panel is smaller than the content's minimum does a scrollbar appear. The overlapping labels came from the same squeeze: Qt had laid the panel out below its minimum and let the labels overlap; inside the scroll area the content always gets its minimum.

### T-901 – Tell the AI what just happened
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user: "i can use cheat engine to watch a value address and chase it as it changes, why is this not doing the same"; the 12:31 log showed "Lifes" at ram[777], a byte that never moves at a death, still paying after a rejection, while the real counter changes too rarely for statistics alone)
- **Objective:** A cheat finder works because the person says *when* it happened. "What the AI is doing" gets a "Tell the AI:" row with three buttons: "I just lost a life", "I just got further", "My score just went up". A press compares the newest memory snapshot with the one five seconds older and keeps the bytes that moved the right way (down by exactly one for a life; up for progress and score), intersected with the previous presses of the same button. When one byte is left after two presses that agree, the AI takes it as the person's word: the byte is confirmed with that kind and name ("Lives", "Progress", "Score", or the name the person already gave it), the evidence says why ("You said you lost a life 2 times and this went down by one each time."), and every other guess of that kind that is not confirmed by the person is rejected by the AI with the reason. An empty intersection starts again from the new press; a settled search starts fresh on the next press. The panel's note line says what each press found in plain words; the log says the same.
- **Scope:** `understanding/moments.py` (`Moment`, `MomentFinder`, `Narrowing`), `DiscoveryService.tell` (with a `moment_window` setting so tests can use a window shorter than the toy racer's crashes are apart), the panel row. **Out of scope:** the AI pressing the buttons for itself around restarts (T-902); value sizes other than one byte (two-byte counters move in their low byte, which the search still finds).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With no link, a press raises the usual "not connected" error; with fewer than two snapshots it says "Play for a few seconds first, then press this right after it happens."
  2. A press keeps the bytes that moved the right way in the window, intersected with earlier presses of that button; nothing left starts again from this press.
  3. One byte left after two agreeing presses settles it: confirmed by "you", kind and name set, a "told" evidence line, the AI's other guesses of that kind rejected with a "told-otherwise" line, one `DiscoveryUpdated` for the lot, and the search forgotten.
  4. A guess the person already named keeps the name; a rejected guess's name is treated as part of the mistake and replaced.
  5. On the toy racer, telling the AI about lost lives finds `ram[7]` (the real counter) and drops a wrong lives guess that was sure.
  6. The panel's three buttons follow the connection state and show the result in the note line.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/moments.py` (new), `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_moments.py` (new), `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `tests/ui/test_discovery_panel.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_moments.py` (7: the window, the moved rule per moment, the intersection, starting again, settling and forgetting, the texts, bad settings); `test_telling_the_ai_about_a_lost_life_finds_the_counter`, `test_telling_about_a_named_guess_keeps_its_name` (service, toy racer); `test_tell_buttons_narrow_the_byte_down` (panel); discovery, panel and understanding suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 10 new tests pass; discovery + panel + moments 47 passed; full suite 1295 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #156. **PR:** #156.
- **Completion report:** The toy racer crashes every 60 frames or so, so with the five-second window two crashes fall in one press and the counter moves by two, failing the "down by one" rule; the service tests therefore use a 30-frame window through the new setting rather than a looser rule, because on a real game "down by exactly one" is what tells a life counter from a timer. The AI's wrong guesses are dropped only when the person's word settles a byte, never on a single press, so a mistimed press costs nothing.

### T-902 – A rejected guess leaves the rewards and steps at once
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's screenshot after T-895: "Lifes" rejected, yet "Lifes goes up" still in Rewards and "Get further in Lifes" still a Progress step; the 12:31 log showed the same)
- **Objective:** The panel's Yes, No and Rename buttons wrote straight to the knowledge and announced nothing, so the reward and goal services, which rebuild their suggestions on `DiscoveryUpdated`, did not hear a verdict until the next review happened to add a guess. And `GoalService.refresh_suggestions` announced only when a step was added, so a step retired for a rejected guess stayed on the Progress panel. `DiscoveryService` now has `confirm`, `reject` and `rename`, each announcing the changed variable; the panel calls them; and the goal service announces when a step was retired or reworded too.
- **Scope:** `DiscoveryService.confirm/reject/rename`, the panel's three actions, `GoalService.refresh_suggestions`. **Out of scope:** the Research window's "Name it" box (already went through `name_value`, which announces).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Rejecting a guess through the service removes its rewards from the profile and its step from the goal at once, each with one announcement; confirming it again brings the rewards back.
  2. Renaming a guess rewords its step at once.
  3. The panel's No button publishes a `DiscoveryUpdated` carrying the rejected variable.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/goals.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, tests, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_rejected_guess_leaves_the_steps_at_once` (goals), `test_a_rejected_guess_loses_its_rewards_at_once` (rewards), the announcement check in `test_confirm_reject_and_rename` (panel); services and UI suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 3 new or extended tests pass; goals + rewards + panels + discovery 81 passed; full suite 1297 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #157. **PR:** #157.
- **Completion report:** This is the generic rule the user asked for: a person's verdict is a change like any the AI makes, heard by everything that builds on the knowledge. It also explains the earlier puzzle of the rejected "Lifes" still paying: the rewards were not wrong about the verdict, they had not been told.

### T-903 – The AI's steps leave the GUI thread
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 12:31 log: "ai step ×150 avg 30 ms" and "GUI stall ×40 avg 84 ms" in every 5 s window, with the AI's thinking on a trained brain taking 30 to 40 ms a step on the thread that draws the window)
- **Objective:** The Learning panel's timer called `TrainingService.step()` on the GUI thread, so every step froze the panels for the emulator's round trip plus the AI's thinking; on the user's brain (270,000 trainings) that was 70% of the time. The timer now hands each step, or each fast slice, to a one-thread `QThreadPool` worker and reports back by signal, the way the play clock and the frame pump already do (T-818); a pulse that finds the previous step still running is skipped, so the rate settles to what the link and the brain can do. The training service takes a re-entrant lock around its public changes (`step`, `run_for`, `start`, `pause`, `resume`, `stop`, `forget`, `set_mode`, `set_speed`, …) so a button pressed on the GUI thread waits for the step in flight instead of changing the go under it.
- **Scope:** `LearningPanel` (`_on_timer`, `_StepTask`, `_on_step_done`, `in_flight`, `skipped`, `worker_steps`; `tick()` stays synchronous for tests and takes nothing while a step is in flight), `TrainingService._lock` and the `_locked` decorator. **Out of scope:** making the AI's step cheaper (a later task if the stalls that remain are the GUI's own drawing); the disconnect handler, which stays unlocked so a disconnect from the GUI thread cannot wait on a worker that is waiting on the connection.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With training started from the panel, the steps run on a thread other than the GUI thread, the status line follows them, and Stop closes the go cleanly.
  2. A fast slice runs on the worker too, many steps per pulse.
  3. A step that raises is logged and shown in the panel's note; training is left to the person.
  4. A control called on the GUI thread while a slice runs on another thread waits for it: the go count matches the goes announced, and no go is half closed.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/learning_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/ui/test_learning_panel.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_timer_steps_on_a_worker_thread`, `test_a_failing_step_is_shown_not_lost` (panel, with the real timer under `qtbot.waitUntil`); `test_the_gui_buttons_wait_for_a_step_on_another_thread` (service); training and panel suites three times; full suite.
- **Results:** 3 new tests pass; training + panel suites 62 passed, three runs; full suite 1300 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #158. **PR:** #158.
- **Completion report:** The lock covers what the GUI calls, not `_on_disconnected`: a disconnect started on the GUI thread holds the connection's lock while it announces, and a worker mid-step may need that lock to record a dropped link, so locking the handler too could deadlock. The handler therefore keeps today's behaviour of closing the go under a step, which the step already survives. The "App work" log line keeps timing "ai step"; after this change it measures the worker's time and the "GUI stall" count should fall to the panels' own drawing.

### T-904 – The AI narrows the life counter down at restarts
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (promised with T-901: "I'll also have the AI do the same narrowing by itself each time the game throws the player back to a start point")
- **Objective:** A restart (several values jumping back to their start together, T-867) usually follows a death, so it is the moment a cheat finder would scan. `RestartWatch` in `understanding/moments.py` takes the restart's snapshot ring, compares the snapshot just before the restart with the one `WINDOW_FRAMES` earlier, and gives a vote to every byte that went down by exactly one; bytes the AI already believes are something else (fairly surely) or that anyone rejected get none. A byte with at least 3 votes, more than any other, from at least two thirds of the restarts seen wins; 8 restarts with no winner clear the count. The discovery service, on a `RESET` event, lets the watch vote and, on a winner, makes that byte its lives guess with "restarts" evidence strong enough to take the seat (weight 9.0), logs it in plain words, and announces the change. Nothing is dropped: the AI's finding adds to what it knows and the reviews keep judging every guess. A lives guess the person confirmed is kept whatever the restarts say.
- **Scope:** `RestartWatch`, `DiscoveryService._narrow_at_restart`, the hook in `_observe_memory`. **Out of scope:** restarts the training's own checkpoint restores cause (the detector is not reset there; a restore puts values home with no death before it, so it earns no votes and the forget rule absorbs it); progress and score, which the person's buttons still cover.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Votes go only to bytes that went down by one in the window before the restart, minus the skipped ones; a tie or too few votes gives no winner; a winner clears the count; 8 restarts without one clear it too; 3 votes of 5 restarts is not a winner, 4 of 6 is.
  2. On the toy racer with the usual guesses taught (speed known as speed), three game-over restarts make `ram[7]` the AI's lives guess at HIGH confidence, the best of its kind, with a planted wrong lives guess left in place (not rejected) and one log line saying so.
  3. A lives guess the person confirmed stays confirmed through further restarts.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/moments.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_moments.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_restarts_vote_for_the_byte_that_went_down_by_one_before_each`, `test_restarts_with_no_clear_winner_are_forgotten` (watch); `test_the_ai_finds_the_life_counter_from_restarts` (service, toy racer); moments and discovery suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 3 new tests pass; moments + discovery 33 passed; full suite 1303 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #159. **PR:** #159.
- **Completion report:** On the toy racer the speed byte also goes from 1 to 0 at every crash, so without the "known to be something else" rule the vote would tie forever; that rule uses what the AI has already learned rather than anything about the game. The first cut also rejected the AI's other lives guesses that never voted, and the first-demonstration test caught why that is wrong: on the toy racer three crashes come inside one five-second window, so the real counter moves by three, gets no vote, and would have been dropped for a byte that happened to move by one. The AI's finding is now evidence only, strong enough to take the seat; the person can still say no, and a guess the person confirmed is never overridden.

### T-905 – A narrowed byte must move steadily
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 13:24 log: "I just lost a life" settled on ram[1826] after three presses, and the next review found it "went up 18 times and down 18"; "I just got further" settled on ram[1991], the byte he had just confirmed as Score)
- **Objective:** Two presses, each comparing only the two ends of a five-second window, let a flickering byte through: it was down by one at both presses by chance, among hundreds of bytes that flicker. And the score goes up whenever one gets further, so "got further" found the score byte. `_moved_steadily` now looks at every snapshot in the window: a lost life is a byte that changed exactly once, down by one; a score or progress byte must be up over the window and never down inside it. `MomentFinder.tell` takes a `skip` set, and the service fills it with the bytes the person confirmed as another kind, so a confirmed Score can never become Progress. The restart watch uses the same rule.
- **Scope:** `understanding/moments.py` (`_moved_steadily`, `_window_items`, `skip`), the skip set in `DiscoveryService.tell`. **Out of scope:** the number of presses needed (two still, now that each press is much stricter).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Over a window of several snapshots, a byte that is down by one but changed three times is not a lost-life candidate; one down by one in one step is; a byte up overall but down inside the window is not a score or progress candidate; one up steadily is.
  2. A byte in `skip` is never a candidate, and the restart watch applies the same steadiness.
  3. On the toy racer, with the score byte confirmed as Score by the person, "I just got further" does not list it, while "My score just went up" does.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/moments.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_moments.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_byte_must_move_steadily_to_count` (finder and watch), `test_a_confirmed_score_is_never_the_progress_byte` (service); moments, discovery and panel suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 2 new tests pass; moments + discovery + panel 52 passed; full suite 1305 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #160. **PR:** #160.
- **Completion report:** The window is now a stack of every snapshot in it (up to 300 × the memory size as 16-bit numbers), which is a few megabytes on a console with 2 KB of RAM and tens on one with 64 KB; a press and a restart are rare enough for that. The stricter rule is what a cheat finder's "decreased by exactly 1" scan does when it is run on every frame rather than once.

### T-906 – The stall line names the GUI work behind a stall
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 13:24 log, "You play" at 13:28–13:31: one to six GUI stalls of 60–150 ms in every 5 s while the picture took 0 ms; no line said what the window's thread was doing)
- **Objective:** The stall watch (T-894) counts the gaps but the only GUI work it times is the picture, so a stall with "picture ×28 avg 0 ms" beside it names nothing. Every event the bridge delivers to the window is now timed under `on <Event>`, a panel's coalesced refresh under its name (`discoveries refresh`, `rewards refresh`), and the game picture's paint under `paint`; the stall line leaves out names whose longest run was under a millisecond, so hundreds of free deliveries do not bury the one that cost 100 ms. The next log will say which handler the stalls are, instead of a guess. A profile of 20 s of play on the fake console already points at the discovery summary, which is computed three times over 3,400 values for every review and again by two handlers; that is the next task once the log confirms it.
- **Scope:** `core/worklog.py` (`quiet_under_s`), `ui/stall_watch.py`, `ui/bridge.py`, `ui/coalesce.py` (`name`), the two named coalescers, `ui/frame_view.py`. **Out of scope:** making any handler faster (T-907 when the log names it).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `WorkLog.summary(quiet_under_s=...)` and `take_window` leave out names whose longest run is under the threshold, and say "nothing" when every name is.
  2. A bridge delivers an event under `on <EventName>` in its work log, whether the handlers are on the same thread or another; a delivery with no handler is timed too (it costs nothing and is left out of the line).
  3. A named coalescer times each run under its name; an unnamed one times nothing.
  4. The stall watch's line uses the threshold: a 2 ms handler is named, a 0.1 ms one is not.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/core/worklog.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/stall_watch.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/bridge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/coalesce.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/rewards_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/frame_view.py`, `tests/unit/core/test_worklog.py`, `tests/ui/test_bridge.py`, `tests/ui/test_coalesce.py`, `tests/ui/test_stall_watch.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_quiet_work_is_left_out_of_the_line` (worklog), `test_deliveries_are_timed_by_event_name` (bridge), `test_a_named_coalescer_times_its_runs` (coalesce), `test_the_line_leaves_out_work_under_a_millisecond` (stall watch); worklog, bridge, coalesce, stall watch, frame view and panel suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new tests pass (a paint-timing test joined the four planned); worklog, bridge, coalesce, stall watch, frame view and panel suites 60 passed; full suite 1310 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #161. **PR:** #161.
- **Completion report:** Timing a delivery costs two clock reads, which the fake-console profile puts under 20 µs per event. The first full run showed the stall-watch test reading a line written by another window's watch, now that every window's bridge feeds the shared log; the test picks the line that carries its own timing. On the fake console the line during play reads `on DiscoveryProgress ×6 avg 3 ms max 5 ms; paint ×150 avg 1 ms max 3 ms; picture ×150 avg 0 ms max 1 ms`.

### T-907 – The discovery summary is computed once per change
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (T-906's profile of play on the fake console: `DiscoveryService.summary()` at 12 ms a call over 3,400 values, called by the review on the worker and again by two GUI handlers for every review, six times in every 5 s)
- **Objective:** The summary line ("Watching 3,400 values, 12 fairly sure, 40 could use your help.") walks the variables three times (`__iter__`, `confident`, `needs_help`), each walk re-summing every variable's evidence, and is asked for by `review()` and `experiment()` on the worker, by `_on_progress` for each of the two `DiscoveryProgress` events a review publishes, and by the panel's coalesced refresh and `refresh`. The user's 13:24 log shows one to six stalls of 60–150 ms in every 5 s of play, the cadence of those progress events on a slower PC with a bigger memory. The summary is now built in one pass and remembered with the knowledge's version and wake button; a handler that asks again gets the string back.
- **Scope:** `DiscoveryService.summary` and its cache. **Out of scope:** `_set_stage`'s label work, the panel's other refresh costs (the T-906 line will name them).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Two calls with no change in between walk the variables once; a proposal, a verdict, or a change of the wake button gives a fresh line; disconnect gives "Not watching a game." and reconnect a fresh line.
  2. The line's words are unchanged (the existing summary tests pass as they are).
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- **Results:** 1 new test passes; discovery + panel suites 43 passed; full suite 1311 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #162. **PR:** #162.

### T-908 – The brain's slots go to the useful values
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 15:41 log: 30 goes of the AI dying to the same first enemy, "Losing Lives" 4–7 times a go, with 8 "Position" guesses at ram[516]–[532] found during the goes; the user asked how the AI could see what it is playing)
- **Objective:** The AI's eyes are the believed variables: `StateEncoder` takes up to 16 of every kind, lowest address first, and `FeatureMap` hands its 32 slots out in the order it first meets a variable, for good. On the user's game the low addresses are full of flag guesses, so the slots filled with switches before the position values (and the enemy's, if it is among them) ever got one: the network could not see them however many goes it played. The encoder now picks its variables kind by kind in a rank order (position, lives, health, speed, score, progress, then flags, then the rest), the best of each kind first as `believed` ranks them (confirmed, confidence, evidence, address), round-robin until `most` (the feature map's width) are chosen, and still orders the chosen ones by address so a tabular key stays stable. A full feature map now gives a newcomer the slot of a variable that is absent from the state (the one unseen longest) instead of dropping the newcomer; only when every slot's holder is present is the newcomer dropped. A saved brain keeps its map; a slot that changes hands reads as a new input the network relearns.
- **Scope:** `learning/state.py` (`KIND_RANK`, `most`), `learning/features.py` (eviction), `understanding/knowledge.py` (`belief_rank` shared with `believed`). **Out of scope:** seeing the screen (a new phase, pending the user's choice), the feature map's width.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With 30 sure flags at low addresses and a position, a lives and a score guess at high ones, an encoder capped at 8 keeps the position, lives and score guesses, and the best flags fill the rest; the parts stay in address order.
  2. A full feature map gives a newcomer the slot of an absent variable; a newcomer with every slot's holder present is dropped; the map still round-trips through JSON.
  3. The toy-racer and the T-850 state tests pass unchanged in meaning.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/state.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/features.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_state.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_features.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_state_takes_the_useful_kinds_first` (state), `test_a_full_map_gives_an_absent_slot_to_a_newcomer` and the updated overflow test (features), `test_belief_rank_orders_as_believed_does` (knowledge); learning, understanding and training suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 3 new tests pass (one existing feature-map test updated to the new rule); learning + understanding + training suites 262 passed; full suite 1314 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #163. **PR:** #163.
- **Completion report:** The feature map's `ids` were in insertion order, which stopped matching the slots once one changed hands, so `ids` and `to_dict` are now in slot order; and the hand-over tie-break is the lowest slot, so a map loaded from disk (nothing seen yet) hands slots over as the live one did, which the round-trip test checks vector by vector. The user's saved brain keeps its 32-wide map; its flag-filled slots will be handed to the positions as soon as a flag is absent from a state, and the network relearns those inputs over the next goes.

### T-909 – "What the AI is doing" refreshes only what changed
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 15:41 log, with T-906's line: `discoveries refresh ×14 avg 52 ms max 139 ms` in one 5 s window, next to one to five GUI stalls of 70–350 ms in nearly every window of play)
- **Objective:** T-896 stopped the variables list being rebuilt, but the coalesced refresh still cleared and refilled the "What just happened" and "What leads to what" lists on every update, and the service announces an update on every snapshot with an event. On the user's PC each refill cost 20–140 ms, up to 14 times in 5 s. The refresh now refills the events list only when the event log's `total` moved since the last fill, and the relationships list only when an update carried relationships and a `RelationshipWatch` (T-842's, shared with the goals and rewards services) says the confident set differs; a full `refresh` fills both and resets the watch.
- **Scope:** `ui/discovery_panel.py`. **Out of scope:** the other handlers the line names (`on EpisodeFinished` 940 ms and `on TrainingStateChanged` 294 ms at a go's end: the Learning panel's goes table and chart; next).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. An update with neither events nor relationships refills neither list; a new event refills the events list only; relationships whose confident set is unchanged refill nothing; a new confident relationship refills the relationships list.
  2. A full refresh fills both lists.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, `tests/ui/test_discovery_panel.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_lists_under_the_variables_refill_only_when_they_moved`; discovery panel suite; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new test passes; discovery panel suite 18 passed; full suite 1312 passed, 10 skipped (3.13, before the rebase onto T-908; the change touches only the panel).
- **Commit:** see PR #164. **PR:** #164.
- **Completion report:** The events list still rebuilds its eight rows when the log grew, which is cheap; what cost the time was sorting every confident relationship and describing sixteen of them on every update, and clearing two list widgets while they were being painted.

### T-910 – The go-end freeze: behaviour changes read the last snapshot
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 15:41 log: `on EpisodeFinished ×1 avg 940 ms` and `on TrainingStateChanged ×1 avg 294 ms` at a go's end, the window's longest stalls)
- **Objective:** At every go's end the Learning panel shows its insights, and `TrainingService.behaviour_changes` compared a *fresh* `policy_of(agent)` with a snapshot: for a DQN that is a feature-vector pass over every remembered situation (10,000 at capacity; 160 ms here, close to a second on the user's PC), on the GUI thread, and again when `TrainingStateChanged` refreshed the panel. The service already takes a policy snapshot on the worker as each go ends; "now" is that snapshot, so the GUI does a dictionary comparison and nothing more.
- **Scope:** `TrainingService.behaviour_changes`. **Out of scope:** the worker's own snapshot cost at a go's end (one pass per go, off the GUI thread), a faster `FeatureMap.batch`.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Two goes take two policy snapshots; `behaviour_changes` (any number of calls) takes none more and still reports the goes compared and the situations known.
  2. The existing behaviour-changes and experiments test passes unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_behaviour_changes_do_not_ask_the_brain_again`; training, insights and Learning panel suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new test passes; training + insights + Learning panel suites 65 passed; full suite 1315 passed, 10 skipped (3.13).
- **Commit:** see PR #165. **PR:** #165.
- **Completion report:** `FeatureMap.batch` over 10,000 states of 32 parts costs 160 ms here in pure Python per part; it still runs once per go on the worker for the snapshot, which is 0.5% of a 36 s go. Vectorising it is worth a task only if the go-end line on the worker ever shows.

### T-911 – Things that move both ways in memory become positions
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 15:41 log: dying to the same first enemy every go; the user chose "memory eyes only for now")
- **Objective:** The classifier knew counters, scores, climbing positions, flags, lives, progress and speed. A byte with many values moving both ways and changing often, which is what an enemy's x is, fell through to UNKNOWN, and the brain leaves UNKNOWN out, so the enemy was invisible to it. Propose such a byte as a POSITION with `MOVER` evidence; name it "Something moving" rather than "Your position" until the input link shows a button moves it; keep it out of the long look's drivers and steppers.
- **Scope:** `ByteStats.max_hop`; `_is_mover` and the new rule after SPEED in `_classify_single`; `INPUT_LINKED`, `MOVER`, `MOVING_WORD`, `is_mover` and `plain_names` in `knowledge.py`; `linking.py` uses the constant; `LongLook._believed` skips movers. **Out of scope:** finer position buckets in the state (next task), telling the player's position from the enemies' before an input experiment, 16-bit movers.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A byte drifting both ways by up to 3 a frame (an enemy's x) is proposed as POSITION with `MOVER` evidence; one that hops by 40 stays UNKNOWN; one that moves every 20 frames is not a POSITION; a mover drifting over the 0/255 edge is still a POSITION.
  2. The lives, flag, progress, speed and junk bytes of the kinds test keep their kinds; the toy racer's noise is still not proposed.
  3. `max_hop` reads a roll-over the short way round and is in `to_dict`; `max_step` keeps its meaning.
  4. `plain_names` calls a mover "Something moving" and a climbing or input-linked position "Your position"; `is_mover` turns false once `INPUT_LINKED` evidence is added.
  5. A mover is neither a driver nor a stepper in the long look; a plain position still drives.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/{variables,knowledge,linking,longlook}.py`, tests in `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_{variables,knowledge,longlook}.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_tracker_records_the_largest_hop_either_way`; `test_something_moving_both_ways_in_small_steps_is_a_position`, `test_a_mover_that_jumps_or_hardly_moves_is_not_a_position`, `test_a_mover_rolling_past_zero_is_still_a_position`, `test_movers_do_not_steal_the_other_kinds`; `test_a_mover_is_called_something_moving_until_the_stick_moves_it`; `test_a_mover_is_neither_a_driver_nor_a_stepper`; observation, understanding, learning, services, rewards and integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 7 new tests pass; observation + understanding + learning + services + rewards + integration 446 passed; full suite 1323 passed, 10 skipped (3:46).
- **Commit:** see PR #166. **PR:** #166.
- **Completion report:** The rule is generic: nothing names a game or an address. Its limit is `SMALL_HOP`: a thing that teleports (a respawn, a screen wrap read as a big hop) stays UNKNOWN until it has been seen moving, and a mover's downs and ups are not told apart, so a lift that only ever rises and drops stays what the older rules make of it. The brain still sees positions in logarithmic buckets, which is too coarse to dodge with; linear bins for positions are the next task.

### T-912 – The state holds each moving thing's distance from the player
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (follows T-911; the user chose "memory eyes only for now")
- **Objective:** A mover in the state was still read in doubling bins, where 64 and 127 are one bin, so an enemy a jump away and one touching the player were the same situation. Give the brain what matters: each mover's distance from each of the player's own positions, exact when close and rough when far.
- **Scope:** `StateEncoder.refresh` pairs movers with own positions (same encoding and region, best-believed first, `MOST_DISTANCES` = 8), keeps 8 seats off `most` whenever a mover is believed, and `encode` appends a part per pair under `distance_id`; `split_distance`, `_part_name` for `State.describe`. **Out of scope:** finer bins for lone positions (tried: 16- and 32-wide bins slowed the practice game's beginner table and failed the first-demonstration test across six seeds; see the architecture note), telling x from y, distances between two movers.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With my position at 100 and a mover at 105, 80 and 230 the state holds a distance part of 5, -10 and 12; touching, a jump away and across the screen are three different buckets; parts are values by address then distances; the key round-trips.
  2. `describe` names a distance "<mover> from <mine>" with the knowledge and by ids without it; `distance_id` refuses ids holding the separator; `split_distance` returns None for plain ids.
  3. No pairs without a mover, without an own position, across encodings or regions; a snapshot too short for the mover gives no distance part.
  4. With a mover believed, `most - 8` variables are seated, positions first; at most 8 pairs, ordered by the own position's rank then the mover's; a state never holds more than `most` parts; `most=1` still seats one.
  5. The first-demonstration test and every existing state, feature, training and panel test pass unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/state.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_state.py`, `tests/ui/test_stall_watch.py` (the idle test now ignores another window's line, after a CI run showed one), `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_distance_from_each_moving_thing_is_part_of_the_state`, `test_distance_parts_are_named_in_words`, `test_no_distances_without_both_a_mover_and_a_position_of_my_own`, `test_seats_are_kept_for_distances_when_anything_moves`; learning, training, services, integration and Learning panel suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 4 new tests pass; learning + training + services + integration + Learning panel 264 passed; full suite 1327 passed, 10 skipped (3:53).
- **Commit:** see PR #167. **PR:** #167.
- **Completion report:** The even-bin attempt is recorded in `ARCHITECTURE.md` so it is not tried again: a lone position's finer bins cost the beginner table learning time and tell it nothing it can act on. Distances pair every mover with every own position, so a game whose player has an x and a y and whose enemies have both gets x-to-y pairs too; the brain can ignore them, but they take seats, and telling x from y (two values that move with the same button family) is a possible later task. The next thing to learn from the user's log is whether "Something moving" values appear at all on the real game and whether the first enemy stops costing a life.

### T-914 – A thing moving every step in small hops is not noise
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 18:01 log: "It sees … 10 positions" with no moving thing after 54 goes)
- **Objective:** The noise rule (change rate ≥ 0.9, not mostly up) ran before the mover rule and threw away a walking enemy's position, which changes every step. Let smooth movement through, whichever way it goes, without letting random bytes or timers in.
- **Scope:** `ByteStats.small_hops`, `unit_hops`, `smooth`; `SMALL_HOP` moves to `observation/diff.py`; `_is_noise`, `_is_mover`, `_is_counter` and a downward COUNTER branch in `understanding/variables.py`. **Out of scope:** which own position distances are measured from (T-915); 16-bit movers.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A byte walking left 2 or 3 every step and respawning at the right is a POSITION with MOVER evidence.
  2. A random byte every frame is still not proposed; a byte going down by one every frame is a COUNTER; a byte going down by one every six steps is not a POSITION.
  3. `small_hops` and `unit_hops` count as named (a roll-over read as one step) and are in `to_dict`.
  4. Every T-911 mover test, the kinds test and the toy racer tests pass unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_tracker_counts_small_and_unit_hops`; `test_a_thing_moving_every_step_in_small_hops_is_not_noise`, `test_random_every_step_is_still_noise_and_a_countdown_is_a_counter`, `test_a_slow_steady_countdown_is_not_a_mover_either`; observation, understanding, learning, services and integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 4 new tests pass; observation + understanding + learning + services + integration 405 passed; full suite 1333 passed, 10 skipped in 259.55s (0:04:19).
- **Commit:** see PR #169. **PR:** #169.
- **Completion report:** The log also showed lives lost per go rising from 0.8 to 2.3 as exploring fell from 81% to 5%: with no enemy in view the network settled on grabbing points and taking hits. A thing that moves exactly one every step in one direction is still not a mover (it reads as a timer); that is the price of keeping countdowns out, and the climbing-position rule has paid it since T-302.

### T-916 – The AI rethinks a guess it made itself
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 20:49 log: 77 goes on the T-914/T-915 build, still "10 positions" and no moving thing)
- **Objective:** `propose_candidates` judges a byte once, at the first review after it changes, when it has often changed a handful of times, and never again: an enemy slot became "a speed" or "progress" on five values and stayed so for 77 goes, so T-914's mover rule never ran on it. Re-judge the AI's own guesses at every review when the classifier now says another kind.
- **Scope:** `rethink_candidates`, `CLASSIFIER_KINDS`, `HELD_KINDS` in `understanding/variables.py`; `AI`, `rejected_by_ai` in `understanding/knowledge.py`; `DiscoveryService.review` calls it and logs the change. **Out of scope:** a change of width (8-bit to 16-bit); refreshing evidence when the kind is unchanged; a guess rejected by a person.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A guess the AI proposed takes the classifier's new kind and evidence; evidence other findings attached (the input link, "played", the long look's) is kept and zero-weight doubts about the old kind are dropped.
  2. A confirmed, named or person-rejected guess, one with "told" or "restarts" evidence, and an input-linked position are left alone.
  3. A guess the AI dropped stays dropped while the classifier says the kind it dropped, and comes back un-rejected when it says another.
  4. A candidate of the same kind changes nothing (idempotent); a walking enemy first judged on its first few changes is a mover after the full stream.
  5. The discovery review announces rethought guesses as changed and logs one line naming them.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_ai_changes_its_mind_about_its_own_guess`, `test_a_persons_word_and_the_links_verdict_are_never_rethought`, `test_a_guess_the_ai_dropped_comes_back_as_another_kind`, `test_a_walking_enemy_judged_early_becomes_a_mover_later`, `test_a_review_rethinks_the_ais_own_guesses`; understanding + services + learning + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new tests pass; understanding + services + learning + observation + integration 411 passed; full suite 1339 passed, 10 skipped in 292.88s (0:04:52).
- **Commit:** see PR #171. **PR:** #171.
- **Completion report:** The toy racer's second review already rethinks three of its own guesses (the speed, a progress byte, an unknown), which is the rule working, not a fault: a byte's first label is a guess on a handful of changes. A doubt about the old kind goes with the kind, so a rethought lives guess is not dropped a step later for the sins of its past label. Not done here: refreshing stale evidence words when the kind is unchanged, and a byte that should widen to 16 bits.

### T-917 – A guess is rethought once the byte has changed twice as often
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 22:12 log: 374 reviews changed 1,384 guesses, 654 to progress and 648 back to unknown)
- **Objective:** T-916 rethinks at every review, so a byte on the edge of a rule (sixteen values, a change in ten steps) flips kind every 120 steps and the brain's seats, rewards and steps churn with it. Rethink a guess only when the byte has changed at least twice as often as when it was last judged.
- **Scope:** `Candidate.changes`; `Variable.judged` (saved and loaded, older files load as 0); `Knowledge.propose(judged=)`; `propose_candidates` and `rethink_candidates` in `understanding/variables.py`. **Out of scope:** the mover rule's own thresholds (T-918).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A guess judged on N changes is left alone by a candidate with fewer than 2N changes, whatever its kind, and rethought by one with 2N or more; the rethought guess records the new count.
  2. A guess proposed records the changes it was judged on; a guess with no record (an older file, a planted guess) is rethought on the first differing verdict.
  3. `find_candidates` fills `Candidate.changes` from the byte (the lower byte of a pair).
  4. A variable's `judged` survives `to_dict`/`from_dict`, and a dict without it loads as 0.
  5. The walking-enemy test still turns the early "speed" into a mover (40 changes → 399).
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_guess_is_rethought_once_the_byte_has_changed_twice_as_often`, `test_candidates_and_proposals_carry_the_changes_judged_on`, `test_judged_is_saved_and_loaded` plus the T-916 tests; understanding + services + learning + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 3 new tests pass; understanding + services + learning + integration 296 passed; full suite 1341 passed, 10 skipped in 209.65s (0:03:29).
- **Commit:** see PR #172. **PR:** #172.
- **Completion report:** The count lives on the variable rather than in an evidence line because the panels print every evidence line and a "judged on 37 changes" row would read as a reason to believe. A byte judged on two changes is rethought at four, eight, sixteen … so early mistakes are still fixed quickly; the churn the log showed came from bytes judged on hundreds of changes being rethought on one more.

### T-918 – A thing on screen only now and then is still a mover
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 22:12 log: 1,384 rethinks, six to a position, none to "Something moving")
- **Objective:** The mover rule asked for a change in more than a tenth of all steps and small hops in 95 of 100 changes. An enemy slot on the user's game is filled for a few seconds of each go: it spawns with one big hop, walks, and is cleared with another, so a short visit is under 95% small hops and the byte changes in a twentieth of the steps. A simulated slot alive 12 or 30 steps in every 200 was UNKNOWN. Let rare, briefly visible things count.
- **Scope:** `SMOOTH_SHARE` and `ByteStats.smooth` in `observation/diff.py`; `_is_mover` in `understanding/variables.py`. **Out of scope:** a briefly visible thing with sixteen values or fewer (the lives and progress rules keep it); the rules' order.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A slot alive 12 steps in every 200 (spawn hop, 2–3 a step, cleared) is a POSITION with `MOVER` evidence; alive 30 and 60 steps likewise.
  2. The same slot moving through ten values only is not a position; a rare stepper with few values stays what it was.
  3. A random byte every step is still noise; a countdown is still a counter; a slow steady countdown is still not a mover; every T-914 kind test is unchanged.
  4. `smooth` is true at three small hops in four and false below.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_thing_on_screen_only_now_and_then_is_a_mover`, `test_a_brief_visitor_with_few_values_is_not_a_position`, `test_smooth_takes_three_small_hops_in_four`; observation + understanding + learning + services + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 3 new tests pass; observation + understanding + learning + services + integration 416 passed; full suite 1344 passed, 10 skipped in 198.47s (0:03:18).
- **Commit:** see PR #173. **PR:** #173.
- **Completion report:** Three in four is the lowest share that still leaves random bytes far below (about one small hop in fourteen); a slot visited for fewer than six steps at a time is still rough and stays unknown, and a brief visitor with sixteen values or fewer stays with the lives and progress rules, which doubt and drop it in their own time. The simulated slot is the evidence here, not the user's memory: the next log's "It sees … (N moving)" is the real test.

### T-919 – The log takes a census of the busiest bytes
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 00:21 log: T-917 and T-918 in, 84 rethinks, still no moving thing)
- **Objective:** Three builds in a row changed the mover rule on simulated bytes and the user's game still shows no "Something moving"; the log names kinds but never the statistics they rest on, so each fix was a guess. Print, every 25th review, the sixteen bytes with the most changes and the numbers the classifier reads.
- **Scope:** `DiscoveryService.census()`, `census_every` and the census log line in `services/discovery.py`. **Out of scope:** any change to the rules; a census in the UI.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `census()` lists at most sixteen bytes, most changes first, each with changes, steps, values, small hops, unit hops, largest hop, ups, downs, wraps and the AI's current word for it ("nobody's guess" when none).
  2. The review logs the census on every `census_every`-th review and not otherwise; `census_every` below 1 is refused.
  3. With nothing connected `census()` is empty.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_census_names_the_busiest_bytes_and_their_numbers`, `test_the_review_logs_the_census_on_schedule`; services + understanding + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 2 new tests pass; services + understanding + integration 233 passed; full suite 1346 passed, 10 skipped in 207.41s (0:03:27).
- **Commit:** see PR #174. **PR:** #174.
- **Completion report:** Sixteen lines every three minutes is a few hundred lines an hour, which the stall watch and the link already exceed. The census ranks by changes, so a byte that moves rarely but matters (a brief enemy) can miss the top sixteen; if the next log shows the enemy bytes below the cut, the next step is a second list of the bytes with the most distinct values.

### T-921 – A value climbing by the same amount every step is a counter
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 01:52 log: "Position at ram[9]", 25,368 changes in 26,999 steps, every hop four)
- **Objective:** The climbing-position rule asks for small steps that are not all one; a frame counter read every four frames steps by exactly four every step and took a position seat (one of the six), a candidate for the player's own position in `_pair_up`. A climbing byte whose hops are all the same size, nearly every step, is a counter.
- **Scope:** `ByteStats.min_hop` in `observation/diff.py`; the counter branch under `mostly_up` in `understanding/variables.py`. **Out of scope:** rare steady steppers (a score that only ever adds ten stays a score).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A byte stepping by four every step, rolling over, is a COUNTER with evidence saying "Always by exactly 4."
  2. A byte stepping by 0–4 (the T-302 position test) is still a POSITION; a byte stepping by one is still a COUNTER with the T-302 words.
  3. A byte adding ten now and then (rare, many values) is still a SCORE.
  4. `min_hop` is 0 for an unchanged byte, the smallest hop either way otherwise, and in `to_dict`.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_tracker_records_the_smallest_hop_too`, `test_a_value_climbing_by_the_same_amount_every_step_is_a_counter`; observation + understanding + learning + services + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 2 new tests pass; observation + understanding + learning + services + integration 420 passed; full suite 1348 passed, 10 skipped in 221.07s (0:03:41).
- **Commit:** see PR #176. **PR:** #176.
- **Completion report:** The rule needs the byte to change in nine steps of ten so that a score adding the same amount now and then stays a score; a clock that ticks every other step would slip through, and the census will say so if one does. With the frame counter out, the six position seats and the "own position" candidates of T-915 are one junk byte lighter.

### T-922 – A guess is rethought when its evidence changes, not only its kind
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 11:16 census: forty sprite positions with mover statistics, "Position" all, "It sees … 6 positions" with nothing moving)
- **Objective:** `rethink_candidates` skips a candidate of the same kind. A byte judged on its first few changes while climbing is a POSITION by the climbing rule; once it has moved both ways the classifier says POSITION by the mover rule, the kind matches, and the guess keeps its climbing evidence for good, so `is_mover` never holds and the brain's distance seats never fill. Rethink when the classifier's evidence kinds differ too.
- **Scope:** `rethink_candidates` in `understanding/variables.py`. **Out of scope:** seats for movers (T-923); the frame counter's jumps (T-924).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A POSITION with climbing evidence and a POSITION candidate with `MOVER` evidence (twice the changes) is rethought: same kind, mover evidence, other evidence kept.
  2. A candidate with the same kind and the same evidence kinds (details or weights aside) changes nothing.
  3. The held guesses of T-916 are still held; the doubling rule of T-917 still gates it.
  4. `enemy_rows` judged early as climbing, then on the full stream, is a mover.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_guess_is_rethought_when_its_evidence_changes`; understanding + services + learning + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new test passes (the T-916 same-kind case now carries matching reasons); understanding + services + learning + integration 302 passed; full suite 1349 passed, 10 skipped in 207.89s (0:03:27).
- **Commit:** see PR #177. **PR:** #177.
- **Completion report:** Reasons are compared as sets of evidence kinds, not weights or words, so a fresh "Only 7 different values seen" against "Only 5" is not a rethink and the knowledge's version does not churn on counts.

### T-923 – Moving things get the distance seats without a position seat
- **Status:** Done

- **Objective:** `_pair_up` draws movers from the seated variables, and the seats go kind by kind in `belief_rank` order, where a climbing position (2.5) and a played one outrank a mover (2.0); with sixteen positions believed and five or six seats, no mover is seated, so the eight distance seats reserved for them stay empty.   2. A seated mover is counted once.
  3. The T-912 and T-915 tests hold: pairs come from the best-believed own position first and are capped at `MOST_DISTANCES`.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/state.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_state.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

The frame counter at ram[9] still takes a seat and is one of the own-position candidates; T-924 takes it out.

### T-924 – A counter that steps evenly but jumps now and then
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 11:16 census: `Position at ram[9]`, 62,000 changes, hops 4 to 128, still seated)
- **Objective:** T-921 reads a climbing byte as a counter when its smallest and largest hops are equal. The 11:16 census showed the frame counter's largest hop at 128: the game resets it now and then (a level start, a death), so one jump in thousands of steps kept it a position. A climbing byte that changes nearly every step and whose hop is the same as its previous hop in nearly every change is a counter, however it jumps between.
- **Scope:** `ByteStats.last_hop`, `same_hops` and `usual_hop` in `observation/diff.py`; the same-hop counter branch under `mostly_up` in `understanding/variables.py`. **Out of scope:** rare steady steppers (a score adding ten stays a score); the by-one counter rule.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A byte stepping by four every step that jumps back to zero every 300 steps is a COUNTER with evidence saying "Nearly always by 4."
  2. A byte stepping by four with no jump is still a COUNTER saying "Always by exactly 4."; the T-302 position and the by-one counter are unchanged; a score adding ten now and then is still a SCORE.
  3. `same_hops` counts the changes whose hop equals the previous change's hop, `usual_hop` is the hop last seen twice running, both are 0 for an unchanged byte, and both are in `to_dict`.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_tracker_counts_repeated_hops`, `test_a_counter_that_jumps_now_and_then_is_still_a_counter`; observation + understanding + learning + services + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 2 new tests pass; observation + understanding + learning + services + integration 424 passed; full suite 1352 passed, 10 skipped in 204.34s (0:03:24).
- **Commit:** see PR #179. **PR:** #179.
- **Completion report:** The rule compares each hop with the one before it, so a reset of any size is one miss among thousands. A thing that walks at one speed for a whole session reads as a clock too; in practice an enemy stops, turns or is cleared, which breaks the run. The census will show whether ram[9] has become "Counter" and left the position seats.
