### T-961 – A start spot that loses a life by itself is moved past the loss
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 9 Nov log, Practise at 64×: 604 goes all ended by the step cap, and in 177 of the last 179 the first life was lost at step 32, with exploring at 5% and at 25% alike; earlier goes lost it at a median of 75 to 83 steps. A loss that early, at the same step whatever the AI presses, is the start spot's: the checkpoint was taken while the game was already losing the life, and every go opened with a −10 the AI could do nothing about, 3,000 goes of it. Nothing here is Super Mario's: any game whose checkpoint lands in a dying moment does this)
- **Objective:** `_note_doomed_start` collects, over goes that restarted from the checkpoint, the step the first life or health was lost at; `DOOMED_GOES` (3) goes in a row with that step within `DOOMED_STEP_SLACK` (2) of one another and at most `DOOMED_START_STEPS` (75) raise a suspicion. At the next go's start, before the go begins, `_move_start_past_loss` restores the checkpoint and steps the game that many steps holding nothing, reading the AI's best life and health guesses each step. No drop: the loss is the AI's doing, and the goes are left alone (one log line). A drop: the game is stepped on, holding nothing, until the AI's buttons change something again (`probe_inputs`, in chunks of `DOOMED_LISTEN_STEPS` (8) steps, at most `DOOMED_LISTEN_MOST` (20) chunks), and that spot becomes the checkpoint the goes restart from; the log says so. A pinned spot is not changed: changing mode starts from the pinned spot again, and the check runs again. A start cleared of a loss at a step is not probed again for that step (within the slack) until the checkpoint moves: the demo's AI drove into the next obstacle at step 4 go after go, and probing every third go would have cost a restore and eight steps each time.
- **Scope:** `training/service.py`, `training/episode.py` (a `restored` property), `tests/integration/test_first_demonstration.py` (the child brakes before handing the car over: the demo's checkpoint was itself a car crashing at step 1, which the new check moved the goes past, and the 30-go demo then came out differently; explored 100 frames longer so its comparisons hold from the braked start). **Out of scope:** the reward for the loss itself, the learner's credit for a delayed loss, the pinned spot.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Three restored goes whose first loss is at the same early step, when the game loses the life by itself from the checkpoint holding nothing, move the checkpoint to just after the loss: the next go starts with one life fewer, the saved training slot differs, and the log names the step.
  2. Three such goes whose loss does not happen holding nothing leave the checkpoint where it was, with a log line saying the loss is the AI's doing.
  3. Goes whose first-loss steps differ by more than the slack, goes with no loss, goes whose loss is later than `DOOMED_START_STEPS`, and goes that did not restart from the checkpoint (an emulator without save states) never raise the suspicion.
  4. Without save states (the harness unavailable) nothing changes.
  5. The training suite passes unchanged otherwise.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `tests/integration/test_first_demonstration.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_start_that_loses_a_life_by_itself_moves_the_goes_past_the_loss`, `test_a_loss_the_ai_causes_leaves_the_start_where_it_is`, `test_losses_at_different_steps_or_late_raise_no_suspicion`, `test_goes_without_a_checkpoint_do_not_count_as_a_doomed_start`; training suite; full suite.
- **Results:** four tests; training suite 99 passed; full suite 1447 passed, 10 skipped (201.53s); ruff and mypy clean. The demo test needed its start changed (see Scope): with the checkpoint a car crashing at step 1, the first run moved the goes past it ("loses a life by itself, 1 steps in") and the 30-go comparison came out differently.
- **Commit:** see PR #216. **PR:** #216.
- **Completion report:** `_note_doomed_start` raises a suspicion from three restored goes losing their first life at the same early step; `_move_start_past_loss` checks it holding nothing and moves the checkpoint past a loss the game has by itself, to where the game listens again; a cleared step is not probed again until the checkpoint moves. The user's next log says whether the goes still open at step 32 with a loss.
### T-960 – The link log says where a step's time goes beyond its frames
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 9 Nov log: `step ×263 avg 10 ms`, `step:frames avg 3 ms`, `step:latch avg 0 ms`, every five seconds for 95 minutes; after T-958 and T-959 the emulator's 10 ms are two thirds of the AI's step, and 7 of them have no line: the script's 2 KB memory read through EmuHawk's Lua bridge and its base64, the JSON encoding, the socket, the app's decoding)
- **Objective:** `handlers.step` times `serve_reads` into `took.reads` (only when reads were asked) and `serve_line` times the whole serve, from decoding the request to just before the answer is sent, into `took.serve` for every answer that carries `took`. `BizHawkAdapter._note_took` keeps `step:reads` and `step:serve` beside `step:latch` and `step:frames`, so the link line reads `step ×N avg A ms; step:serve avg B ms; step:reads avg C ms; step:frames avg D ms`: `A − B` is the wire and the app's own encoding and decoding, `B − C − D` the rest of the script. `link_totals` exposes the link's lifetime timings by name.
- **Scope:** `emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`. **Out of scope:** making any part faster (that waits for the measurement), the protocol version.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A step's answer carries `took.serve` always and `took.reads` when it read; `frames ≤ serve`.
  2. After a step without reads and one with, the adapter's link summary names `step:serve ×2` and `step:reads ×1`, and the totals order `step ≥ step:serve ≥ step:frames`.
  3. Every other answer is unchanged; the BizHawk suites pass unchanged otherwise.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_link_log_keeps_the_scripts_step_timing` (extended), `test_a_step_costs_its_frames_in_turns_and_requests_share_a_turn` (the answer's keys); the BizHawk suites; full suite.
- **Results:** two tests extended; emulator suites 346 passed; full suite 1443 passed, 10 skipped (148.80s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #215. **PR:** #215.
- **Completion report:** the script's step answer carries `took.reads` and `took.serve`, the adapter logs them as `step:reads` and `step:serve`, and `link_totals` gives the lifetime timings by name; nothing is faster yet, the next user log says where to look.
### T-959 – The feature map keeps a finished row per situation
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 9 Nov log: `step: learn` 3.7 ms ×251,000, a fifth of the 18 ms step; a profile of `DQNLearner.learn` at the user's scale here (24-value situations, a full 10,000-transition memory) spent 2.2 of its 3.0 ms in `FeatureMap._fill`, 65 rows of 24 parts each worked out in Python with two `min`/`max` a part, for situations the replay memory hands back over and over)
- **Objective:** `FeatureMap` keeps the finished row of every situation it has encoded, keyed by the situation's parts, and reads it back while the slot layout is the one the row was built under; a slot changing hands or a newcomer taking a free slot clears the cache. The record of when each variable was last seen moves on at a hit as before, so slots change hands exactly as they did. `built` counts the rows worked out part by part.
- **Scope:** `learning/features.py`. **Out of scope:** the network's arithmetic, the replay memory's sampling, the batch size.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A situation's row is built once and read back until a slot changes hands; after that it is built again under the new layout.
  2. On random sequences of situations over more variables than the map has slots, a cached map's vectors, slot order, evictions and drops equal an uncached map's (a property test).
  3. `DQNLearner.learn` at the user's scale: from 3.0 ms to about 1 ms here.
  4. The learning suite passes unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/features.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_features.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_situations_row_is_built_once_and_read_back_until_a_slot_changes_hands`, `test_the_cache_changes_no_vector_slot_or_count` (property test, 100 examples); learning suite; full suite; the learn profile before and after.
- **Results:** two tests, the property test 100 examples; learning suite 78 passed; full suite 1443 passed, 10 skipped (151.45s); ruff and mypy clean; `DQNLearner.learn` at the user's scale 2.99 ms → 1.12 ms; the policy snapshot `_record` takes at every go's end over the 10,000 remembered situations, which encodes them all, 103 ms → 37 ms.
- **Commit:** see PR #214. **PR:** #214.
- **Completion report:** `FeatureMap._fill` reads a cached row by `state.parts` and only refreshes the last-seen record; `_place` clears the cache; `ROW_CACHE_MOST` bounds it at 20,000 rows; `learn` at the user's scale 2.99 ms → 1.12 ms here.
### T-958 – The diff builds its byte changes only when they are read
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 9 Nov log: 18 ms a step, of which the emulator 10 ms, `observe: track` 1.2 ms ×255,000; a profile of the whole step at the user's scale here spent 0.7 of the tracker's 1.05 ms in `diff_snapshots` building a `ByteChange` per changed byte, 1,000 a step, which nothing on the step reads: `MemoryObserved.diff` has no reader in the app, the detector and the finder work from the snapshot and the believed guesses)
- **Objective:** `SnapshotDiff` keeps, per region, the two byte strings and the array of differing offsets, and builds the `ByteChange` tuple the first time `changes` is read, keeping it afterwards. `changed_count`, `is_empty` and `offsets` answer from the arrays. The class stays immutable and compares equal to a diff built with the same frames and changes.
- **Scope:** `observation/diff.py`. **Out of scope:** the counting arrays (`_RegionStats.count`), the detector, the long look.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Diffing two snapshots builds no `ByteChange` until `changes` is read; `changed_count`, `is_empty` and `offsets` do not build them; a second read gets the same tuple.
  2. On random pairs of two-region snapshots, the count, the emptiness and the offsets read before the changes are built equal those of a diff built from the changes, and the two compare and hash equal (a property test).
  3. `SnapshotDiff(0, 1, ())` and the existing diff tests behave as before; assigning any attribute raises.
  4. The profiled step at the user's scale: `observe: track` from 1.05 ms to a few tenths.
  5. The observation, services and understanding suites pass unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_diff_builds_its_byte_changes_only_when_they_are_read`, `test_counts_and_offsets_agree_with_the_changes_before_they_are_built` (property test, 60 examples), `test_snapshot_diff_is_immutable` (extended); observation, services and understanding suites; full suite; the step profile before and after.
- **Results:** three tests, the property test 60 examples; observation, services and understanding suites 445 passed; full suite 1441 passed, 10 skipped (153.14s); ruff and mypy clean; profiled step at the user's scale: `observe: track` 1.05 ms → 0.21 ms a step, the step 9.42 ms → 7.94 ms.
- **Commit:** see PR #213. **PR:** #213.
- **Completion report:** `SnapshotDiff` is a slotted immutable class with lazy `changes`; `diff_snapshots` hands it the differing offsets per region; the tracker's share of a step at the user's scale fell from 1.05 ms to 0.21 ms here.
### T-957 – The review stops walking for ids and byte statistics
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log after T-951: `discovery: review` 290 × 285 ms = 83 s of a 16-minute run, every review holding the AI's step; a profile of a review at the user's scale here spent 34 of its 100 ms in `Knowledge.new_id`, which scans `v1, v2, …` from the start for every proposal (252 proposals over 1,500 ids: a quadratic walk), and 20 ms in `ChangeTracker.all_stats`, which builds the 2,048 `ByteStats` one numpy call at a time)
- **Objective:** `new_id` keeps a floor, the lowest `vN` that might be free (raised by `add` when it takes the floor's id, lowered by `remove` when a lower id is freed), and scans up from it, returning the same lowest free id as before. `_RegionStats.all_stats` turns each statistics array into a Python list once and groups the distinct values of every byte from one `np.nonzero` over `seen`, building the same `ByteStats` objects as `stats(offset)` does one by one.
- **Scope:** `understanding/knowledge.py`, `observation/diff.py`. **Out of scope:** the rest of the review (`rethink_candidates`, `doubt_bouncing`), the review's schedule.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. On random sequences of proposals, adds of foreign ids and removals, `new_id` equals the old scan from `v1` after every operation (a property test against a copy of the old scan).
  2. After random observations, with jumps, `all_stats()` equals `{location: stats(location)}` for every byte of every region (the T-945 property test, extended, and a two-region test).
  3. The profiled review at the user's scale loses the two costs: `new_id` from 34 ms to under 1 ms, `all_stats` from 20 ms to a few.
  4. The understanding, observation and services suites pass unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_new_id_is_the_lowest_free_id_as_the_old_scan_gave` (property test, 100 examples), `test_the_arrays_count_exactly_as_the_byte_loop_did` (extended), `test_all_stats_covers_every_region_in_one_pass`; understanding, observation and services suites; full suite; the review profile before and after.
- **Results:** two property tests 100 examples each; understanding, observation and services suites 402 passed; full suite 1432 passed, 10 skipped (148.83s); ruff and mypy clean; profiled review at the user's scale: `propose_candidates` 43 ms → 9 ms (`new_id` no longer registers), `all_stats` 20 ms → 10 ms.
- **Commit:** see PR #212. **PR:** #212.
- **Completion report:** `new_id` scans from `_id_floor`, raised by `add` and lowered by `remove`; `_RegionStats.all_stats` builds every `ByteStats` in one pass and `ChangeTracker.all_stats` uses it; both proved equal to the old results by property tests.
### T-956 – The save writes its file off the AI's thread
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's log after T-955: the knowledge is 1,492 guesses, 99,731 relationships and 27 MB, and each of the 16 saves took 1.2 s on the AI's thread, 20 s of a 16-minute run; a profile at that scale here splits a 240 ms save into the snapshot (`to_dict`, 86 ms) and the encoding and writing of the file (150 ms), which need nothing from the AI once the snapshot exists)
- **Objective:** the periodic save (`_save_if_due`, T-949) takes the snapshot under `discovery: save` as before and hands the encoding and the file write to a daemon worker thread, timed `discovery: save write`, which logs the "Saved …" line when the file is on disk. An explicit `save()` (the Save button, a stop, a disconnect) waits for a running write, then writes at once as before, so the file always ends with the newest state. A periodic save that falls due while a write is still running is left pending for the next step. The work log takes a lock, since a third thread now times into it. The JSON itself is written in pieces (`json_pieces`: a top-level list longer than 2,000 items is encoded 2,000 items at a time, byte for byte what one `json.dumps` gives), because one `json.dumps` of a 27 MB file holds the interpreter for its whole run and the worker would have stalled the AI's thread for 130 ms anyway.
- **Scope:** `services/discovery.py`, `storage/knowledge.py` (`save_dict`), `storage/jsonfiles.py` (`json_pieces`), `core/worklog.py`. **Out of scope:** the file format and its size; a smaller or faster-to-encode format is a separate decision.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A due periodic save returns before the file is written (a store whose write waits on a gate), the AI goes on stepping meanwhile, and the file holds the snapshot once the gate opens; `saves` counts the snapshot and the "Saved …" line names the time the write took.
  2. An explicit `save()` while a write is running waits for it and then writes the newest state; the writes land in that order.
  3. No second write starts while one is running; the change stays pending and is written at the next due step after the write ends.
  4. `WorkLog.note` from several threads at once loses no count.
  5. The discovery, training and UI suites pass (the T-949 interval test waits for the writer between its clock steps; the zero-interval test checks that a change arriving during a write is written by the next step after it).
  6. `json_pieces` joined equals `json.dumps(data, indent=2, sort_keys=True)` for random documents and piece sizes (a property test), and a write of data that is not JSON leaves no half file.
  7. Measured at the user's scale: the AI's thread is held for the snapshot only, and the worker's write stalls it by a few ms at most.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/storage/knowledge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/storage/jsonfiles.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/core/worklog.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `tests/unit/storage/test_jsonfiles.py`, `tests/unit/core/test_worklog.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_due_save_writes_its_file_in_the_background`, `test_an_explicit_save_waits_for_the_running_write_and_writes_the_newest_state`, `test_no_second_write_starts_while_one_is_running`, `test_notes_from_several_threads_are_all_counted`, `test_the_pieces_join_to_exactly_what_one_dumps_gives` (property test, 150 examples), `test_a_long_list_is_written_in_pieces_and_reads_back`, `test_data_that_is_not_json_leaves_no_half_file`; services, core, storage, training and UI suites; full suite; the save hold and worker-stall measurements at the user's scale.
- **Results:** services, core, storage, training and discovery panel suites 351 passed (three runs) and the new storage tests; full suite 1437 passed, 10 skipped (144.81s); ruff and mypy clean. At the user's scale (1,500 guesses, 100,000 relationships, 21 MB) a synchronous save held the thread 186–223 ms here; the background save holds it 31 ms (the snapshot) and the worker's write, 150 ms, stalls the main thread by 4–5 ms at its worst (one `json.dumps` on the worker stalled it 126–128 ms). On the user's PC, where a save took 1.2 s, the AI should lose well under a tenth of that.
- **Commit:** see PR #211. **PR:** #211.
- **Completion report:** `save_in_background` snapshots and starts a daemon writer; `save` waits for it and writes at once; `_save_if_due` uses the former; `KnowledgeStore.save_dict` and `json_pieces` write the file in pieces; `WorkLog` is locked.

### T-953 – A guess is found by its address in an index, not by a walk
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's stage log: `on EpisodeFinished` 14 × 524 ms on the GUI thread; the Research window refreshes on every finished go even while hidden, and its memory table asks `Knowledge.at` for each of up to 1,800 addresses; `at` walked every variable comparing region and offset, 1.4 million comparisons a refresh at 1,600 guesses, 41 of the refresh's 45 ms in a profile at that scale; the discovery service asks the same question when a person names a value, tells it about a moment, after an experiment and in the review's summary)
- **Objective:** the knowledge keeps `_at`, a dict from (region, offset) to the ids of the variables starting there in insertion order, kept by `add`, `update` (a change of region or offset moves the id) and `remove`. `at` reads the dict and returns what the walk returned: the first variable in insertion order at that location, or None; when two share a location (a rejected guess and the one that replaced it) the walk order still decides.
- **Scope:** `understanding/knowledge.py`. **Out of scope:** the Research window's refresh while hidden (T-954).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. On random sequences of adds (rejected clashes allowed, as `add` allows), updates that move a variable, rejections and removals, `at` equals the old walk for every location of a small grid after every operation (a property test against a copy of the old walk).
  2. The understanding, services and UI suites pass unchanged.
  3. The profiled Research refresh at the user's scale falls from 45 ms to under 10 ms.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_at_reads_the_index_and_answers_like_the_old_walk` (property test, 100 examples); understanding, services and UI suites; full suite; the Research refresh profile before and after.
- **Results:** property test 100 examples; understanding, services, Research and discovery panel suites 297 passed; full suite 1426 passed, 10 skipped (141.05s); ruff and mypy clean; profiled Research refresh at the user's scale 45 ms → 6 ms (`at` 41 ms → under 1 ms).
- **Commit:** see PR #208. **PR:** #208.
- **Completion report:** the knowledge keeps a (region, offset) → ids index kept by `add`, `update` and `remove`; `at` reads it and, when two variables share a start, lets insertion order decide as the walk did, proved by a property test against a copy of the walk.
### T-952 – The confident relationships are kept in an index, not found by a walk
- **Status:** Done

- **Results:** property test 100 examples; understanding, rewards, services and discovery panel suites 338 passed; full suite 1426 passed, 10 skipped (155.95s); ruff and mypy clean; synthetic panel refresh with a relationship change 9.1 ms → 2.6 ms, full refresh 18.9 ms → 12.3 ms, the walk itself 41 ms → under 1 ms.
- **Commit:** see PR #207. **PR:** #207.

### T-951 – Proposals look bytes up instead of comparing every pair
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's stage log: `discovery: review` 77 × 2.7 s = 206 s of 18 minutes, each review holding the AI's step; a synthetic review at the user's scale, 904 candidates against 904 known variables, spent 150–265 ms of its 170–290 ms in `propose_candidates`, which asked `any(_overlaps(v, c) for v in knowledge)` for every candidate: 409,000 pair checks, each computing two widths through the encoding enum; with 1,600 guesses and as many candidates it is over a second)
- **Objective:** `propose_candidates` builds `_covered_bytes(knowledge)`, the set of every (region, offset) a variable spans, once per call, and a candidate is skipped when any byte of its span is in the set; a candidate it adds puts its bytes in the set so the next candidate over them is skipped as before. The same candidates are added and skipped as before, in the same order.
- **Scope:** `understanding/variables.py`. **Out of scope:** `rethink_candidates` (already a dict lookup), the rest of the review.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. On random layouts of known variables (bytes, words, sized digit runs in two regions) and random candidates over them, the new rule adds exactly the variables the old rule added, in order, and the knowledges end up the same (a property test against a copy of the old check).
  2. The understanding, linking and discovery suites pass unchanged.
  3. The synthetic review's propose share falls from about 150 ms to a few ms.

### T-948 – The suggestions ask the knowledge whether anything moved
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's first stage log, T-947 build, 18 minutes of Practise fast: 8,857 AI steps at 105 ms, of which `step: observe` was 78 ms and `discovery: announce` 94 ms × 5,221 = 492 s; the emulator 20 ms, learning 6.5 ms. An announce carries the step's events and relationships to the rewards and goals services, and each asked its `RelationshipWatch` whether the confident relationships had changed, which built a frozenset of (id, confidence) over every relationship in the knowledge, computing each pair's rates, twice a step, on a knowledge with tens of thousands of pairs)
- **Objective:** `Knowledge.confident_relationships_version` goes up when a relationship becomes at least LOW or stops being, changes level, or is confirmed, rejected or removed, and stays put when a write only moves a pair's counts within its level (the finder's usual write). `RelationshipWatch.changed(knowledge)` compares the knowledge object and that number with what it saw last. It reports every change the old walk found; it can also report a change the old walk would have missed (a level that moved and moved back between two reads), which costs one extra rebuild and nothing else.
- **Scope:** `understanding/knowledge.py`, `rewards/suggestions.py`. **Out of scope:** the save's cost (1.1 s each, 124 saves), the review's cost (2.7 s each), the Discovery panel's refresh (136 ms).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The version moves exactly on the writes that change what the suggestions read (a table of writes, confirms, rejects and removals).
  2. On random edit streams with reads between, the watch says "changed" whenever the old walk would have, and only when an edit since the last read changed the confident set (a property test); a fresh knowledge object counts as a change; `reset()` forgets.
  3. The rewards, goals and discovery suites pass unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** the files above, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_suggestions.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_confident_relationships_version_moves_only_when_the_suggestions_would_see_it`, `test_the_watch_reports_every_change_the_old_walk_found_and_no_more_than_the_edits_made` (property test, 80 examples); knowledge, rewards, rewards-service, goals and discovery suites; full suite.
- **Results:** knowledge, rewards, rewards-service, goals and discovery suites 164 passed (property test 80 examples); full suite 1422 passed, 10 skipped (162.51s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #203. **PR:** #203.
- **Completion report:** pending.

### T-946 – The relationship finder writes only what moved
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the T-945 profile of a synthetic step at the user's scale, 2 KB of RAM and 1,600 guesses: 12 ms a step, of which `RelationshipFinder.observe` took 6.5 ms; 355,040 `Relationship` objects were built over 300 steps, about 1,180 a step, because the finder rebuilt and rewrote every stored pair on every call so that its rates would follow the cause and effect counts)
- **Objective:** a pair's record changes only when its support grows or its cause or effect count moves. The finder writes the pairs whose support grew this call at once, each once, with the counts as they stand after the whole frame (before, a pair written early in a frame carried counts a later event of the same frame moved). The pairs whose cause or effect merely happened again are written by `flush()`, which runs every `refresh_every` calls (15, a second of play at four frames a step) and before the discovery service saves the knowledge, through an index from event key to the pairs it is in. A rate shown in the Discovery panel is therefore at most 15 steps old; a saved knowledge is exact.
- **Scope:** `understanding/relationships.py`, `services/discovery.py` (flush before save). **Out of scope:** the DQN's share of a step, the knowledge's relationship store.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. After every `observe`, the pairs reported as grown equal the ones the old rule reported, each written with the counts as they stand after the frame, and the knowledge holds exactly the pairs the old rule held; after `flush()`, every relationship equals the one the old rule wrote (a property test against a copy of the old refresh-everything rule on random streams with repeated keys, same-frame events and gaps longer than the window).
  2. A flush runs every `refresh_every` calls and before a save; `reset()` clears the index and the stale set; the existing finder tests pass, with a flush before a test reads a count-only change.
  3. The synthetic step's finder share falls from about 6.5 ms. (The first plan, an index alone with every touched pair rewritten each step, left it at 7.3 ms: the synthetic frame has 16 events, so nearly every stored pair touches one of them. Deferring the count-only writes is what cuts it.)
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/relationships.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_relationships.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_grown_pairs_are_exact_at_once_and_every_pair_after_a_flush` (property test, 60 examples), `test_a_flush_runs_every_refresh_every_calls_and_before_a_save`, `test_reset_forgets_the_index`; the understanding, discovery-service and discovery-panel suites; full suite.
- **Results:** the property test passes (60 examples); understanding, discovery-service and discovery-panel suites 205 passed; full suite 1420 passed, 10 skipped (156.90s); ruff and mypy clean; synthetic step finder share 6.5 ms → 2.75 ms (whole step 12.0 → 8.6 ms). One earlier full run stalled with every thread waiting at about the play-clock tests (`tests/ui/test_play_clock.py`, off-the-GUI-thread steps behind gates); it did not recur in the full rerun or in six runs of those files, and the finder takes no lock, so it is noted here as a flake to watch.
- **Commit:** see PR #201. **PR:** #201.
- **Completion report:** pending.

### T-945 – The change tracker counts in arrays
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the T-944 profile of a synthetic step at the user's scale, 2 KB of RAM and 1,600 guesses: 17.7 ms a step, of which `ChangeTracker.observe` took 5.1 ms walking every byte of the region in Python, with twenty attribute updates per changed byte, on every step of every go; a slower PC and the interpreter lock multiply it)
- **Objective:** `ChangeTracker` keeps one `_RegionStats` per region, an int64 array per statistic (samples, changes, ups, downs, minimum, maximum, last, wraps, carry-ins, unit steps, max step, max/min/small/unit/last/same/usual hop) and a bytes × 256 boolean table of the values seen, and counts a transition with array operations: the same rules as before, roll-overs, carries from the byte below, first-change `min_hop` and repeated hops included. `stats(region, offset)` builds a `ByteStats` from the arrays (a fresh object, not a live one), `all_stats()` builds every byte's, `classify()` groups by masks, and `diff_snapshots` finds the changed offsets in one array comparison before making `ByteChange` objects for them alone. `move_on` (an expected jump, T-935) takes the values without counting, as before.
- **Scope:** `observation/diff.py`. **Out of scope:** the relationship finder and the DQN's share of a step.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Every statistic of every byte equals what the one-byte-at-a-time loop counted, on random streams with walks, roll-overs with carries, repeats and restores (a property test against a hand-written copy of the old loop).
  2. The existing tracker, classifier, discovery, observer and research tests pass unchanged, except that a test reading a statistic after further snapshots re-reads it.
  3. The synthetic step's tracker share falls from about 5 ms to about 1 ms, most of that the diff's change objects.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_arrays_count_exactly_as_the_byte_loop_did` (property test, 60 examples); the observation, variables, discovery, observer and research-dialog suites; full suite.
- **Results:** the property test passes (60 examples); observation, variables, discovery, observer and research-dialog suites 223 passed; full suite 1417 passed, 10 skipped (154.70s); ruff and mypy clean; synthetic step tracker share 5.1 ms → 0.97 ms.

### T-944 – "Practise fast" rests the panels
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user, recording the game under "Practise fast": "nothing about it looks fast to me"; the game ran at about two thirds of normal speed, and the log said why: the emulator took 30 ms a step, the app's own step 140 ms on average over 44,916 steps, while the window's thread was busy 58% of the time, 1,500 s of it in "discoveries refresh" and "on DiscoveryUpdated", the knowledge panel refreshing three times a second after every step's events and repainting; a synthetic step at the same scale, 2 KB of RAM and 1,600 guesses, costs 18 ms of pure work, so the rest is the window's thread holding the interpreter)
- **Objective:** While the AI practises fast, nobody is watching the panels, so they rest: `TrainingService.practising_fast` is true while training runs in an AI mode at `Speed.FAST` (the condition `_apply_speed` already used), `Coalescer.rest(on)` lengthens a panel's refresh window to `REST_DELAY_MS` (2,000 ms) and `rest(False)` puts its own delay back, and the Discovery and Rewards panels follow `TrainingStateChanged` with it, as the Game panel already drops its pictures to 2 a second (T-836) through the same condition. A refresh still runs when the AI stops or slows down, so what a person sees when they come back is current.

### T-943 – A removed reward stays removed
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user: "every time i remove something from the rewards it auto readds it" and "i want to be able to remove everything i want to"; T-937's Remove rejects the AI's guess behind a suggested row, and the AI's next guess of the same kind takes the offered seat and brings a row of the same name straight back, while the reset row and the button rows could not be removed at all, and rejecting a guess a person had confirmed threw away their own word)
- **Objective:** Remove takes out whichever row is picked, and what a person removed is a rule the profile remembers. `RewardProfile.dismissed` holds rule keys: `rule_key(event_kind, var_kind)` ("setback:<lives>") for a suggested row, so no later guess of that kind gets a row for that event, and the behaviour id for the reset row and the button rows. `suggest_behaviours` and `apply_suggestions` skip dismissed rules, `ensure_button_rows` skips dismissed rows, and the keys are saved with the profile. `RewardService.dismiss(bid)` works out the key from the row's trigger and the knowledge, records it, takes the row out, re-reads the own-reward watch list, saves and announces; `suggest_again()` clears the dismissals and refreshes. The Rewards panel's Remove calls `remove_own` for a row of one's own and `dismiss` for every other row, with a note saying it stays out for this game, and a "Bring back suggestions" button (enabled while something is dismissed) undoes it. Remove no longer rejects a guess: telling the AI a guess is wrong stays under What the AI is doing.
- **Scope:** `rewards/model.py`, `rewards/suggestions.py`, `rewards/buttons.py`, `services/rewards.py`, `ui/rewards_panel.py`. **Out of scope:** the Progress panel's steps.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Dismissing "Losing Lives" removes the row, and a new lives guess of the AI brings no "Losing …" row back; "Gaining Lives" and the rows of other kinds are untouched; the guess is not rejected.
  2. The reset row and a button row can be removed and stay out across a reconnect; the profile round-trips its dismissals; an older file loads with none.
  3. "Bring back suggestions" restores every dismissed rule on the next refresh.
  4. Remove on a row of one's own still simply removes it.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/rewards/model.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/rewards/suggestions.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/rewards/buttons.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/rewards/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/rewards.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/rewards_panel.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_suggestions.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_model.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_buttons.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_rewards.py`, `tests/ui/test_rewards_panel.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_dismissed_rule_is_not_suggested_again` (suggestions), `test_dismissals_round_trip` (model), `test_a_dismissed_button_row_is_not_added_back` (buttons), `test_dismiss_takes_a_row_out_for_good_and_suggest_again_undoes_it` (service), `test_remove_takes_any_row_out_and_it_stays_out` (panel, replacing T-937's test); the rewards suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new tests pass; rewards model, buttons, suggestions, service and panel tests 81 passed; full suite 1407 passed, 10 skipped (149.28s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #198. **PR:** #198.
- **Completion report:** pending.

### T-942 – Playing yourself runs the game at the emulator's own pace
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user, connected with the AI idle and playing the game himself, found it "difficult to play with all the pause buffers going on": in hold mode the app drives every frame, each timer pulse sends the frames the wall clock owes, up to six in one request, and the link's round trip sits between the bursts, so the game runs in lurches)
- **Objective:** A person's play is the emulator's own time. `EmulatorAdapter` gains `hold`, `free_run`, `supports_free_run` and `set_free_run` (the base holds and cannot switch; BizHawk can; mGBA never holds). `PlayClock` asks the adapter at connect: when the emulator can run on its own (free run offered, or no hold at all), Play switches it to free run through the first step's task (`mode free_run=true`, on the worker, before the step) and every timer pulse sends one frame to watch (reads, the pad, a picture) with no owed-frame accounting, since the emulator keeps time; Pause queues `set_free_run(False)` on the worker after any step in flight, so the game stands still exactly as before. A holding emulator with no free run (the practice console) is driven as before. In the script, the free-run main loop applies the app's held pad only when the last step did not watch, so a person's real controller reaches the game between watched steps. `TrainingService.start` puts a free-running link back in hold mode before its first step, so the AI's steps stay one request per frame and a go's restores and experiments are untouched. Nothing here is game-specific.
- **Scope:** `emulator/base.py`, `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `emulator/mgba/adapter.py`, `emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `ui/play_clock.py`, `training/service.py`. **Out of scope:** the picture pump's rate; the AI playing in free run.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. On a BizHawk link, Play sends `mode free_run=true` before the first step, each pulse then sends a one-frame step however many frames the wall clock owes, and Pause sends `mode free_run=false` after the step in flight; the simulator reports the game unpaused while playing and paused after.
  2. On the practice console the clock behaves exactly as before (owed frames, the cap, forgiveness).
  3. In the script, with free run on, a watched step leaves the person's controller to the game on the turns between steps; a step that holds the pad takes it back.
  4. Starting training on a free-running link puts it in hold mode before the first step.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/base.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/mgba/adapter.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/play_clock.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/ui/test_play_clock.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `tests/unit/emulator/test_base.py` or the adapter tests, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_play_runs_a_bizhawk_link_free_and_pause_holds_it` (clock, simulator); `test_the_timer_sends_the_frames_the_wall_clock_owes` unchanged (practice console); `test_free_run_leaves_the_real_controller_alone_between_watched_steps` (Lua host); `test_the_base_adapter_holds_and_cannot_free_run`; `test_training_starts_in_hold_mode` (training service, simulator); the play clock, Lua, adapter and training suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new tests pass; play clock, emulator, training and game panel tests 450 passed; full suite 1408 passed, 10 skipped (148.37s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #197. **PR:** #197.
- **Completion report:** A person's play no longer waits on the app: BizHawk runs the game at its own pace from Play to Pause while the app watches a frame at a time, and the real controller is left alone between the app's looks. The AI's own play is unchanged: training holds the game again before its first step. The practice console is stepped as before. What this does not do: the AI still plays in hold mode, one request per frame, which is right for its restores and experiments.

### T-941 – A number held over several bytes is one value
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user tracked the score's hundreds digit with Search values: 6 = 600, 7 = 700, 8 = 800, then at 1,000 the byte read 0 again, because the game keeps the score one decimal digit per byte over six bytes and the search kept, named and rewarded single bytes)
- **Objective:** A number a game holds over several bytes is found, shown, named and rewarded as one value. `Encoding` gains `UINT_LE`/`UINT_BE` (binary over the value's bytes), `BCD_LE`/`BCD_BE` (packed decimal over several bytes) and `DIGITS_LE`/`DIGITS_BE` (one decimal digit per byte, lowest or highest digit first); their width is the variable's own `length` (1 to 4 bytes for binary and BCD, 1 to 8 for digits), and `decode` reads them, refusing a byte that is not a digit. `ValueSearch` keeps candidate values, each a span of bytes with an encoding, instead of bytes: `forms(value)` lists every (encoding, bytes) a game might hold the number as, `spans(data, value)` finds them in a region and extends each over the zero bytes beyond its high end (a 600 shown as 000600 is six digits, not three), so a typed number starts a search of every layout at once; "Went up", "Went down", "Changed" and "Didn't change" compare the decoded number of each span, and a span whose bytes stop being digits is dropped; "Is exactly" keeps the spans holding the number whose bytes are all still in the running. A search from every byte starts with one-byte values as before. `Match` carries the encoding and length and an `address` like `ram[2013..2018]`. The Research window shows one row per value with a "Held as" column, keeps "Now" live for spans, and `reward_search` names the value with its layout: `DiscoveryService.name_value` takes the encoding and length, corrects the AI's guess at that address to the person's layout (its kind reset when the layout changed and nobody had confirmed it), and sets aside, by the person's word, the AI's unconfirmed guesses on any other byte inside the span, since a byte that is part of a value a person named is not a value of its own. The Rewards table and the discovery list show a span's range. Nothing here names a game: the layouts are the ones 8-bit and 16-bit games use, and every rule reads the typed number and the bytes.
- **Scope:** `understanding/knowledge.py` (`Encoding`, `Variable.length`/`address`, `decode`, `propose`/`correct`), `observation/search.py` (`forms`, `spans`, `ValueSearch`, `Match`), `services/discovery.py` (`name_value`), `ui/research_dialog.py`, `ui/rewards_panel.py` (`_address`), `ui/discovery_panel.py` (`variable_line`). **Out of scope:** the classifier proposing multi-byte values on its own; signed or floating layouts.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `decode` reads every sized encoding both ways round and refuses a non-digit byte; a `Variable` with a sized encoding needs a length in range and its `width` is that length; files without a length load as before.
  2. Searching 600 in a region holding 000600 one digit per byte lists the six-digit span (and its shorter readings); after the number becomes 1000, "Went up" keeps the span reading 1000 and drops the three-digit reading; "Is exactly 1000" agrees.
  3. A search from every byte, then "Is exactly 3", "Went down" and the rest work as before (one-byte values), and a 128 KB region still scans in under 50 ms.
  4. In the Research window a found span shows as one row with its range, its number and how it is held; picking it and pressing Reward makes a value of that layout, confirmed under the name, whose reward pays when the whole number goes up; the AI's guesses on the other bytes of the span are set aside.
  5. The Rewards table's Address column and the discovery list show the range of a multi-byte value.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/search.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/research_dialog.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/rewards_panel.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/discovery_panel.py`, tests under `tests/unit/understanding`, `tests/unit/observation`, `tests/unit/services`, `tests/ui`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_sized_encodings_decode_both_ways_round`, `test_a_sized_variable_needs_a_length` (knowledge); `test_forms_name_how_each_pattern_is_held`, `test_a_score_over_six_digit_bytes_is_one_value`, `test_a_span_that_stops_being_digits_is_dropped`, `test_is_exactly_keeps_spans_over_bytes_still_running` (search); `test_naming_a_span_sets_the_byte_guesses_inside_it_aside` (discovery); `test_a_value_over_several_bytes_is_one_row_and_one_reward` (dialog); `test_the_address_column_shows_a_range` (rewards panel); the existing search, dialog and knowledge tests; full suite.
- **Results:** 10 new tests pass; search, knowledge, discovery and UI tests 150 passed; full suite 1403 passed, 10 skipped (154.29s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #196. **PR:** #196.
- **Completion report:** A number is now a value, not a byte, wherever a person meets it: the search finds a score held one digit per byte as one row that reads 600, follows it through 1,000 as one number, and names and rewards it in that layout, with the AI's stray guesses on its digits set aside. Typing the number with its zeros (000600) settles how many digits it has. The layouts are the ones every 8-bit and 16-bit game uses, so nothing is game-specific. The classifier still proposes single bytes on its own; teaching it to see a digit-per-byte number without a person's help is a later task.

### T-940 – The survival count follows the lives the AI rewards
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 950-go log: "lost a life 45 times" a go from every life-count guess's setbacks, while the confirmed Lifes row paid about once a go; T-939 sets the stray guesses aside once a person confirms one, but with nobody confirming anything the measure would still add up every guess)
- **Objective:** `EpisodeSignals.watch_setbacks(ids)` keeps only the `SETBACK` events of the given variables (None keeps every one, as before). `EpisodeRunner.begin` sets it at every go to the knowledge's best lives guess and best health guess (`Knowledge.best`, which puts the person's confirmed guess first), through a new `StateEncoder.knowledge` property, so the go line's "lost a life N times" and the survival line count the one life count and the one health the rewards also follow. The rule reads the knowledge's own ranking and nothing about the game.
- **Scope:** `training/episode.py` (`EpisodeSignals.watch_setbacks`, `EpisodeRunner.begin`), `learning/state.py` (`StateEncoder.knowledge`). **Out of scope:** the rewards (they already follow the offered guesses); counting resets as deaths.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With two lives guesses, a setback on the lesser one is not counted and one on the best is; the first-loss step follows the counted one.
  2. With the watch set to None every setback counts (the signals on their own are unchanged).
  3. A person's confirmed life count outranks a surer-looking unconfirmed one.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/state.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_only_the_best_life_count_s_losses_are_counted` (runner); training suite; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new test passes; training and learning tests 169 passed; full suite 1384 passed, 10 skipped (151.93s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #195. **PR:** #195.
- **Completion report:** The survival measure now counts what a death is to the rewards: the drops of the one life count and the one health the knowledge ranks first, with a person's confirmed guess ahead of any evidence. The signals on their own still count every value, so nothing else that reads them changed. On the user's game the line will read about one death a go instead of 45, which is the number to watch.

### T-939 – One life count, one health, one score per game
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 950-go log: "lost a life 45 times" a go, the first always at step 31, while the confirmed "Lifes" row paid about once a go; the AI held three other "life count" guesses that ticked down like a timer, and they sat in what the AI sees, in the survival line and in the setback events every step)
- **Objective:** `ONE_PER_GAME` = lives, health, score. When `DiscoveryService.confirm` or `rename` makes a guess of one of those kinds the person's word, every other non-rejected, unconfirmed guess of that kind is given a "your-word" evidence line ("You confirmed Lifes as the game's lives; a game has one.") and rejected by "your word" (not by the AI, so it is not rethought), the log says so, and the `DiscoveryUpdated` carries the confirmed guess and the ones set aside, so the rewards, the steps, the state and the survival count all follow at once. The rule is the one `tell` already applies for a moment the person reported; it reads nothing about the game.
- **Scope:** `services/discovery.py` (`ONE_PER_GAME`, `_set_others_aside`, `confirm`, `rename`). **Out of scope:** progress, positions, flags (a game has several); guesses the person confirmed themselves (two confirmed life counts are both the person's word).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With two lives guesses, confirming one rejects the other with a "your-word" line, `by_kind(LIVES)` holds only the confirmed one, and the announced update's `changed` holds both.
  2. Naming (rename) a score guess does the same for the other score guesses; confirming a position guess leaves the other positions alone; a second lives guess the person confirmed earlier is left alone.
  3. The set-aside guess is not rethought (`rejected_by_ai` is false).
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_confirming_the_lives_sets_the_ai_s_other_life_counts_aside` (discovery); services suite; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new test passes; discovery tests 31 passed; full suite 1383 passed, 10 skipped (159.96s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #194. **PR:** #194.
- **Completion report:** The person's confirmation now carries the weight the reported moment already did: one life count, one health, one score. The user's three timer-like life counts will be set aside the moment "Lifes" is confirmed again (or at once if it still is, on the next confirm or rename of it), and with them go the false setbacks in the state, the rewards and the survival line.

### T-938 – Steps of guesses the AI no longer has are retired
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's Progress panel showed four green "Get further in Progress" steps, two of them "detected by Progress of v…", while the Rewards panel had two progress rows; the steps were built on guesses the knowledge no longer had at all)
- **Objective:** `merge_suggestions` retires every suggested progress step whose guess is not among the offered progress guesses, whether the knowledge has changed its mind about the guess (as before) or no longer has it at all (new), unless the knowledge is empty (the AI has not looked at the game yet, and a person's edits on a suggested step would be lost for nothing). The Rewards panel's `apply_suggestions` already removes a reward whose guess is gone; the Progress panel kept such a step on purpose ("an objective for a variable the knowledge has forgotten entirely is kept") and that is what piled up: a step built on a guess that is gone can never complete or be reworded, its "Detected by" reads "Progress of v12", and a "Done" it carries is from a run with other guesses. A person's own steps (not `suggested`) are never touched.
- **Scope:** `rewards/goals.py` (`merge_suggestions`), its tests. **Out of scope:** why the user's knowledge lost those guesses (a knowledge rebuilt from scratch keeps the goal file's steps; the rule covers every cause); the Rewards panel (already right).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A suggested step whose variable id the knowledge does not know is removed by `merge_suggestions` once the knowledge has any guess, and the removal counts as a change the goal service announces.
  2. A suggested step whose guess the knowledge still offers stays, a person's own step with an unknown variable stays, and nothing is retired while the knowledge is empty.
  3. The service test: a goal file holding a step for a guess the knowledge lacks keeps it while the knowledge is empty and loses it as soon as the AI knows the game without that guess.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/rewards/goals.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_goals.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_goals.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_merge_retires_and_rewords_stale_suggestions` (the orphan is now retired, a person's own orphan kept, an empty knowledge retires nothing), `test_a_step_of_a_guess_the_ai_no_longer_has_goes` (service); goal tests; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new service test and the reworked unit test pass; goal tests 27 passed; full suite 1381 passed, 10 skipped (184.47s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #193. **PR:** #193.
- **Completion report:** The Progress panel now follows the same rule as the Rewards panel: a suggested step whose guess the AI no longer has goes, whether the AI changed its mind or lost the guess with a rebuilt knowledge. The one case kept is an empty knowledge, when the AI has not looked at the game yet, so a person's dependencies and ticks on suggested steps survive a reconnection, as the existing service test checks. The user's four stale steps will go at the first refresh after the next connection.

### T-937 – Remove works on every reward row
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user found the real score and lives, and asked for "a way to remove all the bits in the image that are wrong, it's like they're hard baked in there": the Rewards panel's Remove only took out rewards of one's own, and the wrong guesses had to be rejected in another panel)
- **Objective:** `RewardsPanel.remove_selected()` takes out whichever row is picked. A reward of one's own is removed as before. A row the AI suggested has a guess behind it (`trigger.variable_id`): the panel calls `DiscoveryService.reject` on it, saves the knowledge, and the note says 'Told the AI that "Lifes goes up" is wrong: its rewards are gone and it will not guess that byte again.'; the existing rule removes every reward row of a rejected guess and keeps the guess as rejected so it is not re-proposed. A row with no guess behind it (Starting over, the button rows) is not removable; the note says to untick it to switch it off. The button is enabled whenever a row is picked and the game is connected; its tooltip says what it does to a guess. This is the same "No, that's wrong" the discovery panel has, reached from where the person sees the wrong row, so nothing new is decided for the person and nothing is game-specific.
- **Scope:** `ui/rewards_panel.py` (`remove_selected`, the button's tooltip and enabling). **Out of scope:** removing a guess the person confirmed (rejecting it un-confirms it, as the discovery panel does); a confirmation dialog (a rejection can be undone with "Yes, that's right" in the discovery panel).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With an AI-suggested row picked, Remove is enabled; pressing it leaves no reward row for that guess, the guess is rejected in the knowledge, the note names the row, and a later `refresh_suggestions` does not bring it back.
  2. With a row of one's own picked, Remove takes it out as before ("Reward removed.").
  3. With a button row or "Starting over" picked, the rows stay and the note says to untick it.
  4. With no row picked, or no game connected, the button is disabled.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/rewards_panel.py`, `tests/ui/test_rewards_panel.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_remove_tells_the_ai_a_suggested_guess_is_wrong` (panel); the own-reward test calls `remove_selected`; ui suite; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new test passes; rewards panel tests 14 passed; full suite 1381 passed, 10 skipped (182.87s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #192. **PR:** #192.
- **Completion report:** Remove now does from the Rewards panel what "No, that's wrong" does from the discovery panel, for the row the person is looking at. The service rules were already there (a rejected guess loses its reward rows and is not proposed again); the panel only needed to reach them. Rows with no guess behind them keep their tick as the way to switch them off.

### T-936 – The value search takes any number the game shows
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user typed the score 300 into Search values and got "A byte holds 0 to 255"; a score is almost never one byte)
- **Objective:** `parse_number` accepts 0 to `MAX_VALUE` (99,999,999). `patterns(value)` lists every byte sequence a game might hold that number as: binary in as many bytes as it needs (one to four), little- and big-endian; packed BCD (two digits per byte), either byte order; and one decimal digit per byte, most-significant first or last (for numbers of two digits or more). `holding(data, value)` marks every byte that takes part in any of those sequences, and `ValueSearch.start(value)` and `refine(EXACT, value)` keep those bytes. A number that fits one byte behaves as before, plus its one-byte BCD form (12 also matches 0x12). The hint, placeholder and error text name the real range. Nothing is game-specific: the representations are the ones 8-bit and 16-bit machines use.
- **Scope:** `observation/search.py` (`MAX_VALUE`, `RANGE_TEXT`, `patterns`, `holding`, `parse_number`, `ValueSearch.start`/`refine`), the placeholder in `ui/research_dialog.py`. **Out of scope:** showing a multi-byte value as one number in the table (the rows stay bytes, which is what naming and rewards work on).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `patterns(300)` includes `(0x2C, 0x01)`, `(0x01, 0x2C)`, `(0x03, 0x00)`, `(0x00, 0x03)` and `(3, 0, 0)` and `(0, 0, 3)`; `patterns(12)` is `(12,)`, `(0x12,)`, `(1, 2)` and `(2, 1)`; `patterns(5)` is `(5,)` alone; `patterns(70000)` has three-byte binary forms; no pattern is listed twice.
  2. Searching 300 in a region holding `2C 01` at 10, `03 00` at 20 and `03 00 00`... keeps bytes 10, 11, 20, 21 (and the digit run); a region without it keeps none; a window never crosses the end of a region.
  3. `refine(EXACT, 300)` keeps only candidates that still take part in a 300; `refine(EXACT, 3)` on bytes is unchanged.
  4. `parse_number("300")` is 300, `parse_number("99999999")` is accepted, `"100000000"` and `"-1"` are refused with the range in the message; the dialog accepts "300" and its note no longer says 0 to 255.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/search.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/__init__.py` (exports), `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/research_dialog.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_search.py`, `tests/ui/test_research_dialog.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_patterns_list_every_way_a_game_holds_a_number`, `test_a_big_number_is_found_across_bytes`, `test_is_exactly_with_a_big_number`, new rows in the parse tests; the dialog test types 300; observation and ui suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 3 new tests and 3 new parse rows pass; search and dialog tests 44 passed; full suite 1380 passed, 10 skipped (185.57s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #191. **PR:** #191.
- **Completion report:** The search now takes the number a person sees. A score of 300 is looked for as two binary bytes in either order, as packed decimal digits in either order and as one digit per byte in either order, and every byte that takes part stays in the search until a scan rules it out. The rows stay bytes, which is what naming a value and rewarding it work on, so the rest of the panel is unchanged. The criterion that `patterns(12)` is two forms was tightened to four: a two-digit number also gets its digit-per-byte forms, which is what the rule says for every number of two digits or more.

### T-935 – A restore is not a change
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 20:03 log: "You confirmed "Lifes", but it went up 17 times and down 17; progress does not bounce" at 18:15, when the lives byte only ever went down in play; every go starts by putting the game back to the checkpoint, and the change statistics counted each restore as the lives going up)
- **Objective:** When the AI puts the game back to a saved state (the go's checkpoint, the practise spot, or an experiment's start and end), the next snapshot's jump is not a change the game made. `ChangeTracker.expect_jump()` makes the next `observe` move on (previous snapshot, each byte's `last`, minimum, maximum and distinct values) without counting a sample, change, up, down, hop or wrap, and the diff is still returned so anyone watching the transition can see it; `transitions` leaves the jump out. `ObservationService.expect_jump()` forwards to the tracker. `ExperimentHarness(connection, *, slot, on_restore)` calls `on_restore` after every `load_state` (`restore()`, and the start and end of a trial), and the training and discovery services build their harnesses with `on_restore=self._observation.expect_jump`; `TrainingService._restore_spot` calls it too. The rule reads nothing about the game, so it holds for any game and any guess.
- **Scope:** `observation/diff.py`, `services/observer.py`, `services/experiments.py`, `training/service.py`, `services/discovery.py`. **Out of scope:** the event detector's view of a restore (the runner already ignores a reset seen just after a restore); undoing the ups the user's current statistics already hold (they start from zero at every connection).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. After `expect_jump()`, an `observe` whose snapshot differs returns the diff, leaves `samples`, `changes`, `ups`, `downs` and `transitions` as they were, and sets `last` to the new value; the observe after that counts normally from the restored value. `expect_jump()` before the first snapshot and `reset()` leave nothing pending.
  2. A harness built with `on_restore` calls it once for `restore()` and twice for a trial that restores afterwards (once for a trial that does not), and never when a restore fails before `load_state`.
  3. A go that starts from a checkpoint leaves the observation tracker's `ups` for the lives byte unchanged by the restore (the practise spot and the training harness both tell the observation service), while a byte the game changes during the go is still counted.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/observation/diff.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/observer.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/experiments.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/observation/test_diff.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_experiments.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_observer.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_tracker_skips_an_expected_jump` (diff), `test_the_observation_service_forwards_expect_jump` (observer), `test_the_harness_reports_every_restore` (experiments), `test_a_go_s_restore_is_not_counted_as_a_change` (service); observation, services and training suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 4 new tests pass; observation, services and training suites 315 passed; full suite 1374 passed, 10 skipped (173.61s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #190. **PR:** #190.
- **Completion report:** A restore is the AI's own doing and the change statistics now treat it that way. The tracker moves past the snapshot after a restore without counting it, the harness tells the observation service after every load, and the practise spot does the same, so the lives byte the user confirmed will no longer be told it "went up 17 times" because 17 goes started from the checkpoint. The diff is still returned, so nothing that watches a transition is blinded. The user's current statistics are not rewritten: they start from zero at every connection anyway.

### T-934 – Survival in the log
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 20:03 log: whether the AI was learning the first enemy had to be worked out by hand from the "Losing Lives" earners of 160 go lines; the review promised a measure per build)
- **Objective:** A go's result records how many life or health drops (`SETBACK` events) it saw and the step of the first; the "Go N ended" line ends ", lost a life N time(s), the first at step S" when any were lost; and every `RESULTS_WINDOW` (10) goes, from the twentieth, the service logs "Survival over the last 10 goes: lives lost per go 1.3 (was 0.4 over the 10 before); the first life lost after 120 steps on average (was 310)." from the history, so a log says whether the AI is surviving longer without anyone counting. The measure reads only the events the AI already acts on, so it holds for any game.
- **Scope:** `EpisodeSignals` counts setbacks per step (`take_setbacks`), `EpisodeRunner._settle` notes the first one's step, `EpisodeResult.setbacks`/`first_setback_step` (saved and loaded, older goes load with none); `describe_survival(goes, window)` in `training/curve.py`; the two log lines in `TrainingService._record`. **Out of scope:** the Progress panel (the log is the ask); counting resets or stuck ends as deaths.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A go with two setback events at steps 3 and 7 ends with `setbacks == 2` and `first_setback_step == 3`; a go with none has 0 and None; a saved result round-trips both and one saved before this task loads with none.
  2. The go line ends ", lost a life 2 times, the first at step 3" and says nothing about lives when none were lost.
  3. `describe_survival` with fewer than two windows says so in one short sentence; with twenty goes it gives both averages and the "was" of each; goes with no loss count towards lives per go and are left out of the first-loss average, which says "never" when no go in the window lost one.
  4. The survival line is logged after the twentieth and thirtieth goes, not after the tenth or the twenty-first.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/curve.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/__init__.py` (export), `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_curve.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- **Results:** 4 new tests pass; training suite 91 passed; full suite 1370 passed, 10 skipped (172.31s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #189. **PR:** #189.
- **Completion report:** The log now carries the measure the review asked for. Each go line ends with the lives lost and the step of the first loss, and every tenth go from the twentieth a "Survival over the last 10 goes" line compares lives lost per go and the steps before the first loss with the ten goes before. The runner found the toy racer losing lives of its own during the test, which is what the measure is for; the test counts at least the losses it sent.

### T-933 – The log says whether the saved brain was picked up, and Start from scratch asks first

The user's next log will show whether a run carried on from the one before.

### T-932 – Exploring follows results
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 20:03 log: in a 109-go run the chance of a random move hit its 5% floor at go 14 and lives lost per 30-second go then climbed from 0.17 in the first dozen goes to 1.83 in the last dozen, with points per go falling the same way; a 53-go run after a restart did the same)
- **Objective:** Exploring falls on a fixed schedule (T-875's line over `explore_steps` plus the per-go decay) whether or not the goes are getting better, so a brain that locks into a bad habit as exploring ends has no way back. A `ResultsWatch` (new `learning/exploring.py`) keeps the rewards of the last twenty goes; when the latest ten average worse than the ten before by more than a tenth of their spread, exploring goes back up to `Settings.rebound` (0.25 by default, 0 to 1, 0 turns it off) and is held there while the goes stay worse; once a window is no longer worse the lift eases off by a tenth of the rebound a go, and the schedule rules again. Both learners follow it (so the skills learner does too), the runner hands each go's reward to `end_episode`, the brain saves the watch, the Research window gets an "Exploring rebound" row, and the rise is logged once ("Exploring back up to 25% after go N: the last 10 goes averaged +3.8 points, down from +21.9.").
- **Scope:** `learning/exploring.py` (`ResultsWatch`, `RESULTS_WINDOW`, `NOTICEABLE`); `Settings.rebound`, `QLearningAgent.lift`/`recent_rewards`/`_scheduled`, `end_episode(reward)` in `learning/qlearning.py`; the same in `learning/dqn.py`; the `Learner` protocol, `SkillsLearner.end_episode`, `GreedyPlayer.end_episode`; `EpisodeRunner.finish` passes the go's reward; `SETTING_FIELDS` in `ui/research_dialog.py`. **Out of scope:** judging the goes by anything but their reward (a survival measure is T-934); keeping the brain between runs (it is kept already; T-933 logs it).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A table learner at its 5% floor given ten goes of +10 and then ten of 0 is at 25% after the twentieth, stays there while the latest ten are worse than the ten before, and is back at 5% ten goes after they stop being worse; a learned step in between does not pull it below the lift.
  2. The network learner does the same, and a saved brain of either kind comes back with its lift and its last goes; a brain saved before this task loads with none.
  3. A drop within a tenth of the rewards' spread, or no spread at all, lifts nothing; `rebound` 0 lifts nothing; `rebound` outside 0 to 1 is refused.
  4. The runner hands each go's reward to the learner; the skills learner hands it to every skill.
  5. The Research window lists "Exploring rebound" at 0.25 and applies 0.
  6. The rise is logged once per rise, not every go.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/exploring.py` (new), `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/qlearning.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/dqn.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/learner.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/skills.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/greedy.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/episode.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/ui/research_dialog.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_exploring.py` (new), `tests/unit/learning/test_qlearning.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_dqn.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_skills.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_learner.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `tests/ui/test_research_dialog.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_exploring.py` (four tests: rise and ease, noise and off, rebound change and round trip, the log line); `test_exploring_rises_again_when_the_goes_get_worse` in the table and network suites; the skills, runner and dialog assertions; learning + training + integration + dialog suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 4 new tests in `test_exploring.py` and 2 in the learner suites pass; learning + training + integration + dialog suites 176 passed; full suite 1365 passed, 10 skipped (174.19s); ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #187. **PR:** #187.
- **Completion report:** Exploring now has a thermostat. The schedule still takes it to the floor, but a `ResultsWatch` per learner compares the last ten goes' rewards with the ten before and, when they are worse by more than a tenth of their spread, holds exploring at `Settings.rebound` (25%) until a window is no longer worse, then lets it ease off over ten goes. The judgement reads nothing but the goes' rewards, so it is the same for every game and every reward profile. The user's 20:03 run, whose lives lost per go climbed for 95 goes at 5% exploring, would have gone back to 25% at the first worse window. Whether that is enough to learn the first jump is for the next run to say; T-934 adds the measure.

### T-931 – Copies of the player's position count once in the distance pairs
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 17:03 log: "8 distances to moving things from Position at ram[533] which the stick moves" with one thing moving: the same enemy measured from ram[533], 537, 541 and 545, four sprite columns of the player moved by A, Left and Right)
- **Objective:** `_pair_up` pairs each mover with each of the player's own positions (T-915: the x the stick moves and the y, say), and `MOST_DISTANCES` (8) caps the pairs. Four own positions that the same buttons move are one position drawn four times; pairing every mover with all four spends the eight seats on two movers. Own positions with the same input-link evidence are copies and the best-believed one stands for them; own positions with different buttons (an x and a y) are both kept, as are confirmed ones.
- **Scope:** `_pair_up` in `learning/state.py`. **Out of scope:** the order of the pairs (the best own position's first, as before).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Four own positions with the same input-link detail, one with another, and three movers: six pairs, the three movers with the first copy and then with the other axis, no pair with a later copy.
  2. Eight movers and the same own positions: eight pairs, all with the first copy.
  3. The T-912, T-915 and T-923 pairing tests hold.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/learning/state.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_state.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_copies_of_my_position_count_once_in_the_pairs`; learning + training + integration suites; full suite.
- **Results:** 1 new test passes; learning + training + integration 158 passed; full suite 1359 passed, 10 skipped (177.96s).
- **Commit:** see PR #186. **PR:** #186.
- **Completion report:** The 17:03 log spent all eight distance seats on one moving thing measured from four copies of the player's OAM x (ram[533], [537], [541], [545]), each linked to the same buttons. `_pair_up` now keys each own position by the set of buttons it is linked to and keeps the first (best-ranked) per set, so the four copies count once and the seats go to eight different moving things. Positions with no input link keep their own seat each, so nothing is lost for a game whose player position is unlinked. The generic rule is in the state encoder, not in any game knowledge.

### T-110 – Spike: confirm BizHawk Lua ↔ socket protocol
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** Fix the communication route between EmuHawk and the app, the message framing and the command set, record it in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §5, and say exactly what still needs a real emulator.
- **Scope:** Documentation only. Checked the BizHawk Lua reference for the `comm`, `memory`, `joypad`, `emu`, `savestate`, `client` and `gameinfo` functions, and Archipelago's `connector_bizhawk_generic.lua` as a worked example of a Lua-side TCP server with newline-delimited JSON on current EmuHawk. Chose LuaSocket (script is the server, app connects) over the built-in `comm.socketServer*` client (needs `--socket_ip/--socket_port` at launch; framing changed in 2.6.2). Defined the commands (`hello`, `read`, `input`, `step`, `screenshot`, `speed`, `save`, `load`, `mode`, `ping`, `bye`), the bundled `step` so a training step is one round trip, and two run modes (hold: emulator paused, frames advance on request; free-run fallback). Decided the test strategy: the Lua script runs in CI under `lupa` (Lua 5.4 in-process) against stubbed EmuHawk globals, plus a Python `BizHawkSimulator` over a real socket. **Out of scope:** any code (T-111 onward); measuring throughput on a real machine (T-117).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §5 documents route, framing, commands with the Lua API behind each, timing, run modes, throughput expectations and the testing approach.
  2. ADR-005 is Accepted with the limitation named (real-emulator behaviour verified in T-117).
  3. Every assumption that only a real EmuHawk can confirm is listed in the "verify" column so T-117 has a checklist.
- **Expected changes:** `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** n/a (documentation). The protocol itself is tested from T-111 on.
- **Results:** n/a. Full suite unchanged (819 passing).
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #59.
- **Completion report:** The honest limitation: no EmuHawk runs in this environment, so the spike confirms the route from documentation and a widely used working script, not by measurement. Everything that depends on the real emulator (the domain list format, button names per core, whether `emu.frameadvance()` moves a paused emulator, the screenshot file format and cost, `client.speedmode` range) is listed in §5.2 for T-117. The hold-mode question is the one that matters for the adapter's semantics; the free-run fallback keeps the adapter usable either way. `lupa` is added as a dev dependency in T-111 so the Lua script is exercised in CI.
